# Provident-Electron — Active Decisions Summary

Maintained by the document-archival loop (AGENTS.md item 6). ACTIVE =
governs the current shell; SUPERSEDED = replaced (provenance only).
Decisions are recorded as `DECIDED:` / `ACTIVE` / `SUPERSEDED` rows and kept
in sync with `docs/specs/mcp-endpoint.md`. Imported constraints from the
upstream project are marked **IMPORTED** (their canonical record is in
`../Preempt-Providence/docs/decisions.md` / `RENDER_PROCESS_NOTES.md` §10.10).

**Version annotations in this file:** version pins recorded inside rows below
(`provident-ssr@0.1.2`, `0.1.3`, `0.2.0`, `0.2.0-rc.2/rc.3/rc.4`, `^0.2.1`) are
**(historical provenance)** — they record what was true at the row's date and are
**annotated, not rewritten** (this is the repo's archival convention: a DONE/ACTIVE
row's historical pins are provenance). The **current** pin and its move are the
`ENGINE-PIN-0.5` row.

| **DECIDED: AUTONOMY AFTER SPEC APPROVAL — TWO RETURN POINTS ONLY (no per-gate permission asks)** | 2026-09-27 | **The architect's ruling: *"stop asking me for permission to advance to greens or post-greens docs review. After spec is approved, return to the user only on final unit close-out, or if a blocker requires a user decision."*** Once a unit's **spec gate is approved**, the orchestrator **proceeds without further permission** through the entire remaining chain — `red → green → gate 4 (adversarial + PBT audit) → gate 5 (blind greens) → gate 6 (live/UI battery) → gate 7 (proofreader) → gate 8 (documentation review) → gate 9 (trio) → gate 10 (DONE row + ledger move)` — **including every repair/remand cycle those gates force** (a red that exposes a contract defect, an adversarial finding, a doc drift, a register re-grain, a stale-cell sweep), committing at each gate boundary (`RCA-8(a)`). **The orchestrator returns to the user ONLY on (1) FINAL UNIT CLOSE-OUT** (DONE row written, trackers reconciled, trio green, ledger moved — reported outcome-first), **or (2) a BLOCKER REQUIRING A USER DECISION** (an architect/design question the contract cannot answer; a scope change; admission of a new unit; a budget/priority fork; an irreversible action needing consent; an unsatisfiable clause pair; a frozen-contract change; a fork-facing compatibility break) — and a blocker report must be concrete and minimal: what is blocked, the evidence, two or three options with a recommendation, and what each blocks. **Never ask for permission to advance a gate, fix a defect a gate found, re-grain a register, sweep stale cells, re-run the red/trio, or write the DONE row; never ask twice about a settled decision** (an architect ruling recorded here is applied without confirmation). **This supersedes the `PAUSE and get the user's go-ahead` steps at every gate AFTER the spec gate** — the **spec gate is the one approval the chain waits for**. Binding on the orchestrator and ported into `AGENTS.md` item `10a`. | the architect's ruling of 2026-09-27, ported to `AGENTS.md` item `10a`; enforced by the gate-commit discipline (`AGENTS.md` `RCA-8(a)`) |

an INVALID-drag `reset` whose own clamp answers a number reads `1` sink write of the **CLAMPED PRE-DRAG SIZE**; a `cancel`/`pointercancel` reads `0`; and a `reset` at an **UNUSABLE BOUNDS PAIR** reads `0` sink writes (`E3` refuses **before** its write site — `gutter.md` `§2.3` item 4 clause 3 / `§3.2 F-14`) **while the SESSION's own channel still fires ONCE with the value it was handed (`NaN` at that pair) — which is exactly why that channel must not be the sink**. **A harness that gives the sink to BOTH channels reads `2 vs 1`; | **DECIDED: SEAM-THROW-DISPOSITION-VALUE-READING-SEAMS-ABSORBED — a throwing VALUE-READING seam is ABSORBED by the module's own total gate; the three `void` PRESENTATION/SINK seams and `E3`'s own `isDragValid` PROPAGATE** | 2026-09-27 | **THE RULING, one paragraph dense: a throwing VALUE-READING seam — `pointerOf`, `sizeFromPointer`, `axisOf`, `boundsOf`, `startSizeOf`, `resizableOf` — is ABSORBED by the module's own total gate: the move turns INVALID ⇒ the `reset` arm (`stats().resets === 1`), NO preview write, NO sink write, and NO throw out of the turn; the record is discarded in the module's `finally`.** **THE THREE `void` PRESENTATION/SINK SEAMS (`applyPreview`, `applyCursor`, `commit`) and `E3`'s own `isDragValid` PROPAGATE — they are consumer code at the boundary, and a swallow would be the silent no-op the seam contract forbids.** **⟶ CORRECTED 2026-09-27, THE FOURTH REPAIR PASS — THE `commit` HALF OF THAT SENTENCE IS SUPERSEDED FOR THE SINK SEAM, AND THE MEASURED SITE IS THE LANDED `E3`: a throwing `commit` seam is ABSORBED BY `E3`'s OWN WRITE SITE (`src/shared/gutter.ts`'s `write()` is `try { … commit(gesture, narrowed) } catch { return }`), so the seam IS invoked once at the committing terminal, the throw never reaches the consumer boundary, `E3`'s `stats().sinkCalls` reads `1` while `written` reads `0`, the terminal turn does NOT throw, and THIS module's own invocation count of the seam stays `0`.** **GOVERNING SITES (three): `docs/specs/gutter.md` `§2.1` seam `6`, `§2.4` item 2's `commit` row, and `§3.2 F-11` (*"the write is SWALLOWED by the session's commit seam — ALREADY COUNTED, never retried"*); the superseded site is `docs/specs/gutter-ui.md` `§R.3`'s `commit` degradation cell (annotated in place, the as-filed text kept visible). THE OTHER TWO PRESENTATION SEAMS (`applyPreview`, `applyCursor`) AND `E3`'s `isDragValid` KEEP PROPAGATING, and the value-reading seams keep being ABSORBED — only the SINK seam's disposition is corrected.** **The rows that pin the corrected reading: `docs/specs/gutter-ui.md` `§3.1 M-20` class 4's `commit` arm and `§3.2 F-8`; the pinning row for the swallow itself is `gutter.md` `§3.2 F-11`.** **THE TURN EACH THROW SURFACES IN IS BOUND WITH THE RULING: the value-reading seams' throws are absorbed in the module's OBSERVED-MOVE turn; `applyCursor`'s surfaces at the HOVER turn; `applyPreview`'s at the MOVE turn; and `commit`'s at the TERMINAL turn — so A DRIVE WITH NO TERMINAL CANNOT OBSERVE A `commit` THROW.** **HOW IT LANDED (annotations only, nothing rewritten): `docs/specs/gutter-ui.md` `§2.4`'s seam table, `§R.3`'s degradation table, `§3.1 M-20` class 3, and `§3.2 F-2` — whose as-filed `PROPAGATES` reading is annotated SUPERSEDED, kept visible as the DRIFT SITE, and remanded to the absorbed reading.** **THE GOVERNING SITES (three against one): `§2.4`'s seam table's *throwing-seam* row, `§R.3`'s degradation table, and `M-20` class 3 rule ABSORBED; the single downstream `§3.2` cell ruled PROPAGATES — a drifted row, not an open design question.** **THE ROWS THAT PIN IT: `F-2`, `M-20` class 3, `M-3` and `F-8`.** | **the red-run repair pass (gate 3, second half), 2026-09-27 — its DEFECT 2 disposition, recorded in `docs/specs/gutter-ui.md`'s `⟶ RECORDED 2026-09-27 — THE RED-RUN REPAIR PASS` block (item 3) and at the four sites above; the governing text is `docs/specs/gutter.md` `§2.4` item 2 and `docs/specs/gutter-ui.md` `§3.3 I-7`** |
| **DECIDED: THE DIVERGENCE HARNESS IS A TESTING TOOL AND IS IN THE UPDATE SCOPE** | 2026-09-27 | **The architect's ruling: *"Divergence is fundamentally a testing tool, include it in the update scope."*** **`scripts/electron-divergence.mjs` (the `npm run divergence` leg, `R13`) is a TESTING TOOL, not production surface — so its fixtures are UPDATED as part of the unit whose change they must track, exactly as a test file is, and the `scripts/**` DENIED-set clause does not freeze it against a fixture update that a legitimate unit's own change forces.** **WHAT THIS UNBLOCKS, measured: the leg went RED (`exit 1`, `R13 RESULT: 9 checks, 5 failures`) when `U-GUTTER-UI` (`E10`) grew the demo envelope `12 → 18` nodes with its authored gutter card, because the script carried a HAND-COPIED 12-node envelope literal (its own comment: *"12 nodes: root + h1 + counter-card + …"*) — and a red divergence leg makes `npm run ui` exit `2` `PRECONDITION-FAILED` with NO MEASUREMENT TAKEN, i.e. it blocks EVERY `[U]`/real-DOM claim in the repo, not just this unit's.** **WHAT IS PINNED BY THE RULING:** (1) the fixture update is **BOUNDED to the fixture** — the leg's `N = 9` check set, its spawn discipline (`scripts/electron-spawn.mjs`'s `--no-sandbox` + `--disable-dev-shm-usage` + a FRESH scratch `--user-data-dir` per spawn), its exit-code contract (`{0,1,2,3}`) and its honest-limits prose are UNCHANGED; (2) the fixture must track the demo envelope as its ONE source of truth (`src/shared/demo-envelope.ts`) rather than restate it by hand — a hand-copied literal is the defect class this ruling exists to close; (3) the other `scripts/**` files stay DENIED (no leg driver, no helper, no new script, no `package.json` change); (4) `tests/**` and `src/**` are untouched by this ruling — it widens the SCOPE of a test-tool fixture, never the unit's code allow-list. **Consequence recorded: the same treatment applies to any future unit whose legitimate change alters the demo envelope: its pass updates the divergence fixture, re-runs the leg, and records the reading — the leg stays the strict precondition it is rather than becoming a fixture that no unit may keep in step.** | the architect's ruling of 2026-09-27 (this session), resolving the blocker reported at `f0b9ae2`/`79b3561` (`docs/next-steps.md`'s `⟶ E10 STATE AT f0b9ae2`, next action 1); the leg's contract is `docs/specs/ci-divergence-leg.md` and its live findings are L-1 of `docs/specs/gutter-ui-live-battery.md` |
| **DECIDED: A DECLARED REGISTER TERM IS A DRIVE COUNT (assertions and readings are printed BESIDE it, never counted in it)** | 2026-09-27 | **The architect's ruling: *"Re-grain."*** Every typed property register's **declared attempt term is a DRIVE count** — the number of genuinely distinct drives the row executes — and **assertions, observations and readings are reported BESIDE the declared term and are NEVER counted in it**. **Applied immediately to `U-GUTTER-UI` (`E10`):** `P-GU-SM-1` `22` → **`15`** (its 12 mid-drag items are ASSERTIONS over 5 drives) and `P-GU-TP-1` `18` → **`12`** (its `stats()`/`controller` reads are readings of the same state), so the declared total is **`134` = `15 + 15 + 15 + 45 + 20 + 12 + 12`** (subtotals `SM 45 · IM 65 · TP 24`), and **the three sites that carry the arithmetic move TOGETHER** (`§5.5.1`'s cells, `§5.5.3`, `§5.3` item 11's DONE-row requirement) — no site may print a total that is not the sum of its own printed terms. **WHY:** a term derived from assertions is the exact defect the `E3` PBT audit named (`IM-4`'s "`18` = three drives copied six times"), and because the harnesses count `attemptsRun` as **drives**, a non-drive term can never reconcile with the measured figure — which is how `E3` came to carry the residues `P-GT-IM-1` `18`/`20` and `P-GT-IM-4` `28`/`27`. **Consequences pinned:** (1) the caps stay satisfied and are compared against the DECLARED (drive) figures — `134 ≤ 400`, largest row `45 ≤ 100`; (2) **the same rule retro-closes `E3`'s parked residues** (`18`/`20` and `28`/`27`) when its checks resume — one convention, not two ad-hoc annotations; (3) a register cell that wishes to report assertions must print them as a SEPARATE figure (as the distinct-drive and assertion ledgers already do), never inside the term. | the architect's ruling of 2026-09-27 (recorded in `docs/pending.md` §I-terdecies); derived in `docs/specs/gutter-ui.md` `§5.5.1`/`§5.5.3`/`§5.3`; the class is documented at `docs/pending.md` §I-sexties (`E3`-CELL-2/3) and in `docs/decisions.md`'s `REGISTER-ATTEMPT-TOTALS-PRINT-THEIR-TERMS` |
| **DECIDED: GUTTER-CALLER-SEAMS-ARE-THE-FAMILY'S-DOWNSTREAM-CONTRACT (not demo tools)** | 2026-09-27 | **The architect's ruling: *"The caller seams will need to be used by the downstream projects, these are not just demo tools."*** The eleven caller seams of the panes/zones family's UI layer — `sizeFromPointer` · `axisOf` · `cursorOf` · `applyPreview` · `applyCursor` · `startSizeOf` · `boundsOf` · `resizableOf` · `commit` · `pointerOf` · `moveTypeOf` — are a **PUBLIC, EXPORTED, DOCUMENTED CONTRACT that downstream consumers (forks) IMPLEMENT** to drive the affordance with their own policy, **not demo-internal helpers**. **Consequences this pins:** (1) the seam **types are part of the affordance module's exported census** (so a fork can import them), and each seam's **signature, REQUIRED/OPTIONAL status, totality and DECLARED DEGRADATION rule is normative contract text** — a seam that is absent, non-callable or throwing must produce a declared safe default and a row that can FAIL, never a silent no-op; (2) **this repo's demo supplies exactly ONE implementation of them** — a CONSUMER/SAMPLE, whose home is a demo-side file and which must be **named as an implementation, never as the contract**; (3) **`docs/FORKER.md` carries the seam contract** for downstream, with the same care as the delegate surfaces the `SCH-*` dispositions established; (4) **answers gate-1 escalation `E-1` for `U-GUTTER-UI` (`E10`)** — the escalation's proposed "demo seam bundle as the contract" is **rejected**: the contract is the module's exported types plus the spec's seam table, and only the demo's **example implementation** needs an allowed home; (5) the unit's register/rows must pin the **per-seam degradation** behaviour so a fork's miswiring is observable rather than silently degraded. **⟶ CONSEQUENCE (3) — STATUS OF THE SECOND APPLICANT UNIT, ANNOTATED 2026-09-27 (THE GATE-8 PER-UNIT DOCUMENTATION REVIEW OF `U-RELOCATE`; this ROW IS UNMOVED — a consequence's discharge is not a ruling and this note moves no clause of it).** **CONSEQUENCE (3) BINDS `U-RELOCATE` (`E4`) TOO** — this row's own scope is *"the caller seams of the panes/zones family"*, and the fork-facing seam contract is what `docs/pending.md` `§L-4b` tracks for that unit. **`U-RELOCATE`'s SEVEN seams are NOT yet carried in `docs/FORKER.md`: the consequence REMAINS OWED, `docs/pending.md` `§L-4b` remains `OPEN`, and the gate-10 DONE row for `E4` must carry it as OWED, never as landed.** **THE SECOND SOURCE `docs/FORKER.md` CITED FOR THAT OBLIGATION WAS MIS-CITED AND IS CORRECTED AT ITS OWN SITE:** it read `docs/specs/relocate-greens.md`'s `§K` `K-9`, which does not resolve (that set's `§K` stops at `§K.7`); the `K-1`…`K-9` request rows live in **`docs/pending.md` `§K`**, whose `K-9` (`H-8`) asks for a glossary in the fork-facing doc. **`K-9` IS A REQUEST, NOT A RULING** (`§K`'s own header) — so the obligation this ACTIVE row's consequence (3) carries is independent of it, and the two share one remedy in `docs/FORKER.md`. **`E10`'s OWN ELEVEN SEAMS (`§R.3`) ARE CARRIED; `E4`'s SEVEN ARE NOT — that is the whole of the drift this note records.** | the architect's ruling of 2026-09-27 (recorded in `docs/pending.md` §I-undecies); derived into `docs/specs/gutter-ui.md` `§2.1`/`§2.4`/`§2.6`, `§5.1`, `§5.5.1`, `§5.3` and `docs/FORKER.md`; the escalation is filed at `docs/pending.md` §I-decies `E-1` |
| **DECIDED: UI-UNITS-MAY-TOUCH-THE-RENDERER-WIRING (a diff scope is a DERIVATION, never a template)** | 2026-09-27 | **The architect's ruling: *"Yes the UI change is allowed to touch the renderer — the UI needs to be rendered."*** A **UI/rendering unit's diff scope MAY include a BOUNDED renderer-wiring edit** — for `U-GUTTER-UI` (`E10`) exactly four wiring roles plus one graph-read helper (`startGutterAffordance(runtime)` in `src/renderer/renderer.ts`: construct the session and the `E3` controller, resolve the affordance/target elements from the producing graph, call the factory and attach, own the presentation writes; plus `Runtime.elementForNodeId(...)` in `src/renderer/runtime.ts`) — while **`src/main/**`, the preload/MCP surface, the Electron shell chrome, `package.json`/`scripts`/config, every sibling `src/shared/*` module and the FROZEN pairs (`gesture-session.ts`/its tests, `gutter.ts`/its tests) stay DENIED**. **The governing constraint is unchanged and is about AUTHORING, not about WHICH FILES may change:** all non-shell UI must be provident-rendered data (envelope nodes / handler bodies / hooks) driven through the producing graph, so **a renderer edit that hand-writes DOM instead of driving the graph is a finding**; the renderer edit is WIRING, never UI content. **WHY THIS ROW EXISTS:** `E10`'s first filing DENIED `src/renderer/**` by **inheritance** from the sibling `src/shared` mechanism units (for which the denial is correct, since a pure mechanism touching the renderer would be scope creep) — and that copy-forward deleted the only site at which the affordance could be instantiated, leaving the unit **observability-empty** (hover/press/drag/release/drop never executed in any running app, and its live `U`-rows unmeasurable). **THE RULE THIS PINS: each unit's DENIED set is DERIVED from that unit's own charter, never copied from a sibling; and gate 1 must answer, explicitly, *"does the allowed file set contain a path from the application's entry point to this code?"* for every unit whose charter claims observability.** | the architect's ruling of 2026-09-27 (recorded in `docs/pending.md` §I-nonies; | **DECIDED: GSESSION-CAPTURE-CAPABILITY-IS-SOURCE-SUPPLIED-AND-OPTIONAL** | 2026-09-27 | The node-local interaction session (`U-GSESSION`, wave E / `E6`) invokes the INJECTED SOURCE's own **optional** member **`capturePointer?(element: GestureElement): void`** as its ONE capture call: at most once per gesture, only inside `begin`, only AFTER the gesture is established (after the three tracking attaches), and only for a control that opted in with a truthy `capture`. **Its ABSENCE is the DECLARED DEGRADATION** — zero calls, no throw, no diagnostic, and the gesture still establishes and terminates normally; a THROW from it is swallowed by the same valid-state rule as the source's other callables. The module's bytes carry **no DOM-specific capture-method token** (`R-3`'s token ban still bites via `F-11`'s raw+assembled scans with a positive control), so the capability is the CALLER's, the invocation is the session's, and the count is exactly ONE on an opted-in establishment and ZERO otherwise. **Resolves the 2026-09-27 capture ruling and `ADV-GS-3`/`ADV-GS-4` (owed items `D-1`/`D-4`)**: the member is DECLARED on `§2.1`'s `EventSource`, NAMED in `§2.5` item 2, ROWED at `M-7b`/`M-8b`, pinned by `§5.2` leg 4 (the type-layer red was `TS2353`; it is exit `0` now) and its swallowed throw is rowed. **This is the row `ADV-GS-29`(iii) recorded as missing.** | `docs/specs/gsession.md` `§2.1` (`EventSource.capturePointer`), `§2.5` item 2, `§2.3` item 6(b); tests `M-7b`/`M-8b`/`ADV-GS-26`; gate-record owed items `D-1`/`D-4`; `docs/next-steps.md`'s `## DONE — U-GSESSION` clause (3) |
| **DECIDED: GSESSION-FAILED-ESTABLISHMENT-CONSUMES-NOTHING-AND-ROLLS-BACK** | 2026-09-27 | Three coupled rules for the session's establishment path, each a module clause with a regression row: **(1)** a START attach that FAILS writes **no ledger entry** (`ADV-GS-1`) — and the row that had asserted the violation as its expectation was repaired to the contract's retry form; **(2)** a TRACKING attach that fails is **CHECKED**, the successful attaches are **ROLLED BACK**, the refusal uses the EXISTING closed-domain code **`'not-installed'`** (no eighth member), nothing commits, and the element stays INSTALLED so a transient seam failure is retryable (`ADV-GS-2`); **(3)** the gesture counter and the handle `id` are incremented ONLY by a `begin` that RETURNS `{ok:true}` — the increment sits AFTER the tracking-attach check AND AFTER the `onStart` try/catch — so a refused `begin` and a `begin` whose `onStart` THROWS consume neither, and `stats().gestures` counts SUCCESSFUL `begin` calls only (`ADV-GS-15`/`ADV-GS-16`). | `docs/specs/gsession.md` `§2.1`'s `gestures`/`gestureId` cells, `§2.3` item 1(d), `§2.4` item 4, `M-2`; the module's dated `ADV-GS-1`/`-2`/`-15`/`-16` blocks; rows `F-8` and the two refusal regression rows; `docs/next-steps.md`'s `## DONE — U-GSESSION` clause (8) |
| **DECIDED: GSESSION-THE-DISPOSAL-GOVERNS-A-MID-CALL-BEGIN** | 2026-09-27 | A `begin` whose session is DISPOSED during the establishment call (from `capturePointer`, from `onStart`, or any consumer code running inside that order) must **NOT** report success: the disposal governs (`§2.3` item 7.3's permanently-inert rule), so the call **refuses `{ok:false, code:'disposed'}`** — the existing seven-member closed domain, no eighth member — the discarded record stays discarded, `gesture()` stays `null`, and the gesture counter, the handle id and the listener baseline are NOT consumed. The module's guard is `if (ended || slot !== record) return refuseBegin('disposed')`, at the END of the establishment order and ABOVE the increment; **its SECOND limb (`DIS-1`, `§3b.0`) covers the same-class shape where consumer code inside the order (e.g. `cancel(element)` from `onStart`) discards the attempt's own record with no disposal at all — the same `'disposed'` refusal, because the closed domain offers no more specific member**; a re-lex for the discard-only shape would need its own gate (`§4.4 S-8`/`S-9`). | `docs/specs/gsession.md` `§2.3` items 7.3/7.7 + the `§3b.0` `DIS-1` clause, `§2.2`/`§4.4 S-9`; the module's dated `ADV-GS-30` block; the `ADV-GS-30` rows (both limbs); `docs/next-steps.md`'s `## DONE — U-GSESSION` clauses (5)/(8) |

| **DECIDED: TRANSPORT — MCP server supports BOTH stdio and Streamable HTTP, selected at launch** | 2026-08-21 | `--mcp-transport=stdio\|http` (default `http`) / `PROVIDENT_MCP_TRANSPORT`. `http` = Streamable HTTP on `127.0.0.1:<port>/mcp` (default 3787, `--mcp-port` / `PROVIDENT_MCP_PORT`). The HTTP transport needs `globalThis.crypto` (Node ≥19 / Electron ≥20 embedded Node); the plain-Node dev shell (v18) lacks it — the HTTP path is verified inside Electron, stdio is the fallback for plain-Node hosts. | user choice (2026-08-21) "Both (configurable)"; observed: `crypto is not defined` on Node 18 (REQ-GAP class, environment finding) |
| **DECIDED: GRAPH-LOCATION — the provident-ssr producing process + DomAdapter live in the RENDERER; the MCP server lives in the MAIN process; IPC bridges them** | 2026-08-21 | The renderer owns translate → register → compile → recordResolved → DomAdapter render (+ SSR adapter mirror). Rationale: `DomAdapter` requires a DOM (REQ-GAP-6) and the framework's interaction surface is browser-side; the main process is not a DOM. | user choice (2026-08-21) "Renderer DOM + IPC bridge"; adapters.ts `DomAdapter` constructor |
| **DECIDED: TARGETING — the MCP dispatch tool accepts BOTH vocabularies: authored `css.id` (ergonomic) and `nodeId`/`wire` (authoritative)** | 2026-08-21 | `provident.dispatch` target accepts `{kind:'cssId', cssId}` / `{kind:'nodeId', nodeId}` / `{kind:'wire', wire}` / a bare string (resolved css.id → nodeId). Host-side resolution only — the engine's runtime lookup stays nodeId/wire-scoped (REQ-GAP-2). `list_targets` exposes both per node so an agent can pick. | user choice (2026-08-21) "Both css.id + nodeId"; upstream ssr-synthetic-event.md §2.2 |

No `format: 'legacy'` in the demo. REQ-GAP-1 (the legacy-stub assumption) is a catalogue row, not a demo fix. | upstream feature-showcase.js bodies; REQ-GAP-1 |
| **DECIDED: MAIN-IS-CJS — the Electron main process bundles as CommonJS (`main.cjs`)** | 2026-08-21 | Electron's ESM main cannot `require()` CJS node builtins ("Dynamic require of child_process is not supported"); the MCP SDK transitively requires node builtins. | **DECIDED: CSP-UNSAFE-EVAL — the renderer's CSP allows `'unsafe-eval'` for provident-ssr's function-SOURCE handler bodies** | 2026-08-21 | The renderer's CSP is `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-eval'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`. provident-ssr instantiates function-STRING handler bodies via `new Function` at translate (translate.md §2); a strict `script-src 'self'` (no unsafe-eval) SILENTLY skips every string-body handler (REQ-GAP-7). The carve-out is required for data-only envelopes. Security note: function-source handlers are trusted app data, not untrusted input. | observed: real renderer handlers skipped under strict CSP (2026-08-21); REQ-GAP-7 |
| **DECIDED: MCP-STATELESS-HTTP — the Streamable HTTP transport is STATELESS: a fresh McpServer + transport PER POST request; GET/DELETE → 405** | 2026-08-21 | The MCP SDK's StreamableHTTP transport in stateless mode (`sessionIdGenerator: undefined`) REQUIRES a fresh server + transport per request — reusing either across requests throws ("cannot be reused across requests", message ID collisions) and leaves requests undelivered (the response event never fires). The canonical SDK stateless pattern (simpleStatelessStreamableHttp.js) is: build a fresh McpServer per POST, `connect` a fresh transport, `handleRequest`, close on `res.close`; GET/DELETE → 405. Verified: the SDK client drives all four tools over both stdio and HTTP (tests/mcp-stdio-e2e.test.mjs). | observed: HTTP initialize returned SSE headers but never the response event (2026-08-21); SDK webStandardStreamableHttp.js "Stateless transport cannot be reused" + simpleStatelessStreamableHttp.js example |

Electron 33 (embedded Node 20.18, `@electron/get@2`) installs and runs on Node 18 and fully supports ESM renderer + CJS main. | observed install failure on Node 18 (2026-08-21) |

| **DECIDED: UI-RENDERED-WITH-PROVIDENT — every non-shell UI element is rendered with the provident framework, never hand-written HTML/DOM** | 2026-08-25 | Project-wide constraint (AGENTS.md): all UI elements that are not directly part of the Electron shell (e.g. pre-existing menu/settings dropdowns, panels, dialogs, controls) MUST be rendered with the provident framework — authored as provident-ssr data (envelope nodes / handler bodies / hooks / component bindings) and driven through the producing graph, NOT as hand-written HTML/DOM in the renderer. Rationale: the shell's purpose is full synthetic-event access + rendered-HTML visibility for MCP endpoints — a UI element rendered outside the provident graph is invisible to `provident.dispatch`/`get_rendered_html`/`get_markdown` and defeats the agentic/debugging surface. | **DECIDED: INTERACTION-NODE-LOCAL — all shell interaction mechanisms work through LOCAL handlers on the element that receives the interaction; document-delegated pointer tracking is REJECTED (architect amendment A-d3)** | 2026-09-27 | **Architect ruling A-d3 (2026-09-27), verbatim in substance:** "setPointerdown/other pointer tracking behavior is the incorrect implementation for the pane/gutter control user flows. Where possible, all interaction mechanisms should work through local handlers." **The ruled shape:** a host module with an **injected event source** that attaches each gesture-start listener to **its own control element**, opens any gesture-scoped tracking listeners **ONLY between start and end**, removes them at end/cancel/dispose, commits **exactly once per gesture**, and never references a global element lookup, `document`, `window`, `matchMedia`, `activeElement` or `getComputedStyle`. **FORBIDDEN:** `document.addEventListener('pointerdown', …)` installed at setup plus per-event `closest(selectors)` resolution; a document/window listener that outlives the gesture; per-event/per-frame writes; a mechanism that re-queries the DOM for its own nodes (`querySelectorAll`/`closest`/`getElementById`). **Narrow capture reading (binding):** forbidden is **capture installed from the gesture-start handler, unconditionally, before the interaction is established** (the F-1 retargeting class); capture **after** establishment, on the gesture's own element, **opted in per control** (`capture?: boolean`, default false) is **permitted**. An outright capture ban is rejected because it would make the fork's own `lostpointercapture` criterion **inert** and would lose the pointer when the cursor leaves the window — a shipped correctness regression no node-layer row can catch; the reading is adjudicated from a two-clause sentence and is **open architect decision `Q2`**. **Why this is a correctness rule, not a preference:** a document-level listener is outside the graph, so a human interaction it mediates is **not reachable by `provident.dispatch`**, and for a flow that writes layout state it produces a mutation invisible to `get_rendered_html`/`list_targets` and untriggered by `MUTATING_METHODS` (`src/renderer/renderer.ts:12`) — an MCP/UI equivalence **break**. **What local handlers purchase is origin REACHABILITY — NOT magnitude-equivalence:** the MCP dispatch surface carries an event **name with no pointer coordinates** (`src/renderer/runtime.ts:127-139`, `:1037-1051`), so a session reached through MCP can start/abort deterministically but **cannot commit a magnitude**; **no later pass may claim agent-drivable drags or that a session-driven layout change is magnitude-equivalent to a `state-slice` op** (see the gate record's equivalence table for the four reasons). `SCH-2` is admitted on this shape as `U-GSESSION`; `SCH-6`/`SCH-7` are declined because their surviving lifecycle properties are this session's. | architect amendment A-d3 (2026-09-27); `src/renderer/runtime.ts:127-139`,`:1037-1051`; `src/renderer/renderer.ts:12`; `docs/specs/provident-electron-shell-chrome-handoff-review.md` (`S-d9`, `H-r9`) |

| **DECIDED: U-RELOCATE-THRESHOLD-IS-THE-ZONE-PROXIMITY-DISTANCE — the `threshold` is the DISTANCE FROM A CANDIDATE ZONE at which the relocate affordance engages: the zone EXPANDS to visibility (if empty/minimized) and a GHOST shows where the pane will drop; a release OUTSIDE that proximity is the invalid placement ⇒ the reset behavior** | 2026-09-27 | **THE ARCHITECT'S RULING, verbatim: *"After the pane drag gesture is started and the pane is moved close to somewhere that can contain it, it should A. expand the zone to visibility (if it is empty/minimized) and B. place a ghost showing where the pane will drop into if released. Dropping the pane outside of a position where this happens should cause the invalid placement --> reset behavior. The threshold defines the distance from a zone where this behavior happens."*** **WHAT IT SETTLES (the `E4` gate-1 `FLAWED` verdict's three must-decide items — the `threshold` referent, the terminal semantics of *"gesture end"*, and the reveal's write site — are now answerable from this ruling plus the landed clauses):** **(1) THE REFERENT — a proximity RADIUS around a candidate zone, not a magnitude of the pane, not a time, not a count. The mechanism engages within `threshold` of a zone that can contain the pane and does nothing outside it.** **(2) THE BEHAVIOURS SPLIT INTO THREE DISTINCT CHANNELS, and they are NOT the same write:** **(A) the zone's EXPANSION to visibility** — a *state* change, the consumer's own layout/reveal state (the `revealed` predicate `U-CENSUS` already reads; `docs/specs/census.md` `§8`), which is what `onReveal` writes; **(B) the GHOST** — a *transient presentation* write that follows the pointer and shows the prospective drop position, i.e. a PREVIEW channel (per observed move, at most once per move), NEVER the reveal; **(C) THE RESET ARM** — a release outside the proximity is the *invalid placement*: the gesture resets to the pre-drag state exactly once and the visible state reverts (the session's `reset` terminal, one commit of the **supplied** value), which is the same invalid-arm shape `U-GUTTER-UI` (`E10`) landed for the gutter (`M-13`, `F-10`: the reset taken while the gesture is still active, the pre-drag reading committed once, the later `pointerup` committing nothing).** **(3) THEREFORE THE *"reveal written EXACTLY ONCE PER GESTURE AT GESTURE END"* STRENGTHENING APPLIES TO (A) — THE ZONE'S REVEALED STATE — AND NOT TO (B), THE GHOST**, which is a per-move preview by construction. A contract that counts the ghost as a reveal would make the ledger's headline row unsatisfiable; this ruling is the sentence that prevents that, and the spec must state it in exactly these terms.** **(4) THE ARITHMETIC'S HOME, DERIVED FROM THE LANDED LAYERS (the one thing the ruling does NOT decide, and the spec must):** the distance *"from a zone"* needs the pointer position and the zone's geometry, and **this repo's node layer reads no coordinate and no geometry** (`gutter.md` `§2.2` P-1/P-8, `I-11`; `S-d11`'s binding clause: *the geometry this family produces is UNPROVABLE in this repo today — say so wherever geometry criteria are described*; the session *“reads NO field of any event”*). **So the comparison must be CONSUMER-SUPPLIED in one of exactly two admissible shapes, and the spec must pin ONE:** **(i) the module receives a caller-supplied scalar `distance` (or the resolved candidate) from its injected seam and compares it against the caller-supplied `threshold` — a plain number comparison, no coordinate read, no geometry, no unit string;** or **(ii) the caller supplies the predicate itself (`crosses(state) → unknown`, the shape the family already uses for `isResizable`/`revealed`) and the module holds `threshold` only as an OPAQUE carried value it never evaluates.** **A mechanism-side distance computation (reading a pointer position, a `getBoundingClientRect`, a zone box, a CSS unit) is FORBIDDEN by the landed prohibitions and by `S-11`'s STOP — that is the fork's own business in its own namespace, exactly as the fork's drop criterion (`lostpointercapture`/drop) was ruled to be.** **(5) THE VERIFICATION LAYER, stated so no green is over-read:** (A) and (B) are **RENDERED** facts (a zone visibly expanded, a ghost visibly placed) and are therefore **[U]/geometry territory — unprovable in this repo today**; the node layer can prove the **decision table and the call cadence** (which candidate was resolved for a given caller-supplied reading, the reveal firing once at the terminal, the ghost's per-move cadence, the reset arm's single write, and the absence of retention across the boundary) and nothing about how anything looks. **E4's spec must therefore carry the three-part refusal clause the siblings carry (the refusal · the structural reason · *“may not be moved to the `ui` leg silently”*), and must NOT claim a `[D]` row it does not read.** | the architect's ruling of 2026-09-27 (this session), answering the `E4` (`U-RELOCATE`) gate-1 reviewers' three must-decide items; it lands the `threshold` question the gate-1 `critique` verdict flagged as requiring an architect (the `FLAWED` verdict's item 2) and completes the `VALID-WITH-CONDITIONS` `C-3`/`C-5` conditions (`docs/specs/provident-electron-shell-chrome-handoff-review.md` `§1.4`, `docs/specs/gsession.md` `§2.6` item 7 + `§2.3` item 4, `docs/specs/gutter.md` `§2.2` P-1/P-5/P-8 + `§4.4` S-11, `docs/specs/gutter-ui.md` `§R` `R6`/`§3.2` `F-10`, `docs/specs/census.md` `§8` + `§0A` note 6) |
| **DECIDED: U-RELOCATE-REVEALED-ZONE-HIDES-AGAIN — a revealed zone HIDES AGAIN when the pane would not place into it, on EITHER trigger: it was pulled back out of the `threshold` proximity, OR it has shifted to place into a DIFFERENT zone; the mid-drag hide therefore rides the PER-MOVE channel, and the durable reveal write stays ONCE per gesture at a committing terminal** | 2026-09-27 | **THE ARCHITECT'S RULING, verbatim (2026-09-27, answering the `E4` gate-1 blocker): *"The revealed zone hides again if the pane would not place into its zone. This can be because it was pulled back out of the threshold proximity, or because it has shifted to place into a different zone"*.** **WHAT IT SETTLES (three clauses):** **(1) THE ZONE'S DISPLAYED-NESS DURING A DRAG IS NOT MONOTONIC** — it turns **ON** within proximity and **OFF AGAIN while the gesture is still running**, on either trigger. **So the mid-drag hide CANNOT be the once-per-gesture `onReveal` write: it rides the SAME PER-MOVE TRANSIENT CHANNEL AS THE GHOST** (`docs/specs/relocate-review.md` §4.2 channel **(B)** — the `E10` preview-vs-commit split), **while channel (A) `onReveal` remains the once-per-gesture-at-gesture-end DURABLE write.** **(2) THE RETARGET RULE** — on an observed move that changes the target zone, the **previously displayed zone hides and the new one shows**, both inside the **same observed-move turn**; hide-plus-show is ONE transition, not two gestures. **(3) THE INVALID ARM READS ZERO REVEAL WRITES** — on a release outside every zone, the zone has **already** hidden through (B) as the pane left proximity, so `onReveal` fires **only at `'end'`** (reading (α) of the gate-1 blocker, `docs/specs/relocate-review.md` §6.1), the reset terminal commits only the caller-supplied pre-drag value, and the visible revert is the preview channel's. **DERIVED, AND FLAGGED AS DERIVED — CONFIRMATION OWED AT THE `E4` SPEC GATE:** **⟶ CONFIRMED AT THE SPEC GATE (2026-09-27) — THE AS-WRITTEN *"CONFIRMATION OWED"* IS KEPT VISIBLE AND IS READ AS AS-FILED (THE GATE-7 PROOFREADER PASS — finding `PF-13`, MED; annotate-never-rewrite, so no clause of the ruling above is rewritten).** **THE ARCHITECT'S GATE-2 FILING DECISION WAS *APPROVED AS FILED*, WHICH IS THIS ROW'S CONFIRMATION: the DERIVED mapping is ADOPTED for `U-RELOCATE`.** **WHERE IT IS PINNED: the sites this row itself names — `docs/specs/relocate.md` `§0A` note 6 · `§2.3` item 5 · `§5.5.1 P-RL-SM-1`/`P-RL-SM-6` · `§7a.1` item 3 — plus `§2.3` item 4's channel table and `§3.1 M-11`..`M-13`.** **ITS LABEL IS UNCHANGED: STILL LABELLED **DERIVED** AT EVERY SITE IT APPEARS, with its ALTERNATIVE (the hide as itself an `onReveal` write, which would make the *"exactly once per gesture at gesture end"* strengthening FALSE AS WRITTEN) STILL RECORDED AS **ARCHITECT-REVERSIBLE** — a later pass that takes the alternative opens its own gate; it is a contract change, not a re-reading.** **NO STATUS MOVES IN THIS LEDGER'S OTHER ROWS, and NOTHING IN THE THREE CLAUSES ABOVE IS WEAKENED OR RE-OPENED.** the mapping *(1)+(2) ride (B)* and *(3) leaves (A) at zero* is a **DERIVATION** from the ruling above + `U-RELOCATE-THRESHOLD-IS-THE-ZONE-PROXIMITY-DISTANCE` + the ledger row's *"reveal written EXACTLY ONCE per gesture at gesture end"* strengthening. **THE ALTERNATIVE — that the hide is ITSELF an `onReveal` write, which would make that strengthening FALSE AS WRITTEN and require amending it — is RECORDED AND ARCHITECT-REVERSIBLE.** **Consequence for the unit:** the reveal row's declared terminal domain is `{'end'}`, the preview channel's rows must count its **show/hide transitions** (a retarget drive included), and the invalid arm carries **ZERO** reveal writes as a falsifiable cell. | **the architect's ruling of 2026-09-27 (verbatim above), answering the `E4` gate-1 blocker reported in `docs/specs/relocate-review.md` §6 and tracked at `docs/pending.md` §L-1 — i.e. the return point `AGENTS.md` item 10a(2)**; it **EXTENDS** the row above (`U-RELOCATE-THRESHOLD-IS-THE-ZONE-PROXIMITY-DISTANCE`) and `docs/pending.md`'s `SCH-7` row's three recorded consequences; **the ledger strengthening it must not contradict is `docs/next-steps.md`'s `## OPEN` row `E4`** (*"reveal written EXACTLY ONCE per gesture at gesture end — a deliberate strengthening; the fork's per-crossing implementation fails this row"*) |
**⟶ DISCHARGED / LANDED — STATUS ANNOTATION ON THE TWO `U-RELOCATE-*` ROWS ABOVE (2026-09-27, the supervisor's `U-RELOCATE` (`E4`) GATE-10 pass; this paragraph sits directly under the two rows so their own bytes stand unchanged, and per this file's convention a row's discharge is annotated BESIDE it, never substituted for it).** **BOTH ROWS STAY `ACTIVE`: they are the family's behaviour, not a unit's progress, and the unit's close-out does not retire them.** **WHAT "DISCHARGED" MEANS HERE, ROW BY ROW.** **`U-RELOCATE-THRESHOLD-IS-THE-ZONE-PROXIMITY-DISTANCE` — `DISCHARGED-AND-LANDED`:** its it lands as a **separate unit** right after (a pinned, passing leg must be revertible without touching the new one). **OWNERSHIP:** leg code = a new shared Electron-spawn helper (extracted from the twice-duplicated spawn) + `scripts/electron-ui.mjs`; | **DECIDED: PBT-REGISTER-REQUIRED-FOR-CODE-UNITS — gate 11's zero-row exemption is RESTRICTED to genuinely invariant-free / doc-only / config-only / non-JS units; every CODE-BEARING unit carries a typed `§5.x` register executed deterministically with NO new dependency (architect ruling 2026-09-27, taken at the `U-LISTHOST` spec gate)** | 2026-09-27 | **The ruling (architect, 2026-09-27, at the `U-LISTHOST` spec gate — the option "enforce gate 11"; verbatim in substance: the zero-row exemption covers only invariant-free / doc-only / config-only / non-JS units, so the wave-D specs' recorded exemption is remanded for re-derivation).** **What a CODE-BEARING unit must now carry:** a typed **Property register of ≤8 rows** (`P-IM` invariant / `P-SM` state-machine / `P-TP` totality — **never** `F-` rows, **never** a `§6`/`FS-n` citation as a register row), authored by `role_spec_writer`; a **property layer the TestWriter executes** (deterministic: exhaustive/finite enumeration or a **pinned-seed** generator; caps **≤100 attempts/row · ≤400 total · stop-after-5**; each row reports its **strategy id + held/broken**); and a **read-only PBT audit** by the adversarial pass. **A DONE row lacking register / property layer / audit is a review finding.** The exemption survives **only** as an explicit recorded justification, never a silent default. **Why it was enforced at THIS unit:** all four wave-D specs carried the identical exemption while being code-bearing; `docs/specs/listhost.md` `§5.5` itself admitted **two genuine quantifications** (*for every permutation … identity preserved*; *for every input shape … no throw*) and conceded in writing that its fixed tables do not prove them; and the exemption's **decisive clause is refuted by in-repo precedent** — `docs/specs/engine-pin.md` `§5.5` executed **7 of 8** register rows with **plain deterministic vitest tables and no new devDependency** (repeated-call idempotence over a fixed key set; enumerated boolean members), so a register is executable here **without `fast-check` or any property runner**. **WHAT LANDED UNDER THE RULING:** `docs/specs/listhost.md` `§5.5.1` = **7 rows** — 4 `P-IM` (`P-LH-IM-1`..`4`), 2 `P-SM` (`P-LH-SM-1`/`-2`), 1 `P-TP` (`P-LH-TP-1`) — with strategy ids `S-LH-PERM-1` / `S-LH-IDENT-1` / `S-LH-REPEAT-1` / `S-LH-FOREIGN-1` / `S-LH-MIXED-1` / `S-LH-SEQ-1` / `S-LH-SEED-1`, exhaustive `S₃`+`S₄` hand-authored permutation tables, fixed-shape/sequence tables, and a **pinned-seed `20260927`** hand-rolled 32-bit LCG over a 22-shape pool for totality — **157 attempts**, no `fast-check`, no new dependency, no `F-` register row; the old exemption is kept byte-identical as `§5.5.0` under dated SUPERSEDED banners. **OWED UNDER THE RULING (owners named, non-blocking for `U-LISTHOST`):** the same §5.5 re-derivation for `docs/specs/slothost.md` and `docs/specs/projection.md` **before their own red sets** (owner: those units' spec passes — neither red set may be authored under the exemption); the already-closed units' specs (`mount-invariant-guard.md`, `engine-drift.md`, `ci-ui-leg.md`, `ci-divergence-leg.md`) are **recorded, not re-opened here**. **NOT CLAIMED BY THIS PASS:** the register rows are the execution **design** — nothing was run; the TestWriter's red run is the first execution, and a row that is `YES` in the register but broken in the red is a **spec finding**, not a pass. **Also recorded:** `AGENTS.md` in this repo carries **no gate-11 text** (grep: no `PBT`/`gate 11` match), so this ruling is the standard's home until a process-doc pass lands it there (OWED, owner: the next proofreader/documentation pass). | architect ruling 2026-09-27 (the `U-LISTHOST` spec gate, "enforce gate 11"); `docs/specs/engine-pin.md` §5.5 (the deterministic-execution precedent); `docs/specs/listhost.md` §5.5.0 (superseded exemption) + §5.5.1 (the landed register); the gate-11 rule text |

| **DECIDED: PROJECTION-TOTALITY-AND-VALUES-NON-RECORD — the projection's two totality boundaries and the callable-`values` ruling** | 2026-09-27 | **What it pins, in three parts.** **(1) THE `values` PRESENCE-TEST THROW IS `'accessor-threw'`, NEVER A THROW AND NEVER `missing-value`:** when the module cannot learn whether an entry's key is an own property of `values` because the QUESTION threw (a revoked `Proxy`; a `getOwnPropertyDescriptor`-throwing `Proxy`), the entry's skip carries the entry's own emitted `name` and the reason `'accessor-threw'` — the module asked a **caller-data** object a question about an own property and the object answered with a throw (the same caller-data layer the eighth reason names), and `missing-value` is reserved for an input that contributes no own keys at all. **(2) THE APPLIER'S TWO FIELD READS DEGRADE INDEPENDENTLY:** `applyProjection` reads `projection['applied']` and `projection['skipped']` **each within its own totality boundary**; an unreadable `applied` plans NO writes, an unreadable `skipped` carries NO entries, and each unreadable field degrades to ABSENT **on its own half only** — so an unreadable `applied` beside a **readable non-empty** `skipped` yields `{applied:{}, skipped:<carried entries verbatim>, ok:false}` with zero sink calls, while the `{applied:{},skipped:[],ok:true}` form is the empty-`skipped` case and the revoked-`Proxy` case (both fields at once). **(3) A CALLABLE `values` IS A NON-RECORD** (`FUNCTION-VALUES-1`): a function contributes NO own keys — its incidental own `length`/`name`/`prototype` are **never** projected as caller data — so every otherwise-well-formed entry is skipped `missing-value`; **the ruling applies to the `values` position ONLY**, while the sink, the `applied` field and the projection argument stay deliberately duck-typed. **A spec set the module cannot ENUMERATE (a revoked `Proxy` `specOf`) contributes NO entries** (`{applied:{},skipped:[]}`, nothing decided), and a `specOf` whose enumeration succeeds but whose **entry read** throws stays `malformed-spec` with `name: ''`. | **Source:** the `U-PROJ` adversarial/PBT pass (gate 4, findings `ADV-PJ-1`/`-2`/`-3`/`-15`) + the architect's rulings in `docs/specs/projection.md` `§2.4` item 3's clause (v) and `§3.2`'s `SKIP-THREW-1`, the gate-4 red→green cycle (`f743e40` → `e16ee8e`), and the doc-review + blind-re-verification reconciliation. **Tests that pin it:** `tests/layout-projection.test.ts`'s `ADV-PJ-1` row (the exact ordered `skipped` for the spec's two-key revoked-Proxy case), the `ADV-PJ-2` row (shapes (i)/(ii)/(iii) incl. the discriminating readable-`applied` + throwing-`skipped` drive) and its `(i) SHARPENED` companion (the readable non-empty `skipped` beside an unreadable `applied`, asserting `ok === false`), the `FUNCTION-VALUES-1` row (function `values` ⇒ `missing-value` for keys `'length'`/`'name'`/`'prototype'`, plus the three clause-(iii)(d) position controls), the clause (v)(D)/(v)(E) rows (hostile `specOf`; `values` never enumerated), and the `SKIP-THREW-1` row. **Supersedes nothing:** it completes `§2.4` item 3's non-record clause (i) and `§`2.3 item 4's malformed-projection asymmetry rather than replacing them. || **DECIDED: SPEC-CURRENT-STATE-BLOCK-REQUIRED — every spec carries ONE `CURRENT STATE` block that states only what is true of the build now; history lives in `archive/`.** | 2026-09-27 | **What it pins.** (1) **Every `docs/specs/*.md` unit spec opens with ONE `## CURRENT STATE` block** — placed BEFORE `§0` so nothing follows the `§3b` file-end note — stating, in the file’s own words: the **unit status** (its ledger row and its open/`DONE` state, citing the DONE record by SECTION), the **surface** (module, exact export census, imports, whether any `src/**` file imports it, whether it is in any bundle), the **register** (row count, the total WITH ITS TERMS, seed, held/broken, honest bounded-coverage notes), the **legs** (each with its layer label and the explicit sentence that a node green is envelope/pure-layer evidence only), the **gate records** (adversarial / blind / doc-review, one line each, citing artifacts), the **open residues** with owners, and **one pointer to the archived history**. (2) **A claim that is not true of the build now does not belong in the live file** — not as dead text, not as a dated banner, not as ‘kept visible for provenance’. It belongs in `archive/<topic>/<date>-<name>.md` with a **short pointer** at the site it left (`≤ 2 lines`: what changed, when, why, the archive path, the operative clause). (3) **What this retires:** retaining superseded sentences beside their corrections so a fresh reader must reconstruct current truth from annotation archaeology, and stacking a new dated status note on every previous one. **Measured at the ruling:** `projection.md` carried `63` superseded markers, `zones.md` `18`, `census.md` `16`; after the archival pass the three live specs carry **ZERO** provenance-shaped long bodies and **`1,561` lines of verbatim history** sit in `archive/specs/`. (4) **The mechanical rule that makes enforcement safe: ARCHIVE FIRST, VERIFY, THEN COMPACT.** A pass replacing superseded text with a pointer must (a) write the text verbatim into the archive, (b) **prove the archive contains it**, and only then (c) replace the live line. **Compacting before archiving is a data-loss defect — this session produced one (five unarchived bodies deleted by a mechanical pass, restored from version control per `RCA-8(d)`); the ordering above is its fix and every compaction in the archival pass used it.** | **Source:** the architect’s rulings of 2026-09-27 on the archival loop’s role in truth maintenance and on the property register’s purpose; the annotation-density measurement above; the archival pass (`archive/specs/2026-09-27-{projection,zones,census}-history.md`). **Artifacts that pin it:** the `CURRENT STATE` blocks in `docs/specs/{projection,zones,census}.md` (three worked examples), the per-spec history files, and `docs/specs/projection-greens.md`’s dated line-anchor correction (the loop’s step 3 — repoint every citation — discharged, with the policy **cite sections and row ids, never line numbers**). |
| **DECIDED: REGISTER-ENTRY-COUNT-IS-NOT-CAPPED-AND-ARCHIVE-IS-THE-TRUTH-MECHANISM — (1) a property register enumerates EVERY discernible testable property; the per-section cap is a BREAKDOWN SIGNAL, not a ceiling. (2) superseded contract text MOVES TO `archive/`; the live spec holds only current truth.** | 2026-09-27 | **PART 1 — THE REGISTER HAS NO ROW CAP (architect's ruling).** The `§5.5.1` register exists to **identify the testable elements of the designed process and ensure they can be tested thoroughly**. **The row count is therefore an OUTCOME, not a budget:** if a section has **X** discernible always-true / always-false properties worth testing, it has **X rows**. **The `≤ 8` threshold is a GUIDELINE about WHEN A SECTION SHOULD BE FLAGGED FOR A COMPONENT-BREAKDOWN REVIEW — never a reason to drop a property, merge two distinct properties into one row, or leave a property unenumerated.** A spec-writer pass that finds more than the threshold must **enumerate them all** and **record the breakdown recommendation** (the signal), naming the separable component. **What this RETIRES:** any reading of the earlier register guidance as a ceiling — specifically the practice of landing on exactly 8 rows and recording 8-rows-as-compliance (four sibling units did this; in each case the count was the cap talking, not the property set). **What it does NOT change:** the caps that are still real and still checked — **100 attempts per row MAX · 400 attempts total MAX · stop-after-5-consecutive-failures**, the pinned seed, the deterministic hand-rolled draw, the no-new-dependency rule, and **a total printed with its own per-row terms**. **A section whose property set genuinely demands a breakdown is a FINDING for the next spec pass, not a licence to trim.** **PART 2 — THE ARCHIVE IS THE TRUTH MECHANISM (architect's ruling on the never-rewrite convention).** `archive/README.md` defines the loop: the git-visible `docs/` tree is for **ACTIVE development work + the CURRENT contract/spec state**, and **step 2 moves obsolete material into `archive/<topic>/<date>-<name>.md`** (never deleted — archived), **step 3 repoints every citation.** **`ANNOTATE-NEVER-REWRITE` is a rule about NOT SILENTLY DESTROYING PROVENANCE; it is NOT a licence to retain superseded claims inside the live contract.** The two were conflated and the result is measurable: **`docs/specs/projection.md` carried 63 superseded markers, `docs/specs/zones.md` 18, `docs/specs/census.md` 16** — live specs holding their own contradictory history, so a fresh reader must reconstruct current truth from annotation archaeology and the documentation review must decide which of two sentences in one cell is operative. **THE CORRECTED SHAPE, binding on every spec and tracker pass from now on: (a) the live file states only what is TRUE OF THE BUILD NOW; (b) superseded text is COMPACTED OUT to `archive/specs/<date>-<name>-history.md` (the archive holds the originals verbatim, in order, with their dates); (c) the live file keeps a SHORT provenance pointer at each compacted site (what changed, when, why, and the archive path) — not the dead text; (d) section numbers and row ids are PRESERVED (citation stability), so compaction removes words, never numbers or ids; (e) every citation to a moved passage is repointed.** | **Source:** the architect's rulings of 2026-09-27 on the property register's purpose and on the archival loop's role in truth maintenance; the sibling registers that landed on the threshold (`listhost` 7, `slothost` 6, `projection` 8, `zones` 8, `census` 8) and the compliance notes they carried; the annotation-density measurement above. **Artifacts that pin it:** every `§5.5.1` register's per-row execution record (the counts remain the evidence), and the per-spec history files under `archive/specs/` created by the compaction passes. |
| **DECIDED: REGISTER-ATTEMPT-TOTALS-PRINT-THEIR-TERMS — a property register's attempt total is stated WITH its per-row terms, and a total that is not the sum of its own terms is a finding** | 2026-09-27 | **What it pins.** A `§5.x`/`§5.5.1` typed register's attempt count is a **claim**, so it is stated as **`231` = `23+60+22+36+52+16+12+10`** — the total **and** the terms — and **any DONE row, spec cell or tracker cell that quotes a total without its terms, or whose terms do not sum to the total, is a review finding** (the register's executed counts are the only executed-layer evidence the ledger carries, so an uncheckable total is an evidence gap, not a typo). **Two named sub-rules, both learned the hard way:** **(1) a mis-sum is corrected by annotating, never by silently rewriting** — the as-filed form stays visible with the corrected one beside it (`U-PROJ`'s as-filed `239` vs the counted `231`; `U-LISTHOST`'s as-filed `157` vs the counted `168`); **(2) the DECLARED total and the DISTINCT-DRIVE count are different numbers and both must be reported** — `U-PROJ`'s `P-PJ-IM-6` declares `16` attempts of which one term is four assertions over ONE execution, so its honest distinct-drive count is `≈ 13`; the declared `231` is what is quoted **against the `≤100`/row and `≤400` total caps**, and the distinct count is reported as the honesty note (`§5.3` item 11). | **Source:** the `U-PROJ` `§5.5.1` re-derivation + red-run reconciliation (the mis-sum was caught by the red run, not by the spec pass), the per-unit documentation review's arithmetic findings, and the DONE-pass correction in `docs/pending.md` §G + `docs/next-steps.md`'s `D4` row. **Tests that pin it:** `tests/layout-projection.test.ts`'s register machinery asserts the terms and the total (`PRE-2`/`PRE-3`'s arithmetic rows, seed `20260927`), and `PRE-2` now also **derives and asserts the distinct drawn-pool-member count** (`19` of `20`) so a coverage claim cannot be stated without its measurement. |

| **DECIDED: SLOTHOST-CONTAINER-SOURCE-IS-INJECTED — the slot host's container elements come from an INJECTED element factory; the ambient realm read is DELETED and the static bans are TIGHTENED against assembled/computed member lookups (architect ruling 2026-09-27, taken at `U-SLOTHOST`'s adversarial gate)** | 2026-09-27 | **The finding that forced it (HIGH, `ADV-SH-1`):** `U-SLOTHOST`'s module reached the ambient DOM as **`globalThis['doc' + 'ument'].createElement('div')`** (`src/shared/slot-host.ts:130-140`) — a property name **assembled from two literals** so that the unit's own static rows could not match it (`S-2`'s `/document/i`, `S-4`'s word-boundary list). The read-only adversarial pass ruled it **an evasion of the contract's own prohibition** (`§1`'s "no `document`/`window` reference", `§2.2` prohibition 1's static source row, and the Layer declaration's "reads no ambient global"), and the supervisor verified the line: the module does the banned thing and hides the token. **The architect's ruling (option (a), verbatim in substance: the container source is an injected element factory):** `SlotHostOptions` gains an **optional factory seam** as the SOLE container source; **absent / non-callable / returning an unusable value ⇒ the existing valid-state degradation** (every operation a valid no-op, nothing placeable) — the fifth named safe default alongside `F-10`'s four; the **ambient read is deleted, never documented as a fallback**; and the static rows are **tightened** so an assembled property name, a computed access, or an aliased/re-derived global reference is the same violation as the literal token. **Why this shape (the doctrine it follows):** this repo injects its environment readings rather than reading them ambiently — `U-LISTHOST`'s injected `itemFactory`, the `A-d3` ruling's injected event source for `U-GSESSION`, and `A-d6`'s injected `{prefersDark}` env. It also makes the module portable (no `document` in main/node) and puts the `[T]`/`[U]` split where it belongs: the HARNESS supplies the factory. **⟶ CLOSED-IN-PLACE 2026-09-27 (the unit's documentation review, F-06): `ADV-SH-3`** — the finding that the host wrote a `parent` expando marker onto the caller's node — **is closed by DELETION rather than by the `WeakMap` the adversarial pass prescribed**: the marker had no reader, and a module-local `WeakMap`/registry is **inadmissible twice over** (the unit's own static row `§4.4 S-4` bans the token outright, and `§2.2` prohibition 4 + `I-5` exclude a module-local registry). The assertable form is now *"the caller's node carries no host-written marker AND the host keeps no parent bookkeeping at all"*. **What was rejected and why:** option (b) (a documented ambient factory) would have required re-scoping the bans and rewriting the Layer declaration's "reads no ambient global" sentence — honest but re-introducing the dependency the spec spent a paragraph disclaiming; option (c) (injected plus ambient fallback) was rejected for having two sources with one silent and no row able to say which was used — the falsifiability hole `§2.2` prohibition 6 exists to close; option (d) (keep it as written) was rejected outright, and under it the unit's DONE row could **not** have cited the static rows as "no ambient global" evidence. **Landed under the ruling:** the contract amendment (`docs/specs/slothost.md` — `§2.1`'s options bag + totality-boundary table + the fifth safe default, `§1`/`§2.2`/Layer declaration, `§3.1 M-1`/`M-18`, `§3.3 I-4`, `§3.2`'s per-method table, `§4.4`'s `S-2`/`S-4` re-scope, `§5.2`, `§5.5.1`'s pool note, `§7` item 7, `§8`), then the TestWriter wiring and the Implementer's de-obfuscation. **Recorded with it, so the class is closed repository-wide:** a static prohibition that can be satisfied by splitting a token is **not** a satisfied prohibition — the adversarial pass's evasion sweep found `S-2`/`S-4` passable by assembly (and `S-3`/`S-6`/`S-7` evadable in principle by aliasing/computed specifiers), and `S-5`'s census half brittle and self-referential (it does not assert `VALID_GROUPS` at all). **Every unit that carries source-scanning static rows must state which evasions its rows are closed against.** | architect ruling 2026-09-27 (the `U-SLOTHOST` adversarial gate, option "injected element factory"); `docs/specs/slothost.md` `§3a`/`§3b` (`ADV-SH-1`, and the evasion sweep); `src/shared/slot-host.ts:130-140` (the verified evidence); `docs/decisions.md` `THEME-MECHANISM-AND-AUTHORED-CONTROL` + the `A-d3` ruling (the injection doctrine); the module's import-time side effects are guarded. **The source contract this fix pins:** the MCP tool registers its argument as **`command`** (`src/main/mcp-server.ts:651`, `command: z.unknown()`), so the **wrapped args object `{command: <cmd>}` is the only shape reachable over the transport** (the SDK rejects a bare command object at the MCP layer — `MCP error …`, schema unchanged by this fix); the renderer is therefore the correct — and only — unwrap point, and the unwrap must be **non-destructive**. **Verified in-node:** `tests/op-command-unwrap.test.ts` (**NEW file, 6 rows**) — **`S1`** is the **RED row that pinned the defect** (pre-fix verbatim failure: `expected { command: { kind:'state-slice', … } } to be { kind:'state-slice', … }`), **`S2`** pins the **fallback** (an already-bare payload passes through unchanged), **`S3`** pins that **`load` stays raw**, **`F1`/`F2`** are the fail-safe rows (a throwing `op` → `{ok:false,error}`; an unknown method → `{ok:false,error:'unknown method: …'}`), **`S1b`** pins that a successful op still emits **exactly one** app-graph-changed push. **Verified live (the decisive evidence, after the fix on the rebuilt app):** the **same probe that produced the defect** (`node /tmp/engine-pin-live/op-variants2.mjs` — real Electron, hermetic scratch profile, `--disable-dev-shm-usage`) now returns **`{"status":"applied","dirtied":["node-1"], …}` for BOTH wrapped shapes**, where it returned **`{"status":"rejected"}` before** — the status flip is this defect's own falsifier. **Legs after the fix:** `npm test` **56 files / 795 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed** (was 789 + 2 skipped before the new file) · `npm run typecheck` clean · `npm run build` clean · `npm run battery` **184 checks / 0 failures** · the divergence leg still green (**`R13 RESULT: 9 checks, 0 failures`**). **Layer note (kept):** the envelope layer was **never broken** — the node/battery route was green throughout — the defect lived on the **IPC hop**. **Classification unchanged: HOST-owned and NOT a package defect** → **no `docs/HANDOFF.md` row and no upstream issue is owed**; and it was **PRE-EXISTING, not a `U-ENGINE-PIN` regression** (`src/renderer/renderer.ts` was untouched by that unit). `docs/defects.md`'s `LIVE-OP-REJECT` row moved **OPEN → `## FIXED (in this repo)`**, full as-filed history kept. *(Provenance: the fix pass's landed hunk + its live re-run are the evidence; the 2026-09-27 documentation pass that wrote this row ran no leg — see the provenance note in `docs/defects.md`'s `## FIXED` section.)* | the fix pass's landed hunk + live re-run (2026-09-27); `docs/defects.md` `LIVE-OP-REJECT` (FIXED, with the leg numbers + provenance note); `tests/op-command-unwrap.test.ts`; `src/renderer/renderer.ts:14,35,43`; `src/main/mcp-server.ts:650-651`; `docs/specs/engine-pin-live-status.md` §5 |

the blind-greens FAIL row `RT-08` measured `grep -c PROVIDENT_UI docs/decisions.md` = `0` before it, so a blind re-run could not drive `RT-3`/`RT-4` at runtime from documentation alone):** **`PROVIDENT_UI_BOOT_ATTEMPTS`** — attempts per boot, **default `4`**, **admissible range `1`–`4`** (the operator may **LOWER** it — `1` = *"no retry"* — and may **NEVER RAISE** it above the pinned maximum `4`); ### ACTIVE — the `U-REALDOM-BOOT` SCRATCH-PROFILE decision set (2026-09-27, SIXTH pass — the `F-1` RCA + fix): **spawn the app, register the cleanup at creation**

Added by the **sixth 2026-09-27 pass** (the `F-1` RCA + fix's documentation half; the live battery's
finding is `docs/specs/ci-ui-leg-live-status.md` §6.1). **Two rows, appended at the end of `## ACTIVE`**
for the insertion-hazard reason the placement notes above state: no `docs/decisions.md:<n>` citation
above moves. **Both rules are stated GENERALLY** — they are written so a later leg, harness or agent
tool inherits them, not only so this leg's fix is explained. **The unit stays
`LANDED-GREEN-BUT-NOT-DONE` — these rows record a fix and its verification, NOT a `DONE` row.**

| Decision | Date | What it pins | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **DECIDED: UI-LEG-SPAWN-THE-BINARY-NOT-THE-WRAPPER — NEVER spawn a CLI WRAPPER whose child is the real application: the handle you hold is not the app, and the orphan outlives your cleanup** | 2026-09-27 | **THE RULE, stated so it applies beyond this leg:** when a harness spawns an application through an npm `.bin` entry or any other **launcher**, resolve first **what actually ends up running the app**. If the resolved entry point is a **WRAPPER that `spawn`s the real binary as its own child**, then **the `ChildProcess` handle the harness holds is the WRAPPER, not the app** — and every lifecycle action taken on that handle (kill, wait-for-exit, cleanup-on-exit) governs the **wrapper** while the **real application and its helper processes** (Chromium's, in Electron's case) are **orphaned**. **The failure mode is specifically NASTY because it is a FALSE CLEAN:** the orphan keeps writing into the harness's scratch directories and **RE-CREATES a directory after the harness has unlinked it AND verified the unlink**, so a cleanup report can be **TRUE at the instant it looks and FALSE afterwards** — and a sweep that deletes-and-verifies is **not** a defence against it, because the verifier's own read is taken inside the window. **THE PINNED SHAPE (landed, `scripts/electron-spawn.mjs`):** the helper resolves the **BINARY** — `node_modules/electron/dist/electron`, with a fallback through the package's own **`path.txt` contract** to the bare package entry — under an explicit **NO-FALLBACK-TO-THE-WRAPPER rule that THROWS A NAMED ERROR when no binary is found.** **A named throw is the point:** a silent fallback to the wrapper would restore the defect invisibly, which is exactly how it survived a green unit row set. **THE MEASURED COST OF GETTING IT WRONG HERE (`F-1`, `docs/specs/ci-ui-leg-live-status.md` §6.1):** `4 of 4` repo-leg green runs left **1 root + 2 attempt profiles** on disk while the leg reported `leftover profiles: NONE`; the A/B was **`6 of 6`** runs through the two-process chain leaving the tree versus **`1 of 1`** `exec`-chain runs leaving none; **`31` roots had accumulated in one session**; persistence at **`t+10 s`** ruled out "the check merely raced the deletion". **THE DISCRIMINATING EVIDENCE (the test that names the mechanism, not the symptom):** the leftover profile directories held **only Chromium artifacts and NO `provident-security.json`** — the harness's **own seeded store WAS deleted**, and only the **re-created Chromium state** survived, which is precisely what made the leak invisible. **THE REJECTED ALTERNATIVE, recorded so no later pass "simplifies" into it:** **`detached: true` + a process-group kill was TRIED AND BACKED OUT** — it regressed the SDK stdio transport (**`R13 RESULT: 1 checks, 2 failures`**, i.e. it red the divergence precondition). **A process-group kill is not a substitute for spawning the right process.** **What pins it:** `scripts/electron-spawn.mjs`'s `electronBin` resolution + its named no-binary error; the shared helper is imported by **BOTH** legs, so the rule reaches the divergence leg too (**0 roots** after it, `N = 9` intact); the exit-path matrix at `docs/specs/ci-ui-leg-live-status.md` §6.1 (green · malformed config · timeout-class exhaustion · `npm run ui` — **0 roots immediately and after an 8 s settle**, **0 surviving `electron` processes**); the regression set (`68/68` unit rows · `npm test` **58 / 863 / 2 skipped / 0 failed** · typecheck clean · build clean · battery **184 / 0** · divergence **`9 / 0`**) | the live battery's `F-1` + its fix pass 2026-09-27 (`docs/specs/ci-ui-leg-live-status.md` §6.1); `scripts/electron-spawn.mjs`; `docs/defects.md` `UI-LEG-LEFTOVER-PROFILES` (CLOSED host-side); `docs/pending.md` §E's integrity row |
| **DECIDED: UI-LEG-CLEANUP-HOOK-AT-CREATION — register the process-exit cleanup hook AT THE MOMENT THE RESOURCE IS CREATED, never at the bottom of the file after the validation gates** | 2026-09-27 | **THE RULE, stated generally:** a `process.on('exit')` (or signal) cleanup hook must be registered **in the same statement-block that creates the resource it cleans** — **never later in the module**, and **never after any gate that can call `process.exit`**. A hook registered below a validation gate is a hook that **does not exist on the very path that exits earliest**, and **that path is usually the one a test suite drives most often.** **THE MEASURED DEFECT HERE (`F-1`'s second, strictly deterministic half):** the leg `mkdtemp`'d its run root at **module load** (`scripts/electron-ui.mjs:373`) but registered the `process.on('exit')` hook **after the retry-configuration gate** (`:713-721`), while a malformed value exits at **`:691` — BEFORE the hook exists**. ⇒ **every malformed-config run leaked an EMPTY scratch root**, and **`npm test` created exactly `6` empty `/tmp/provident-ui-run-*` roots per run** — exactly the **six** malformed values the unit's own row `RT-2e` spawns (`tests/ui-leg-contract.test.ts:1654`, `['9','abc','0','2.5','-1',' 4']`). **THE FIX (landed):** the hook is registered **at the moment `scratchRoot` is created**, and it is **IDEMPOTENT** — so the signal handlers and the explicit call sites **still work unchanged**, and a **`typeof activeBoot !== 'undefined'` guard** covers an exit during **module evaluation**. **Note what the shape of this defect teaches, because it is the general lesson and not a detail of this leg:** the **reporting** fix (a delete-and-verify sweep + a recorded cleanup report) had already landed and **could not see this leak at all** — the sweep ran and was **accurate**; the directory was created **after** it. **A cleanup that verifies its own delete is not the same thing as an END STATE that stays clean**, and only a **post-exit** check (immediately **and** after a settle window) can tell them apart. **What pins it:** `scripts/electron-ui.mjs`'s hook registration moved to `scratchRoot` creation + its idempotence + the `typeof activeBoot` guard; the exit-path matrix at `docs/specs/ci-ui-leg-live-status.md` §6.1 (the malformed-config path is a **named row** of it: exit `1`, **roots 0**); the re-runnable command in that record's §10 (`PROVIDENT_UI_BOOT_ATTEMPTS=abc …` ⇒ exit 1 **and 0 roots**); the regression set as the row above | the live battery's `F-1` + its fix pass 2026-09-27 (`docs/specs/ci-ui-leg-live-status.md` §6.1); `scripts/electron-ui.mjs`; `docs/defects.md` `UI-LEG-LEFTOVER-PROFILES` (CLOSED host-side) |
