**the implementation is `scripts/electron-ui.mjs`** (with `scripts/electron-spawn.mjs`'s cleanup half for the profile defect below). **What it pins, exact:** **(1) THE RETRYABLE SET IS TWO SIGNATURES — never a `stderr` text match alone, never a generic *"anything failed"* predicate:** **(i)** an **observed child termination** — a non-zero exit code or a terminating signal, the raw **`(code, signal)`** pair recorded **verbatim** — **AND an unresolved handshake**; **(ii)** the **TIMEOUT class** — the **ABSENCE of an observed termination** **plus** the handshake-not-completed fact, recorded verbatim (**the PRECEDENCE CLAUSE**; it **supersedes `RT-1`'s as-filed conjunction IN PART** and is implemented as an **observed record fact, NEVER by re-parsing the timeout message**). **(2) BOUNDS:** **max `4` attempts per boot** — the operator may **LOWER** via **`PROVIDENT_UI_BOOT_ATTEMPTS`** (default `4`, admissible range `1`–`4`; `1` means *"no retry"*) but **NEVER RAISE**; **a malformed value ⇒ exit `1`, never a silent clamp**; **`30 000 ms`** per-attempt handshake timeout via **`PROVIDENT_UI_BOOT_TIMEOUT_MS`** (default `30 000 ms`, range `≥ 1 ms`; malformed ⇒ exit `1`); **fixed backoff `250`/`500`/`1000 ms`** (the sequence IS the contract; no jitter, no randomness); **`121 750 ms` per-boot wall-clock ceiling** — **the derivation is the pin** (the `122 000 ms` label was a rounded copy that became a second, conflicting pin and is struck in place in the spec; the spec's FIX 1). **(3) A FRESH scratch profile per attempt**, and **EVERY attempt is RECORDED with its verbatim signature** (`RT-5`); **a retried green is LABELLED** (`RT-6 RETRY GREEN … attempt=<k> (retries=<k-1>)`) and **ONE measurement stays ONE** — a retry never multiplies measurements. **(4) EXHAUSTION IS NOT A PASS: exit `1`** — the §3.6 code for a boot/measurement failure, **NOT `0`, NOT `2`, NOT `3`** — printing **`RT-7 EXHAUSTED: n of n attempts failed (k retries)`**, **every attempt's signature in attempt order (attempt `1` first)** and a **no-measurement statement**. **Why `1` and not `2`:** exhaustion is **not** a precondition failure — `2` is reserved for *"`npm run divergence` was not green for the same built tree"* — and reporting it as `2` would misattribute an environment/bootstrap flake to the divergence precondition. **(5) `RT-2`'s classes stay NON-retryable and consume NO attempt** (a **completed** handshake with a wrong measurement or row; a **genuine divergence red — including the `1 checks, 2 failures` bootstrap signature**; a **DISPLAY absence**; a **leg programming error**), **`RT-8`'s `PRE-1`/`PRE-3` authority order is NOT weakened** (a `ui` green is never stronger than a `divergence` red), and the DISPLAY half is still **not** a retry case. **(6) SCOPE — THE RETRY IS LEG-LOCAL to `scripts/electron-ui.mjs`:** the **shared helper and the `divergence` leg carry NONE**, **`N = 9` is untouched**, and the divergence leg's identical flake stays **outside this unit** (`RT-9`(a): its retry belongs to `U-DIVERGENCE-EXT` or a ruling-specific row, on **that** unit's diff scope). **What pins it:** the spec's §3.7 clause set + PRECEDENCE CLAUSE (normative) and **`39` new `RT-*` rows** in `tests/ui-leg-contract.test.ts` (**`59` rows in that file**; with the `9` seam rows the unit's set is **`68/68` green**) — **with the honest process record that those rows were written AFTER the first implementation** (red-first order **INVERTED** for that clause set; falsifiability established **out-of-tree** by a **`32`-mutation matrix** plus stub probes on a `/tmp/` copy), and that the **follow-up timeout re-point RESTORED red-first order** with a real cycle — **3 red (`RT-4c`, `RT-1e`, `RT-7f`) → green** after the classification change. **Falsifiability limits, stated so no row is over-read:** every `RT-*` row is **node/stub-level**; the **runtime half** (a real retry, a real exhaustion, a genuinely red precondition) is **NOT-BLIND-RUNNABLE** (`docs/specs/ci-ui-leg-greens.md` `NBR-03`/`NBR-04`/`NBR-05`) and the retry change has had **NO live verification** — blocked on the runtime row below |
| **DECIDED: ELECTRON-RUNTIME-NON-FUNCTIONAL-ENV-BLOCKER — ⟶ ✅ RESOLVED / MISATTRIBUTED (2026-09-27, FOURTH pass): the blocker was NOT a host/system regression. THE ROOT CAUSE WAS A CORRUPTED npm SHIM INSIDE THIS REPO'S `node_modules` — `node_modules/electron/cli.js`, the file `node_modules/.bin/electron` symlinks to, had been replaced by a SHELL SCRIPT that re-execs itself (`#!/bin/sh` … `exec …/node_modules/.bin/electron "$@"`), so every boot re-entered `exec` in an infinite loop at ~99 % CPU printing nothing. THE FIX: the correct Node wrapper was restored from a healthy install of the same package (`electron` 44.4.3/44.4.5 — identical wrapper: `#!/usr/bin/env node`, `require('./')`, `spawn(electron, process.argv.slice(2), {stdio:'inherit'})`, + the SIGINT/SIGTERM/SIGUSR2 forwarding); a post-repair boot probe exits `0` in `176 ms` printing `BOOT-OK 44.4.5`. THE DISCRIMINATING TEST WAS A TRACE OF THE RESOLVED ENTRY POINT, NOT "does another repo fail too": `strace -f` showed an unbroken cycle of `openat(… node_modules/.bin/electron)` with no `clone`/`execve` progress, `/proc/<pid>/status` = `State: R (running)` at 99 % CPU, and 0 `EPERM`/`EACCES` markers — i.e. no syscall denial at boot. CORROBORATION AFTER THE FIX (measured 2026-09-27, post-repair): `npm run divergence` → `R13 RESULT: 9 checks, 0 failures` (all 8 comparisons green, census 12/12 both legs); `npm run ui` → exit 0, `UI RESULT: 0 failures (11/11 assertions green, mapped onto the five declared rows R0-R4)`, `retries=0`, the ONE measurement `427x22` at `fontSize="16px"` via `provident.get_rendered_html`, scratch cleanup verified (`removed 2 scratch profile(s) … leftover profiles: NONE`); one environment noise line remains and is NOT a failure (`dconf-CRITICAL … '/run/user/1000/dconf/user': Permission denied` — settings persistence only). RESIDUAL INTEGRITY HAZARD (the thing to keep): this is an npm-shim-INTEGRITY hazard, not a system one — the corrupt file's mtime is 2026-09-24 14:01 **⟶ MISATTRIBUTION RCA — WHAT HELD THE WRONG CONCLUSION UP (recorded from the RCA, 2026-09-27; appended to this cell so the row carries its own lesson, and so a later pass cannot re-derive the wrong answer from the as-filed text):** **(1) A SECOND REPOSITORY ON THE SAME MACHINE runs the same spawn vector** — this repo's divergence harness was itself derived from `Astrographer`'s — so running *its* harness reproduced the same `SIGTRAP` / `1 checks, 2 failures` and **appeared to prove the environment was at fault, when in fact both were hitting their own copies of the same problem (each its own corrupted `electron/cli.js`).** **(2) The `/dev/shm` permission denial is REAL but is a SEPARATE, OLDER failure mode** (`/dev/shm` is `drwxrwxrwt` mode 600 root-owned here, so a non-root write fails) **already mitigated by the landed `--disable-dev-shm-usage` + scratch-profile flags — it was NOT the cause of the hang.** **The A/B evidence used in the third pass ("the unmodified HEAD helper fails identically") was CORRECT ABOUT THE HELPER AND WRONG ABOUT THE CAUSE:** both versions **spawn through the same corrupted entry point**, so the experiment **could not discriminate**. **The discriminator that WOULD have settled it, named for the next pass:** a **trace of the resolved entry point** (`strace -f` + `file -L` on `node_modules/electron/cli.js`), never a cross-repo failure comparison. **⟶ THE ROW IS CLOSED AND NOTHING IS BLOCKED BY IT; the recommendation it leaves is a one-line integrity check in the leg precondition — recommended, NOT implemented** (see the `NPM-SHIM-INTEGRITY` row below and `docs/pending.md` §E).ll/reinstall that replaced the wrapper) and only `electron/cli.js` was affected (`node_modules/.bin/{esbuild,tsc,vitest}` intact); NOTHING IN THIS REPO DETECTS IT, because a corrupted entry point produces a silent CPU-spinning hang that is indistinguishable from a wedged host unless someone traces it. RECOMMENDED (not implemented — this pass is documentation-only): a ONE-LINE INTEGRITY CHECK in the divergence/`ui` leg's precondition (is the resolved Electron entry point the expected Node wrapper — `file -L` says a Node script, not a shell script?). See `docs/pending.md` §E's integrity-check item and the ACTIVE lesson row below.** — *the row's as-filed title and text below are the MISATTRIBUTED reading, kept verbatim as the RCA's own evidence and labelled **WRONG*** | 2026-09-27 | **⚠ THE AS-FILED TEXT BELOW IS THE MISATTRIBUTION AND IS MARKED WRONG — it is kept (not deleted) because it is the RCA's evidence, and its two red herrings are recorded in the appended `MISATTRIBUTION RCA` paragraph at the END of this cell.** **The as-filed claim:** *"the host's Electron runtime has stopped booting: an ENVIRONMENT regression, A/B-proven not this repo's diff, and it BLOCKS every live leg"* — **WRONG: there was no environment regression; the defective file was inside this repo's own `node_modules`.** **The measured signature (as recorded, verbatim):** a **direct Electron launch produces NO output and must be killed after 25–40 s**; **`npm run divergence`** fails **`✗ electron connect/drive failed: MCP error -32001: Request timed out`** → **`R13 RESULT: 1 checks, 2 failures`**; **`npm run ui` hangs in its own precondition**; and a bare **`electron -e 'console.log(1)'`** probe **also hangs**. **PROOF IT IS NOT THIS UNIT'S (A/B, run by the implementer):** the **same failure reproduces with the UNMODIFIED HEAD spawn helper**; it **began BEFORE any of this pass's edits**; and the bare probe hangs **outside this repo's code entirely**. **Contributing factors recorded:** the **divergence leg's own leaked Electron wrapper processes (~95 % CPU each)** and the **sandbox's `/dev/shm` denial**. **What it BLOCKED:** **`npm run divergence` AND `npm run ui`** — i.e. **every live leg of `U-REALDOM-BOOT`** (all of its node/stub rows are green; **the retry has had NO live verification**) — and **`U-DIVERGENCE-EXT`'s declared leg** with it. **What it does NOT do:** it **amends NO contract clause**, it does **NOT** mark any unit `DONE`, and it does **NOT** turn the `1 checks, 2 failures` line into a retry case (`RT-8`) or into a `ui` pass — **no unit may read a missing live leg as a green.** **The last GOOD evidence stands, dated:** **`R13 RESULT: 9 checks, 0 failures`** (the divergence leg, measured earlier on the **post-change** tree) — it is **pre-regression** and **cannot retire a live row**. **ON RECOVERY, IN ORDER:** **(1)** re-run **`npm run divergence`** and require **`R13 RESULT: 9 checks, 0 failures`** (`N = 9`); **(2)** re-run **`npm run ui`** and require `UI RESULT: 0 failures (11/11 assertions green, mapped onto the five declared rows R0-R4)` with **`retries=0`** on a clean tree, the ONE measurement **`427x22`** at `fontSize="16px"`, and **`ls -d /tmp/provident-ui-run-*` EMPTY afterwards** (**the `5/5 rows green` form is STALE and must not be re-quoted**); **(3)** drive the **retry's runtime half** with the recorded knobs (`PROVIDENT_UI_BOOT_TIMEOUT_MS` for a deterministic timeout-class retry; `PROVIDENT_UI_BOOT_ATTEMPTS=1` for *"no retry"*) so `RT-1`(ii)/`RT-3`/`RT-4`/`RT-5`/`RT-6`(a)/`RT-7` move off NOT-BLIND-RUNNABLE; **(4)** then the per-unit **documentation review** (item 10d/RCA-6) and the **adversarial pass's own record** (§3a/§3b); **(5)** only then the supervisor's **`DONE` row**. **Revisit condition: the Electron runtime boots again** — the bare `electron -e 'console.log(1)'` probe prints, and `npm run divergence` prints its nine |

| **DECIDED: UI-LEG-TIMEOUT-CLASS-RACE-SNAPSHOT-RULE — the timeout signature IS SAMPLED AT THE TIMER INSTANT, and the retryability predicate may NEVER mix a LIVE flag with a RECORDED fact (the `ui` leg's timeout-class retryability RACE: RCA + fix, 2026-09-27)** | 2026-09-27 | **THE RULE, stated so it can be applied to any later predicate (not only this leg's):** **a retryability/eligibility predicate must be handed values that describe ONE INSTANT.** Where a signature is **conjunctive** — here *"the absence of an observed child termination"* **∧** *"the handshake did not complete"* (`docs/specs/ci-ui-leg.md` §3.7 `RT-4` item 3 + the PRECEDENCE CLAUSE (a)) — **both halves must be read at the instant the verdict settles**, never one at settle and the other after a later `await`. **A LIVE flag sampled after an `await` is a TORN READ**, and the class of the verdict then depends on a race between the leg's own timer and a late event rather than on a deterministic signature. **THE RCA, exact (all measured this pass — the live battery's finding `F-2`):** `scripts/electron-ui.mjs`'s failed-attempt path handed `isBootstrapDeath` the **live** `resolved` flag — `rec.retryable = isBootstrapDeath(rec, resolved, rec.timedOut)` — on a line that runs **AFTER `await observeTermination(child, timeoutMs)`**, the bounded termination-grace look, so the **handshake flag was sampled ~2 s after** the `timedOut` fact. **Instrumented at the decision point:** `timeout=120 ms` → `resolvedAtCatch=false` then `DIAG-after-grace resolvedNow=false death=none` ⇒ **retried (correct)**; `timeout=200/260 ms` → `resolvedAtCatch=false` then **`DIAG-after-grace resolvedNow=true death=none`** ⇒ the **`!handshakeResolved` half flipped false** while the **recorded `timedOut` half stayed true**, so the attempt was classified **non-retryable** and the leg **aborted on attempt 1** (`✗ … attempt 1 failed non-retryably (RT-2)`, attempts 2–4 unrecorded, no `RT-7` marker, exit `1`). **The boundary was NON-MONOTONIC:** `30`/`100 ms` retried to exhaustion, **`200`/`260 ms` aborted on attempt 1**, **`300`/`400 ms` went green** — a boundary that moves the **wrong way** as the budget grows is the signature of a **race**, not of a threshold. **The boot was ALIVE AND SLOW** (a real handshake completing just after the deadline), so the leg was discarding a **perfectly healthy boot**. **THE FIX (landed):** **snapshot the handshake state at the instant the raced verdict settles, before the grace is awaited** — `const handshakeResolvedAtSettle = resolved` in the `catch`, immediately after the message is computed (`scripts/electron-ui.mjs:641`, read) — and hand the predicate the **snapshot**, not the live flag: `isBootstrapDeath(rec, handshakeResolvedAtSettle, rec.timedOut)` (`:660`, read; passed **before** the `await` at `:651`). **THE CONTRACT DID NOT MOVE:** no clause of `docs/specs/ci-ui-leg.md` changed — `RT-1`/`RT-2`/`RT-4` item 3 and the **PRECEDENCE CLAUSE** are byte-unchanged; the fix made the **landed leg match the clause it already had**. **THE EVIDENCE — the boundary is now MONOTONIC:** post-fix **`120`/`200`/`260 ms`** → **`RT-7 EXHAUSTED: 4 of 4 (3 retries)`**, exit **`1`**, **every attempt recorded in order**; **`300 ms`** → **accepted**, **`UI RESULT: 0 failures (11/11 assertions green, mapped onto the five declared rows R0-R4)`**, measurement **`427x22`**. **Regression-free:** the unit's rows **`68/68`** · `npm test` **58 files / 863 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed** · typecheck clean · battery **184 checks / 0 failures** · `npm run divergence` **`R13 RESULT: 9 checks, 0 failures`** · `npm run ui` → **exit 0**, **`11/11`**, **`427x22`**, **`retries=0`**. **WHY THE ROW SET DID NOT CATCH IT (recorded honestly, and it is the general lesson):** the unit's `RT-*` rows drive the predicate with **hand-built attempt records**, so they pin the predicate's **shape** and **cannot observe the live sampling point**; the race lives in the **order of two reads** in the leg's `catch` path, which is only reachable in a **real boot**. **The live battery found it** (`F-2`), and **`F-2` is now CLOSED by this fix.** | **WHAT PINS IT:** the closed finding `F-2` with its instrumented evidence and post-fix boundary table (`docs/specs/ci-ui-leg-live-status.md` §6.2, the as-filed text kept struck-but-visible) · the **status/annotation note** in `docs/specs/ci-ui-leg.md` (**no normative clause changed**) · the **CLOSED host-side row** `docs/defects.md` `UI-LEG-TIMEOUT-CLASS-RACE` (HOST/leg-owned → **no `docs/HANDOFF.md` round and no upstream issue**, either way) · **RECOMMENDED, NOT IMPLEMENTED:** a **snapshot-ordering test row** — a TestWriter-side change, **not this pass's** — that asserts the predicate receives the handshake state read **at settle** (e.g. a harness that flips `resolved` during the termination grace and requires the predicate to still see `false`; the landed `bootAttemptHarness` never exercises this, `tests/ui-leg-contract.test.ts:959-1018`, read). **Read this row as the general rule, not as a leg footnote: any later conjunctive eligibility predicate in this repo inherits it.** *(ANCHOR NOTE, its own row included: the `docs/decisions.md:<n>` citations this row carries (`ELECTRON-RUNTIME-NON-FUNCTIONAL-ENV-BLOCKER` `:166`, `NPM-SHIM-INTEGRITY` `:168`) are **as read before this row was inserted into `## ACTIVE`** — inserting it moves every later line by one. **Cite the row NAMES, not the digits, and treat any digit in this file as a dated read.**)* |

| **DECIDED: NPM-SHIM-INTEGRITY — a CORRUPTED npm SHIM produces a SILENT CPU-SPINNING HANG, and the discriminating test is a TRACE OF THE RESOLVED ENTRY POINT — never "does another repo fail too" (RECORDED 2026-09-27, FOURTH pass — the `ELECTRON-RUNTIME-NON-FUNCTIONAL-ENV-BLOCKER` RCA's lesson)** | 2026-09-27 | **What happened, and why it is a row rather than a footnote:** a live-leg blocker was carried for multiple passes as an **ENVIRONMENT regression** (`docs/decisions.md` `ELECTRON-RUNTIME-NON-FUNCTIONAL-ENV-BLOCKER`, now annotated `RESOLVED / MISATTRIBUTED`) when the true cause was **one corrupted file inside this repo's own `node_modules`** — `electron/cli.js` replaced by a shell script that re-enters `exec` on itself. **THE LESSON, in the order it has to be applied: (1) a SILENT HANG IS AN ENTRY-POINT SYMPTOM, NOT A HOST SYMPTOM.** A **fast crash** (the earlier `SIGTRAP`) and a **silent 99 %-CPU hang printing nothing** are **different failure modes**; the hang is what an **infinite re-exec of the resolved entry point** looks like, so "the host is wedged" is never the first hypothesis while the launch prints nothing. **(2) THE DISCRIMINATING TEST IS A TRACE OF THE RESOLVED ENTRY POINT.** The correct experiment is `strace -f` on the launch (an unbroken `openat(… node_modules/.bin/electron)` cycle, the loader re-running each time, **no `clone`/`execve` progress**, `/proc/<pid>/status` = `State: R (running)` at ~99 % CPU, **0 `EPERM`/`EACCES` markers**) plus an integrity read of the entry point itself (`file -L` ⇒ a **Node** script, not a **shell** script). **The wrong experiment is "does another repository/harness fail too?"** — see (3). **(3) A SECOND REPO ON THE SAME MACHINE IS NOT A CONTROL WHEN BOTH SPAWN THROUGH THEIR OWN COPIES.** This repo's divergence harness was derived from `Astrographer`'s; running that repo's harness reproduced the same `SIGTRAP` / `1 checks, 2 failures` and *appeared* to prove the environment was at fault — **both were hitting their own copies of the same corruption**, so the comparison **could not discriminate** (the same defect as the earlier A/B: *"the unmodified HEAD helper fails identically"* was **correct about the helper and wrong about the cause**, because **both versions spawn through the same corrupted entry point**). **(4) A KNOWN ADJACENT FAILURE MODE IS A RED HERRING.** The `/dev/shm` permission denial is **real** (`/dev/shm` is 600 root-owned here) but is a **separate, older** failure mode already mitigated by the landed `--disable-dev-shm-usage` + fresh scratch-profile flags — **it was not the cause of the hang**, and its presence must not be used to close the question. **(5) THE STANDING RECOMMENDATION, NOT YET IMPLEMENTED:** a **one-line integrity check** in the divergence/`ui` leg's **precondition** — is the **resolved Electron entry point** the expected Node wrapper (`file -L` says a Node script, not a shell script)? **Recommended, never implemented by the recording pass**; parked at `docs/pending.md` §E with its revisit condition (a pass that touches the harness precondition), and the hazard is filed CLOSED host-side in `docs/defects.md`. **Scope note:** the corruption/repair touched **no `src/**`, `tests/**` or `scripts/**` file** — the corrupt path is an **install artifact** (`node_modules/electron/cli.js`, mtime 2026-09-24 14:01, written by an install/reinstall; only that one file was affected). | the `ELECTRON-RUNTIME-NON-FUNCTIONAL-ENV-BLOCKER` RCA (2026-09-27, FOURTH pass); the post-repair live evidence (`npm run divergence` → `R13 RESULT: 9 checks, 0 failures`; `npm run ui` → exit 0, `UI RESULT: 0 failures (11/11 assertions green, mapped onto the five declared rows R0-R4)`, `retries=0`); `docs/defects.md` `NPM-SHIM-INTEGRITY` (CLOSED, host-side); `docs/pending.md` §E; `docs/next-steps.md`'s `WAVE-C UPDATE` resolution block |


### ACTIVE — the `U-REALDOM-BOOT` adversarial-FIX decision set (2026-09-27, the ADVERSARIAL-FIX pass): **the leg's own environment observation, and evidence rows that OBSERVE what they print**

Added by the **adversarial-fix pass** (the documentation half of the fixes for the pass's HOST findings
`G-1`/`G-2`/`G-3`/`G-6`/`G-7`/`G-13`; the pass's record is `docs/specs/ci-ui-leg.md` §3a/§3b, and the DONE
record is `docs/next-steps.md`'s **`## DONE — U-REALDOM-BOOT`** section with its dated
**`⟶ ADVERSARIAL-FIX PASS`** note). **Three rows, appended at the end of `## ACTIVE`** for the
insertion-hazard reason the placement notes above state: no `docs/decisions.md:<n>` citation above moves.
**All three rules are stated GENERALLY** — a later leg, harness or agent tool inherits them. **The unit's
status does not change: `U-REALDOM-BOOT` stays `DONE` on every leg its spec declares, and these rows record
fixed findings + their general rules, not a new gate.**

| Decision | Date | What it pins | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **DECIDED: LEG-ENV-PREREQUISITES-ON-OWN-OBSERVATION — an environment PREREQUISITE must be decided on the LEG'S OWN observation of the OPERATOR's environment, NEVER on a default the harness MANUFACTURES for a child it invokes** | 2026-09-27 | **THE RULE, stated so it applies beyond this leg:** when a leg **decides** a prerequisite about the operator's environment (a display, a socket, a credential, a path, a version), it must read **the operator's own environment as the leg itself sees it**, **taken before** anything it invokes can mutate or substitute that value. **A value injected into a CHILD's env by the harness — `DISPLAY \|\| ':0'`, a fallback path, a default host — is a value the harness MANUFACTURED, and it must NEVER be read as evidence about the operator's environment.** Two distinct consumers, two different rules: the **child** may legitimately be given a default **so it can run** (a substitution that keeps a helper working), while the **leg's own PREREQUISITE DECISION** must use the **un-substituted operator value** — otherwise the leg **cannot observe the very condition its refusal exists to report**, and its documented prerequisite exit code becomes **unreachable through its declared entry point**. **THE ORDERING IS PART OF THE RULE:** the observation must be taken **BEFORE the thing that could substitute it runs**, because after that point the leg can no longer distinguish the operator's value from the manufactured one. **THE MEASURED DEFECT THIS ENCODES (`G-1`, MED, the pass's headline finding; HOST/leg-owned, `scripts/electron-ui.mjs`):** the leg ran the divergence **precondition** through `electronEnv()`, which **injects `DISPLAY \|\| ':0'`** into the precondition child, while the leg's **own DISPLAY check read the RAW `process.env.DISPLAY`** ⇒ on a display-less host the **precondition** failed first and the leg exited **`2` `PRECONDITION-FAILED`**, **never the documented `3` `PREREQUISITE ERROR`** — i.e. the leg's own refusal was reported as *"the tree is bad"* rather than *"you have no display"*. **THE FIX (LANDED, `scripts/electron-ui.mjs`):** the leg takes **its OWN observation of the operator's `DISPLAY` BEFORE the precondition spawns anything**, and the **divergence-child spawn no longer injects `DISPLAY \|\| ':0'`** — so **the precondition cannot manufacture a display the operator lacks**. **WHAT IS EXPLICITLY UNCHANGED:** **§3.1's DECISION order (precondition → display) is unchanged**, so **`PRE-1`/`PRE-3` (`a ui green is NOT stronger than a divergence red`) and `RT-8` item 2 (a red precondition is never retried) are INTACT**; the red-precondition branch now prints a **`G-1` diagnostic NOTE only** (diagnostic-only: **no exit code, no verdict, no measurement moves**); and `DIS-2`'s clause text is the contract and was **not** rewritten. **MEASURED OUTCOME, kept exact so the regime is not over-claimed:** on **THIS** host (a real X server answers at `:0`) `env -u DISPLAY npm run ui` exits **`3` BOTH BEFORE AND AFTER** the fix — **the recorded defect expected `2`, and it did NOT reproduce here** because the manufactured `:0` is **live**; **the 2-vs-3 regime needs a host with NO X server AT ALL**, which the implementer reproduced **separately** (`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` ⇒ red precondition ⇒ **exit `2`**, `PRECONDITION-FAILED`, the `G-1` note, `NO MEASUREMENT TAKEN`, **0 roots**). **THE NAMED RESIDUAL (owner: the divergence leg's `PRE-4` / `U-DIVERGENCE-EXT`):** a **display-less host with no X server still exits `2`**, because the **PINNED** divergence leg manufactures `:0` **for its own child** (`scripts/electron-divergence.mjs`, `PRE-4` — **not this unit's to edit**); parked with its revisit condition at `docs/pending.md` §F. **HOST row:** `docs/defects.md` `UI-LEG-DISPLAY-PRECONDITION`, filed CLOSED host-side — **no `docs/HANDOFF.md` round owed, no upstream issue owed** (the `R13-HOST-FIX` precedent). | the `G-1` fix pass's measurements; `docs/specs/ci-ui-leg.md` §3a `AP-5`/`U-9` + §3b `G-1`; `docs/defects.md` `UI-LEG-DISPLAY-PRECONDITION`; `docs/pending.md` §F |
| **DECIDED: EVIDENCE-ROW-MUST-OBSERVE-WHAT-IT-PRINTS — an evidence row may print ONLY what it OBSERVES: a row that ASSERTS a claim must WITNESS it (a read-only witness of the real subject), and a row that checks a SET of code sites must assert the SET, never a PHRASE** | 2026-09-27 | **THE RULE, stated so it applies to every leg and every harness:** a row's **printed claim** and its **assertion** must be the **same object**. Two shapes are forbidden, and both shipped on this leg: **(1) a claim INFERRED from a proxy** — `R0`(c) printed *"no default-profile store touched"* while checking **only the leg's own scratch store + the `tools/list` code-group surface**: **the real `userData` directory was never looked at**, so the printed claim was about a subject the row never examined. **The fix shape is a READ-ONLY WITNESS of the real subject, taken BEFORE and compared AFTER:** a read-only operator-profile witness — **directory existence + each store's presence/mtime/size, NEVER contents** — captured before the precondition and compared after, printed in the row. **Read-only is part of the rule:** a witness that writes the thing it witnesses is not a witness. **(2) a PHRASE where the clause names a SET** — `R4`'s executed row tested **only `/app\.isPackaged/`** while printing *"no packaged-bundle reference"*, and **its printed text was FALSE**: the source legitimately contains the word `packaged` inside `HONEST_LIMITS`, and the **`webContents.executeJavaScript` / CDP half went UNTESTED**. **The fix shape is a COMMENT-AWARE call-site SET — `app.isPackaged`, `webContents.executeJavaScript`, `webContents.debugger` — with the row PRINTING EXACTLY WHAT IT CHECKS.** **A comment naming a site is not a site** (a substring/phrase scan would go falsely RED on the leg's own honest-limits prose); **a real call site in the leg's own code is a site** (and must fail the row). **WHY THE "PRINT EXACTLY WHAT IT CHECKS" HALF IS NOT COSMETIC:** the row's value is that a reader can check it; a row whose printed claim is **wider** than its assertion buys a green for a claim nobody tested, and a row whose printed text is **false** actively misleads. **FALSIFIED OUT OF TREE:** `G-2` — real run ⇒ **`unchanged:true`**; a **simulated write** to the operator profile ⇒ **mtime change**; a **simulated creation** ⇒ **presence `false→true`** (so the witness discriminates, and it is not a tautology). `G-3` — **real source ⇒ `0` sites**; **a comment naming a site ⇒ `0`** (**no false red**); **a real call site ⇒ `1`** and **the row FAILS**; the `HONEST_LIMITS` prose word *"packaged"* ⇒ **still `0`**. **HOST rows:** `docs/defects.md` `UI-LEG-EVIDENCE-ROW-OBSERVES-ITS-CLAIM`, filed CLOSED host-side (`G-2` + `G-3`; | **DECIDED: PROJECTION-REUSABLE — a `Projection` is a REUSABLE VALUE, not a session: no consumption, no per-projection state, and every call is equivalent to a first call with that value (architect ruling `A-11`, 2026-09-27)** | 2026-09-27 | **THE RULING, verbatim in substance:** *the same projection object may be passed to `applyProjection` repeatedly — to the same sink or different sinks — with independent results; no consumption, no per-projection state. Every call must be equivalent to a first call with that value.* **WHY IT FOLLOWS, rather than being a new promise:** the architect's `A-7` ruling makes the projection **immutable input** and this repo's applier already retains **no reference** to it (`I-5`) while returning **fresh** records (`I-8`) — **there is nothing to consume and nowhere for per-projection state to live.** **WHAT IT PINS:** invariant **`I-11`** (`docs/specs/projection.md` §3.3) and **§2.3 item 1** (the write-or-skip rules read the projection as an **input**, never as a cursor); **`ApplyResult` stays CALL-LOCAL** — `ok`/`skipped` describe **only the call that produced them** (`I-10`), so two calls over one projection yield **two independent results**; and **reuse is NOT a second write per key** — each call **is** a commit, so K keys applied twice is **2K** `setProperty` calls, one per key **per call** (§2.3 item 7). **Rows:** `M-21` (reuse across DIFFERENT sinks, with the projection byte-identical afterwards) and `M-22` (reuse into the SAME sink, and the `applyVarsToRoot` alias reusing identically). **It changes NO unit status:** no code, no red set, and `U-PROJ` remains `BLOCKED` on the wave-D go-ahead and its red set (`PROJECTION-SIGNATURE-APPROVAL-SCOPE` above). **⟶ THE GO-AHEAD HALF IS SUPERSEDED (2026-09-27, the `U-MOUNTGUARD` DONE pass): the wave-D go-ahead WAS GIVEN (architect, 2026-09-27); `U-PROJ` is now blocked only on its own red set (see amendment note 21).** | architect ruling `A-11` (2026-09-27); `docs/specs/projection.md` **§0A ruling note 1**, §2.3 item 1, §3.1 `M-21`/`M-22`, §3.3 `I-11`, §8 |
| **DECIDED: PROJECTION-THROWING-ACCESSOR-IS-A-SKIP — a value accessor that THROWS is caught PER KEY, recorded as the NEW eighth `ProjectionSkipReason` member `'accessor-threw'`, and NEVER propagated: one bad key never aborts a projection (architect ruling `A-2`/`F-4`, 2026-09-27)** | 2026-09-27 | **THE RULING, verbatim in substance:** *when reading a value throws (a hostile or absent accessor), the projection catches per key, records that key as a skip, and continues; one bad key never aborts a projection.* **IT IS THE SAME PRINCIPLE THE APPLIER ALREADY CARRIED:** *"a throwing `setProperty` does not abort the run"* (`docs/specs/projection.md` §2.3 item 3) — the projection half is now **symmetric** with the applier: an environment failure (an accessor that throws, a sink that refuses) is a **recorded skip**, never an abort and never a propagated throw. **THE CHOICE, and this row states plainly that it CHANGES A CLOSED UNION:** `ProjectionSkipReason` is **7 → 8 members** — **`'accessor-threw'`**, with its diagnostic sentence *"the value's accessor threw while it was being read; the key was skipped and the projection continued."* **The rejected alternative:** **reusing `'not-a-number'` would SILENTLY CONFLATE** *"the value is not a number"* (the caller's data is the wrong type) with *"we could not read the value at all"* (the caller's accessor is hostile/broken) — **information the consumer cannot recover from `not-a-number`**, which is the whole point of a closed skip vocabulary. **A closed union may grow by ruling; what it may not do is misreport.** **THE RECOUNT (every place the vocabulary count or the reason list is stated, so no later pass has to hunt one):** §2.1's union (7 → 8) · §2.2 prohibition 1 (*"seven contract diagnostics"* → **eight**) · §2.4 item 3's **precedence** (the read happens ONCE; `accessor-threw` takes the place of `missing-value`/`not-a-number` when the read threw) · §3.2's **`F-4` SPLIT** into **`F-4A`** (the plain non-numeric inputs keep `not-a-number`) + **`F-4B`** (the throwing accessor, with the pre-ruling *"the throw propagates out of the getter"* sentence kept visible and marked SUPERSEDED) · the new row **`F-12`** · §7 item 8 (FOUR unruled seeds → **ZERO**) · §8's `RK-10` row and its totality-range cell (`F-1`..**`F-11`** → `F-1`..**`F-15`**). **THE OPERATIONAL REQUIREMENT, recorded because the architect asked for it explicitly:** **downstream code MUST inspect the result's skip record.** **A consumer that only checks a value's PRESENCE will not see a skipped key** — the projection reports **INTENT** (`Projection.applied`/`skipped` = what must and must not be written), while **the applier's `ApplyResult` is what a caller CHECKS** (`ok`/`skipped` = what actually landed). **A key can be present in the caller's `values` and still skipped**, so a presence test is not the contract. **No unit status changes.** | architect ruling `A-2`/`F-4` (2026-09-27); `docs/specs/projection.md` **§0A ruling note 2**, §2.1, §2.2 prohibition 1, §2.3 item 3, §2.4 items 2/3, §3.2 `F-4A`/`F-4B`/`F-12`, §8 |
| **DECIDED: PROJECTION-RECORD-IS-NULL-PROTOTYPE — `Projection.applied`, `ApplyResult.applied` and EVERY internal key→value map are built on `Object.create(null)`, because a caller-supplied `VarSpec.name` may be any string (architect ruling `A-3`, 2026-09-27)** | 2026-09-27 | **THE HAZARD, as analysed and pinned:** a caller-supplied `VarSpec.name` can be **any string** (the contract requires **no** CSS-legal custom-property spelling, and **the unit may NOT invent a name-legality rule — names are caller data**), so `'__proto__'`, `'constructor'`, `'prototype'`, `'toString'` and `'hasOwnProperty'` are **reachable as keys**. On a plain-object literal, **`applied['__proto__'] = 'x'` SILENTLY CHANGES THE RECORD'S PROTOTYPE instead of creating an own property** — the key **vanishes from `Object.keys`**, **`I-1`'s partition BREAKS** (the entry is in neither `applied` nor `skipped`), **the write order silently loses a key**, and **the projection stays "ok" while being wrong** — the false-green class inside a totality contract. **The same hazard sits in the duplicate-detection and `values`-lookup paths**: a plain-object lookup table read as `seen['constructor']` hands back **`Object.prototype.constructor`** (a truthy value never written ⇒ a non-duplicate misdiagnosed as `duplicate-name`), and a plain-object `values` returns an **inherited value** for such a key (⇒ a spec naming `'constructor'` could be **applied with a string the caller never supplied** instead of skipped `missing-value`). **THE RULING (adopted as recommended): build `applied` — and any internal key→value map — on `Object.create(null)`, and read EVERY key of caller data as an OWN property.** Four reasons, kept as the ruling's own: **(i) it costs nothing**; **(ii) it is TOTAL** — it covers every dangerous key **including ones nobody enumerated**, which is the unit's totality acceptance line applied to the record itself (a denylist is a partial answer to a total requirement); **(iii) it needs NO new skip reason and NO name-legality policy**, so **the caller's authority over names is intact**; **(iv) it is verifiable by ONE row.** **THE ALTERNATIVES REJECTED:** the plain object literal (**wrong today**); `Object.defineProperty` per key (**works, but per-key and it does not fix the duplicate-detection lookup or the `values` inherited read**); **reject/rename/skip a "dangerous" name** (**a name-legality policy this unit may not have**, partial, and it loses a legitimate caller name); a **`Map`** (**changes the return shape** — a different contract needing its own gate) or fixing **only** the returned record (**leaves the internal maps polluted**). **THE CONSEQUENCES, RECORDED HONESTLY:** the returned record has **NO prototype** ⇒ **`applied.hasOwnProperty(k)` DOES NOT EXIST** (use `Object.hasOwn`/`…hasOwnProperty.call`), `instanceof` is unaffected in substance, and **a `toEqual`-style helper that compares prototypes is affected**; **`Object.keys`, spread, `in`, `JSON.stringify`, `structuredClone`, `Object.entries`/`fromEntries` and `Object.assign` all behave** normally, and **`Object.keys(applied)` still yields the pinned deterministic WRITE ORDER**; **a naive copy DESTROYS the null prototype** — `{...applied}` and `Object.assign({}, applied)` produce a plain-prototype object and re-invoke the `__proto__` setter on the target, so **a copying consumer MUST use a null-prototype target** (a consumer obligation, not a module defect); **`readonly` is a TYPE-LEVEL promise that freezes NOTHING at runtime** — `Object.freeze` is permitted (it changes no row) but is **not a contract clause**, and **what IS pinned is directional**: the applier never mutates the projection and no returned record aliases module state; **THE APPLIER NEEDS NO GUARD AND THE TWO HALVES DIFFER** — it calls `CSSStyleDeclaration.setProperty` with a CSS property **NAME**, where `'__proto__'` is an **ordinary string** (a DOM API argument, **no object-key assignment, no prototype-write hazard**), so the record-key hazard (`create(null)`) and the property-name position (**nothing to guard**) are **different objects**; and **`skipped` is an ARRAY of `{name, reason}` records, so NO key-map hazard exists there** — the null-prototype rule does **not** extend to it, and a pass that "completes" the ruling by rewriting `skipped` is changing a part of the shape `A-3` never implicated. **WHAT IT PINS:** **`I-12`** (every caller-supplied name is an OWN key), **`I-13`** (no prototype; own-property lookups; write order intact), the new **§2.5** (the rules) and §0A ruling note 3 (the analysis), prohibition 3's **no-name-legality-policy** clause, and the rows **`F-13`** (the hazard, falsifiably, including the `'constructor'`-misdiagnosis and inherited-value halves), **`M-18`** (`'__proto__'` produces an own key, never a mutated prototype, `I-1`'s partition intact), **`M-19`** (own membership without `hasOwnProperty`) and **`M-20`** (the applier-side contrast). **No unit status changes.** | architect ruling `A-3` (2026-09-27); `docs/specs/projection.md` **§0A ruling note 3**, **§2.5**, §2.1, §2.2 prohibition 3, §3.1 `M-18`/`M-19`/`M-20`, §3.2 `F-13`, §3.3 `I-12`/`I-13` |
| **DECIDED: PROJECTION-IMMUTABLE-INPUT-NO-REENTRANCY-GUARD — the projection is IMMUTABLE INPUT to the applier and sink RE-ENTRANCY is NOT GUARDED; a consumer wanting different writes builds a NEW projection (architect ruling `A-7`, 2026-09-27)** | 2026-09-27 | **THE RULING, verbatim in substance:** *"skip, treat projection as non-mutable and expect consumers to create new projection if they want changes."* **WHAT IT PINS:** the applier **never mutates** the projection, **never caches into** it and **never re-enters** it to change it; **a consumer wanting different writes must build a NEW projection** (`project(values', specOf')`) — editing one in place is **not a supported operation**, which is also why `A-11`'s reuse is safe. **RE-ENTRANCY, stated as far as the invariants prove it:** if the injected sink's `setProperty` **re-enters `applyProjection`** (same or another projection), behaviour is **CALL-LOCAL** per `I-3`/`I-5`/`I-8`/`I-11` — each call keeps **its own** write log, `ok`/`skipped` and write count, a re-entrant call **cannot add a key to, or remove a key from, the outer `ApplyResult`**, and the outer call's **iteration set is fixed when it reads `projection.applied`**, so the remaining keys are still written after the nested call returns. **OBSERVABLE INTERLEAVING *IS* POSSIBLE AT THE SINK — and that is the sink's own choice:** the applier is **blind to the sink's prior state** (`I-6`) and **owns no lock over an object it does not own**. **IS THERE AN ORDERING HAZARD THE INVARIANTS DO NOT COVER? NO — recorded as `CONFIRMED-RULED`, not as an owed guard.** The one candidate (double-writing a key on a shared sink) is the **sink's own ordering choice**, visible in the **sink's own log**, and **neither `ApplyResult` over-claims**: each reports its own write. **NO GUARD IS HELD, and that is the ruling rather than an omission — the module holds no module-level and no instance-level state at all**, so **a guard would have to be STATE**, which is exactly what prohibition 4 forbids; **adding one is a NEW CONTRACT needing its own gate** (and it would refuse a nested call for a reason that has **no `ProjectionSkipReason` member**). **ESCALATION PATH, recorded so it is not lost:** **if a later pass ever finds a re-entrancy case that makes an `ApplyResult` report something its own call did not do, that is a `BLOCKING` finding against `I-3` and belongs in the disposition table as such** — the seed is discharged as ruled here, and the post-green adversarial pass **re-checks it against the landed module**. **WHAT IT PINS:** **`I-14`**, §2.3 **item 8** (the new write-or-skip rule), and the rows **`F-14`** (a re-entering sink ⇒ both results stay call-local, no throw, counts intact) and **`F-15`** (the caller's projection is **observably unchanged** after a call, and a **frozen** projection is accepted). **No unit status changes.** | architect ruling `A-7` (2026-09-27); `docs/specs/projection.md` **§0A ruling note 4**, §2.3 item 8, §3.2 `F-14`/`F-15`, §3.3 `I-14`, §3b |

| **DECIDED: GUTTER-WARRANT-IS-THE-RULING-PLUS-ITS-CLAUSE-ROWS — architect ruling `A-d4` OVERRIDES (it does not answer) the `ARITHMETIC-OVER-INJECTED-VALUES` admission objection to `SCH-6`, while the `SECOND-GESTURE-AUTHORITY` objection IS answered in the contract by composition; and the gate-1 escalations A/B/C are ADOPTED AS RECOMMENDED, with ONE-PASS reversibility for A and B** | 2026-09-27 | **PART 1 — WHAT `U-GUTTER`'s (`E3`) WARRANT IS, stated once so no later pass re-derives it.** **(a) `A-d4` OVERRIDES the `ARITHMETIC-OVER-INJECTED-VALUES` admission objection rather than answering it** — the ruling does not argue the mechanism-vs-UI-element classification (`SHELL-CHROME-CARVE-OUT-FUNCTIONAL`); it ADOPTS the unit by architect authority, so that objection is **overridden, not satisfied**. **(b) The `SECOND-GESTURE-AUTHORITY` objection IS answered in the contract, BY COMPOSITION** — the controller is DRIVEN BY the adopted node-local session (`U-GSESSION`), composes it, and re-expresses NONE of its lifecycle, so it creates no second authority over the gesture lifecycle (`docs/specs/gsession.md` `§2.6` item 6; `docs/pending.md` §A's `SCH-6` row's adoption clause). **(c) THEREFORE `U-GUTTER`'s WARRANT IS THE ARCHITECT'S RULING PLUS ITS CLAUSE ROWS** — the twelve governing rulings of the gate-1 record, the typed register and the red/green legs are the whole of the unit's justification; **no pass may present the unit as having ANSWERED the arithmetic objection.** **(d) `docs/pending.md` §A's `SCH-6` cell's claim that *"Both of this row's objections are CARRIED AS CONTRACT ROWS"* is TRUE ONLY OF THE SECOND** — annotated in the same pass as this row, its as-written clause kept visible. **(e) The `U-CENSUS` blocking edge on `E3` is DISSOLVED** — no import (`docs/specs/census.md` `§1` item 7 forbids it in its own words; the `sizes` VALUE arrives as an argument to the CONSUMER's injected callbacks) — so the unit's only dependency is the landed `U-GSESSION`. **PART 2 — THE GATE-1 ESCALATION ADOPTION AND ITS REVERSIBILITY (architect, 2026-09-27, recorded at `docs/specs/gutter-review.md` §5).** **A, B and C are ADOPTED AS RECOMMENDED:** **A — the axis seam is funded as an OPAQUE TOKEN** (`axisFor: (element) => unknown`, passed to the other seams and never interpreted; absent/non-callable/throwing ⇒ `undefined`); **B — `isResizable` is evaluated EXACTLY ONCE PER GESTURE, at establishment** (truthiness; absent or throwing ⇒ not resizable; `false` ⇒ the gesture establishes and terminates normally with ZERO sink writes, never an install-time gate); **C — the REAL gutter UI is a NAMED FUTURE UNIT OUTSIDE `E3`** (a provident-rendered drag affordance; ledger row `E10`, added in the same pass), **with the `E3` queue cell's `ui` phrase corrected from an apparent OFFER to the three-part REFUSAL it actually is**. **THE REVERSIBILITY CLAUSE, binding:** **the architect remains FREE TO OVERTURN A and B in ONE PASS — and that is cheap because the token is OPAQUE (no axis vocabulary enters the module) and the evaluation point is ONE CLAUSE WITH ONE ROW**; overturning either therefore re-opens one clause and its row, not the composition, the register's families or the leg set. | **Source:** architect ruling `A-d4` (2026-09-27) and the FOUR-STEP GATE-1 REVIEW of `U-GUTTER` whose record is `docs/specs/gutter-review.md` (§2 step-1 validity, §3 step-2 critique, §4 the twelve rulings, §5 the A/B/C adoption sentence and conditions `C1`–`C5`); | **DECIDED: GUTTER-E3-REMAINS-THE-POLICY-FREE-CLAMP-COMMIT-LAYER — THE UI UNIT (`E10` `U-GUTTER-UI`) OWNS THE COORDINATES, THE PREVIEW CHANNEL, THE CAPTURE DECISION AND THE DROP-REVERT** | 2026-09-27 | **THE RULING (the architect's intent statement + scope ruling (A), 2026-09-27), recorded here as decisions and NOT as spec text — the contract it derives into is `docs/specs/gutter.md` (the SpecWriter's amendment pass, IN FLIGHT AND UNCOMMITTED on this tree at this pass — its `§0A` note 13, the `§2.3` item 3 preview rule, the release-mapping block and the `onMove` handle correction are already on disc) and the per-block dispositions are `docs/pending.md` §I-bis.** **WHAT `E3` REMAINS:** `U-GUTTER` (`E3`) is **the policy-free CLAMP + COMMIT-DISCIPLINE layer** — it reads **NO coordinate and NO event field**, performs **no drag arithmetic**, and ships **no UI**: *"the unit ships NO drag arithmetic, no coordinate read and no magnitude — a NAMED, RECORDED COST"* (`docs/specs/gutter.md` ruling 2, `§1` item 2, `§2.2` `P-8`, `§3.4 R-8`, `I-11`). **WHAT THE UI UNIT OWNS:** ledger row **`E10`** (`U-GUTTER-UI`, `PROPOSED — awaiting the architect's admission`; `docs/next-steps.md`'s `E10` row, now carrying the interaction-model seed) owns **the cursor, the coordinates, the live preview channel, the capture decision and the drop-revert** — and is **the ONLY place a COORDINATE SOURCE or a `[U]` row for the panes/zones family can legitimately live** (`E3`'s `[U]` status is a THREE-PART REFUSAL, not an offer). **FOUR DECISIONS PINNED, one line each.** **(1) THE VALUE IS CONSUMER-PRODUCED.** The UI — the only party that sees the pointer — computes the size and pushes it with **`gesture.set(value)`**; the injected **`sizeFor(element, gesture, axis)`** reads **that value once, at the terminal**, and the commit is `clampToBounds(sizeFor(...), boundsFor(...))`. `E3` neither reads a coordinate nor computes a delta/magnitude; a consumer-held `sizes` value enters **only as an argument to the consumer's own callbacks** — a NAMED PRECONDITION, never a channel of this unit (`docs/specs/gutter.md` ruling 2, `§2.3` item 1, `§3.1 M-3`/`M-5`/`M-6`, `I-11`). **(2) THE PREVIEW CHANNEL IS A TRANSIENT VIEW CHANNEL AND IS NEVER THE SINK.** The live *"resized state based on the current cursor position"* is rendered by the **CONSUMER's** channel (the rendered graph / the provident update); the **sink is the session-invoked `commit` seam and is written AT MOST ONCE PER GESTURE, AND ONLY AT A TERMINAL** (`docs/specs/gutter.md` `§2.3` items 1/3, `§2.6` item 4; `docs/specs/gsession.md` `§0A`'s *"the session invokes `commit`"* approval) — **that separation is exactly what keeps *"cancel ⇒ ZERO sink writes"* meaningful while live feedback stays visible.** **(3) THE RELEASE MAPPING, with `gesture.outcome` as the discriminator the consumer reads.** A **VALID** dragged state ⇒ the session's **`end`** terminal ⇒ **exactly ONE commit of the CLAMPED DRAGGED VALUE**; an **INVALID** one ⇒ the **`reset`** terminal ⇒ **exactly ONE commit of the CLAMPED SUPPLIED DEFAULT** — the **pre-drag size the CONSUMER holds**, arriving through the composition's own injected **`defaultSizeFor(element, axis)`**, because **the session holds NO cross-gesture state** (so the intent's *"reset to the pre-drag size"* is the composition's `defaultSizeFor` seam in the spec's own vocabulary — **no new seam is created by this ruling**); **RIGHT-CLICK / DROP ⇒ the `cancel` terminal ⇒ ZERO commits and ZERO sink writes**, with the visible revert carried by the **consumer's preview channel** (decision 2) — *"drop and reset"* is therefore a **consumer-channelled revert plus a session `cancel`**, never a mechanism write, **with `controller.reset(element)` the composition's RESET ENTRY POINT (and `reset` its terminal) whenever the consumer's own path chooses to reset** — the two are distinct paths and neither invents a channel. (`docs/specs/gutter.md` ruling 4, `§2.3` items 1/4, `§2.5` item 5, `§3.1 M-8`/`M-14`..`M-17`, `§3.2 F-12`..`F-15`; `docs/specs/gsession.md` `§2.3` items 2/4/8.) **(4) CAPTURE OWNERSHIP.** The composition **STILL DOES NOT OPT IN**: `capture` is **ABSENT** from `ResizeControllerOptions` and from the options the module passes to `session.install`, so *"no capture before establishment"* is **INHERITED AND OBSERVED** (with `R-10`'s positive control — a composition that passed `capture: true` must FAIL it). **Any capture a real thin-gutter drag needs is `E10`'s decision and `E10`'s install path**, and the session's PARKED release-after-failed-establishment note keeps its **existing trigger** (`docs/pending.md` §I). (`docs/specs/gutter.md` ruling 11, `§2.1` items 3/5, `§2.2` `P-7`, `§3.4 R-10`.) **THE REJECTED OPTION (B) AND WHY:** making **`E3` the drag engine** was offered and **REJECTED** — it would make `E3` read the pointer coordinate and the event fields that the **FROZEN** session contract owns, i.e. **it would RE-OPEN the frozen session's coordinate contract**: a **session gate**, a **`[U]` row** on a unit whose legs refuse one, and the **twelve gate-1 rulings** (`docs/specs/gutter-review.md`) would all come back into play. **Option (A) preserves the frozen `docs/specs/gsession.md` `§2.5` delegate surface unchanged.** **THE COST, named and accepted:** the panes/zones family has **NO working gutter until `E10` is admitted and lands**, and **no agent-drivable drag may be claimed from `E3`'s green**. **THIS ROW FLIPS NO STATUS:** `E3` stays an OPEN `## OPEN` row (its red set has run; its green is a REPAIR cycle on the six `E3`-BLOCK items), and `E10` stays a PROPOSAL. | the architect's intent statement + scope ruling (A) (2026-09-27), derived in `docs/specs/gutter.md`; `docs/next-steps.md`'s `E3` and `E10` rows; `docs/pending.md` §I-bis (`E3`-BLOCK-1..6 dispositions); `docs/specs/gutter-review.md` (the twelve gate-1 rulings); `docs/specs/gsession.md` `§2.5` + `§0A` (the frozen delegate surface and the session-invokes-`commit` approval) |

17. **(⟶ ADDED 2026-09-27 — THE `U-PROJ` RULING-PACK PASS: the architect ruled the FOUR contract inputs the projection spec had left UNRULED (`A-11`, `A-2`/`F-4`, `A-3`, `A-7`), and this note records the pass in the file's own convention; **a contract-ruling landing on the PROJECTION contract only — no other clause of this file or of any other unit's spec was rewritten, nothing was renumbered, no implementation and no test was touched, and `U-PROJ`'s status is NOT advanced by it**. **PLACEMENT: appended at the head-of-tail position of this list, ahead of note 13, for the insertion-hazard reason notes 13/14/15/16 state (`RCA-8(c)` forbids a whole-file write here, and the four rows above were added by anchored `edit`s with the pre-existing bytes verified present); read `17` as the LATEST note** — the list therefore reads `… 9 → 15 → 16 → 17 → 13 → 14 → 12`, and the numbering is left honest rather than renumbered so a citation of any number still resolves.)** **THE FOUR RULINGS, one line each:** **(1) `A-11` — a `Projection` is REUSABLE** (a value, not a session; no consumption, no per-projection state; every call equivalent to a first call with that value) ⇒ `I-11` + `M-21`/`M-22`. **(2) `A-2`/`F-4` — a THROWING VALUE ACCESSOR is SKIPPED and RECORDED, never propagated** (caught **per key**; **one bad key never aborts a projection**, symmetric with the already-pinned *"a throwing `setProperty` does not abort the run"*) ⇒ a **NEW EIGHTH `ProjectionSkipReason` member `'accessor-threw'`**, the **`F-4` split** into `F-4A`/`F-4B`, the new row `F-12`, and the **vocabulary recount 7 → 8** everywhere it is stated (§2.1, §2.2 prohibition 1, §2.4 item 3's precedence, §3.2, §7 item 8, §8); the choice is a **new member rather than reusing `not-a-number`**, because reusing it would **silently conflate** *"not a number"* with *"we could not read it"*. **(3) `A-3` — the prototype-pollution-shaped key ⇒ `Object.create(null)`** for `Projection.applied`, `ApplyResult.applied` and **every internal key→value map**, with **own-property reads** of all caller data (the analysis, the four reasons and the **four rejected alternatives** are the spec's §0A note 3; the rules are its §2.5) ⇒ `I-12`/`I-13` + `F-13`/`M-18`/`M-19`/`M-20`, with the consequences recorded honestly (no prototype ⇒ no `hasOwnProperty`; the copy hazard; `readonly` ≠ frozen; the applier's contrasting **no-guard** position). **(4) `A-7` — sink re-entrancy: SKIP; the projection is IMMUTABLE INPUT and NO re-entrancy guard is held** (a consumer wanting different writes builds a **NEW** projection; a re-entrant `setProperty` yields **call-local** results; **no ordering hazard the invariants do not already cover was found, so nothing is owed**, with an explicit `BLOCKING` escalation path if that ever changes) ⇒ `I-14` + §2.3 item 8 + `F-14`/`F-15`. **THE COUNT RECONCILIATION THIS PASS OWED, stated because it is easy to get wrong:** the projection spec's §7 item 8 went from **FOUR unruled seeds to ZERO** — **NO contract-input seed of that spec remains unruled** — while its §7 item 7 stays at **TEN recorded contract decisions** (the ruling pack did **not** add an eleventh: a decision item there means *"the sources are silent and no ruling exists"*, and that is no longer true of these four). **What remains OWED is not a ruling but the ADVERSARIAL PASS itself** (it runs after the unit's green, RCA-3, and must re-check the four against the **landed module** and fill the spec's §3b disposition table; the spec's §3a marks the four seeds RULED in place and §3b carries them as `CONFIRMED-RULED`). **FILES TOUCHED BY THIS PASS, named so it is auditable: exactly TWO** — `docs/specs/projection.md` (the status block, §0's table row 9, the new **§0A** with the four dated ruling notes, §1, §2.1, §2.2, §2.3 item 8, §2.4, the new **§2.5**, §3.1 `M-18`..`M-22`, §3.2 `F-4A`/`F-4B`/`F-12`..`F-15`, §3.3 `I-11`..`I-14`, §4.2, §4.4 `S-9`..`S-11`, §5.5, §6, §7 items 7/8, §8 and §3a/§3b) and **this file** (the four rows above + this note). **No `src/**`, no `tests/**`, no `scripts/**`, no other spec, and no other tracker was edited by this pass**, and **nothing was archived, moved or repointed**. **NO UNIT STATUS CHANGES:** `U-PROJ` remains **`BLOCKED`** on the architect's wave-D go-ahead and on its red set (`OWED — NOT AUTHORED, NOT RUN`), exactly as `PROJECTION-SIGNATURE-APPROVAL-SCOPE` above and the spec's §4.5 state; `U-CENSUS` remains **wave E and a separate unit**; and **no leg has run.**

| **DECIDED: SLOTHOST-CONTAINER-SOURCE-ADMITS-A-RUNTIME-MATERIALISED-CONTAINER — the `slot-host` container source is SATISFIED by ANY value offering a function-valued `appendChild`, so a runtime-materialised, framework-authored element is ADMITTED as a container: the accepted shape is NOT "a caller-created element", an envelope-authored consumer is NOT out of scope, and this repo adds NO option, NO variant and NO adapter** | 2026-10-04 | **THE GATE-1 DISPOSITION OF THE FORK-SOURCED ASK `SLOT-HOST-ENVELOPE-AUTHORED-CONTAINER-SOURCE` — ONE ACTIVE ROW FOR THE GATE RECORD `docs/specs/slot-host-envelope-authored-container-source-review.md` (step 4 `NOT-DELEGABLE` in the SUBSTANTIVE sense: a decline of the facility arm, a grant of the ask's own arms, and a doc-only coverage duty — no unit to delegate and no red set to author; step 3 clause 1 `GRANT-AS-DOCS-ONLY` / clause 2 `GRANT-AS-DOCS-ONLY` / clause 3 `REDIRECT`; step 2 `SOUND-WITH-CONDITIONS`; step 1 `WELL-FORMED-WITH-CORRECTIONS`, its returned word not in force in this repo). NO UNIT IS ADMITTED, no spec, no code, no test, no register and no leg is owed; this row is the disposition's tracker half, the disposition row is `docs/pending.md` `§N`, and the fork-facing block is `docs/FORKER.md` §4's `### THE HOST CONTAINER SOURCE — WHAT A FORK SUPPLIES, AND WHEN IT IS CALLED`.** **WHAT IT PINS, six clauses:** **(1) THE ACCEPTED CONTAINER SHAPE — "OFFERS `appendChild`", AND NOTHING ELSE.** `docs/specs/slothost.md` **§2.1's container-source clause item 3** states normatively that a container is *"a value that **offers `appendChild`** (**a function-valued `appendChild` member**)"* — *"A real DOM element satisfies it; a shim `ShimElement` satisfies it; a plain object offering an `appendChild` function satisfies it; **no predicate such as `instanceof` or a tag check is asserted or admissible**"* — and the module's own predicates are exactly that one test (`src/shared/slot-host.ts:167-171` `isNodeShaped`, `:199-203` `isUsable`, both **`typeof object['appendChild'] === 'function'`**), with `obtainContainer` (`:150-159`) accepting a factory return on `isUsable` alone. **A runtime-materialised, framework-authored element satisfies the predicate BY CONSTRUCTION.** The module header's phrase *"a container manager for CALLER-CREATED nodes"* (`:4`) describes **whose nodes the host places**, never what a container may be; it is a reading aid, not the normative clause. **(2) THE CONSUMER'S REAL CONSTRAINT IS TEMPORAL, NOT CATEGORICAL — THE ORDERING RULE.** `containerFactory` is invoked **only from `syncContainers()`** (`:305-322`), and only for a declared key whose record still has no container while the injected `container` is **present and usable**; its callers are the driven paths (`setNode` `:504`, `setOrder` via `syncOrder` `:582`, `render` `:612`). **No container is created at construction**, and a key never driven with a present, usable mount never gets one. The consumer's obligation is therefore: the value must offer `appendChild` **and must exist when the host is driven for that key**; if the consumer's own tree re-materialises, the consumer re-supplies — or it takes the **`F-6`-class no-op degradation**, which is a **supported configuration** producing **no** refusal (in particular **not `'no-container'`, declared-but-not-emitted**). **(3) IDENTITY AND DISTINCTNESS ARE ALREADY PINNED, SO NO PARALLEL BLOCK IS WRITTEN.** Observable **2** of `slothost.md`'s *"THE `containerFor` OBSERVABLES — THE ANTI-VACUITY CLAUSE"* pins that `containerFor(k)` returns the **same object** twice and that **two different declared keys' results are different objects (`!==`)** — re-pinned into `§3.1 M-1`/`M-18` and driven; the `null` half is pinned **by value** (`toBe(null)`, never `undefined`). **The ONLY genuine residues are UNPINNED and are carried as `OWED`, not discharged here:** the **call discipline** across drives (the clause says *"called once per declared key that needs a container"* while the implementation re-invokes on **each drive** whose key has no container) and **container survival across a `dispose()`/re-sync or a `null`→present parent transition**. **(4) `owned-list-host` SHARES THE LIMITATION AND DIVERGES STRUCTURALLY.** Its header and option docs say the same caller-created-node thing (`src/shared/owned-list-host.ts:4`, `:33-43`: `mount` is *"the container the host places CALLER-CREATED nodes inside"*; `itemFactory` is consulted *"ONLY when an entry supplies no node … the host NEVER creates a node itself"*), **and it has ONE `mount` and NO per-key container concept at all** — so a per-key-box consumer supplies its boxes as **nodes** on that host, not as containers. **(5) `SLOTHOST-CONTAINER-SOURCE-IS-INJECTED` IS NOT AMENDED, WEAKENED, SUPERSEDED OR RE-READ BY THIS ROW.** Injection and provenance are orthogonal: the container source remains the **injected** factory, the **ambient read stays DELETED**, and the tightened anti-assembly bans stay in force (`ADV-SH-1`'s chain: `§2.2` prohibitions 1/2, `§1`'s out-of-scope list, `§4.4 S-2`/`S-4`, `§3.2 F-11`/`F-12` and the per-method table). **DECLINED and named so no later pass revives them silently:** any `containerSource`-shaped projection over an authored tree, any caller-supplied `key → element` map option, and any suite split of `tests/slot-host.test.ts` — the first two would make the module read a tree (the class the bans exclude) and would **mint vocabulary originating outside this project**, which is the one thing that would make an **adoption dossier** due. **(6) THE FALSIFIER AND THE FORK-SIDE RETURN.** Falsifier: a **shipped in-tree `src/**` consumer** (a call site that actually runs) whose containers **cannot** be caller-supplied and **cannot** exist before the host is driven — i.e. one for which the host would have to read a tree or a graph; **none exists** (`createSlotHost` is imported by **no** `src/**` file, and every container in the landed harness is a caller value, `slothost.md` §5.2). Second falsifier: an architect ruling that the accepted shape is narrower than §2.1 item 3. **Return: this repo writes NO file under `<Astrographer>/`** — the fork's own row (`../Astrographer/docs/defects.md`, `docs/HANDOFF.md`) **stays OPEN and is annotated by the FORK's own pass**; and **no `docs/defects.md` row and no `docs/HANDOFF.md` round is owed** (that catalogue is the `provident-ssr` PACKAGE-gap list, and this ask is neither a package gap nor a host defect). **NO LEDGER MOVE:** the ledger stays `21 DONE / 0 open` — this disposition admits no unit and `RCA-8(f)` admits no successor row. **Measurer attribution (`RCA-12`): no leg, suite, build or battery was run by this disposition; every figure in the gate record is a file read or a step pass's own reading, attributed.** | the architect's gate-1 disposition of 2026-10-04, filed in `docs/specs/slot-host-envelope-authored-container-source-review.md` (§0 the headline correction, §2 the verification table, §3 the per-clause disposition, §5 the `OWED` rows, §6 the STEP-0 zero-row rationale, §8 the risk register, §9 the falsifier); the normative clause `docs/specs/slothost.md` §2.1's container-source clause items 1-5 (+ `§3.1 M-1`/`M-18`, `§3.3 I-4`, `§3.2 F-11`/`F-12`, the anti-vacuity observables clause, `§5.2`'s harness clause); `src/shared/slot-host.ts` (the predicates, `syncContainers`, `isOneOfOurContainers`); `src/shared/owned-list-host.ts` (its header, `mount`, `itemFactory`); the pre-existing ACTIVE row **`SLOTHOST-CONTAINER-SOURCE-IS-INJECTED`** (cited by row name, unamended); the fork's filing `../Astrographer/docs/feature-requests/slot-host-envelope-authored-container-source.md` and its own lockfile's `conformance.excluded` reasons (read-only); the disposition row `docs/pending.md` `§N`; the fork-facing block `docs/FORKER.md` §4 |

| **DECIDED: ZONE-SIZE-DOMAIN-IS-CONSUMER-CARRIED-AND-THE-MINIMUM-CLAMP-IS-FAMILY-SIDE — the architect's zone-size ruling: a zone's size domain is `{min … max} ∪ {minimized}`, an attempt below the minimum is ROUNDED TO THE MINIMUM OR SET TO ZERO, ZERO IS THE MINIMIZE VERB AND NEVER A SMALLER WIDTH, and a minimized zone RETAINS ITS LOCATION so proximity detection can expand it back to its configured size and host a pane; the family may supply the PURE MINIMUM CLAMP and RECEIVE the minimum and the slot key / the caller-measured distance, while the MEANING OF ZERO and the MEASURE OF THE LOCATION stay consumer-side** | 2026-10-01 | **THE ARCHITECT'S RULING, on the gate-1 disposition of the fork-sourced ask `ZONE-GESTURE-PREVIEW-AND-COLLAPSED-DROP-TARGET` (`docs/specs/zone-gesture-preview-and-collapsed-drop-target-review.md`; the disposition row is `docs/pending.md` `§O`; the fork-facing carriers are `docs/FORKER.md` §4's `### THE PREVIEW CHANNEL AND THE GESTURE'S LIFETIME — WHAT A FORK OWNS` and `docs/specs/zone-gesture-preview-and-collapsed-drop-target-feedback.md`). THIS IS A NEW FACT, which is why it is rowed rather than cited: every other limb of that disposition needed a ruling that is already landed, and the `§M`/`§N` precedent lands an ACTIVE row only where a disposition PINS A NEW FACT.** **WHAT IT PINS, five clauses:** **(1) THE RULED SIZE DOMAIN, AND WHAT A BELOW-MINIMUM ATTEMPT DOES.** Verbatim in substance: *"Invalidate and refuse attempts to set a zone to less than a minimum height/width. Attempting to set a zone to a smaller size either rounds the value to the minimum or zero, with zero minimizing the zone instead of changing the width. The minimized zone should still broadcast a location for the proximity detection to expand back to its configured size and host the pane."* So the readable domain is **`{min … max} ∪ {minimized}`**; **a below-minimum attempt is REFUSED as such** — it resolves to the **minimum** or to **zero**, never to a smaller-than-minimum extent. **(2) ZERO IS THE MINIMIZE VERB, AND NEVER A SMALLER WIDTH.** A `0` size is therefore **not in the width continuum** at all: it is the **state transition** to minimized, and **no pass may read it as a width between the minimum and some smaller value**. **(3) A MINIMIZED ZONE KEEPS ITS LOCATION.** Minimizing changes the **extent**, never the **position**: the member **retains a location**, that location is what **proximity detection** reads, and the ruled consequence is that proximity can **expand the zone back to its configured size and host a pane**. **So a minimized member is a drop target that is reachable by proximity, not a member that has been removed** — this is the affirmative half of the ruling, and it is the half a mechanism may not implement by deleting the member from its own enumeration. **(4) THE LAYER SPLIT THE RULING OBLIGES — THE ONE THING THE FAMILY MAY CARRY, AND THE TWO THINGS IT MAY NOT.** **The family MAY supply the PURE MINIMUM CLAMP** (the landed `clampToBounds` is that clamp; a **second** clamp site stays inadmissible) **and MAY RECEIVE the minimum and the slot key / the caller-measured distance** — i.e. a value in, a clamped value out, in the shape of the landed caller-measured comparator (`RelocateOptions`' candidates and `withinProximity`). **The family may NOT read a geometry** (`docs/specs/container.md` §3.4 `R-6`'s *"no ELEMENT parameter anywhere in the module's surface"*; `docs/specs/zones.md` §2.3 item 7, §3.3 `I-10`, §4.4 `S-6`; the mandatory geometry clause `S-d11`). **AND THE TWO CONSUMER-SIDE HALVES — the MEANING OF ZERO and the MEASURE OF THE LOCATION — stay the consumer's, because the module's own limb rules force it:** `src/shared/zones.ts` `trackFor` treats **`0` as a SIZE** (a finite non-negative number yields `String(size) + spec.unit`), so *"zero means minimized"* is a **consumer mapping expressed in the consumer's own `census` + `revealed` predicate**, never a mechanism inference; and the **distance** that proximity compares is **computed by the consumer** and arrives as a number. The derivation is worked through in `docs/specs/foundation-app-data-model.md` §6 (the lawful homes of the minimum value, the minimized state and the minimized member's slot/location). **(5) THE ADMISSION THE RULING CARRIES, AND ITS SEPARATION FROM THE COUNT.** A **NEW UNIT IS ADMITTED** to carry the **family-side half** — the pure minimum clamp over **RECEIVED** values, plus a **received** minimum and slot key — **and its own spec-gate chain, its own red set, its own register and its own ledger row are a LATER, SEPARATELY-GATED pass**: **THIS row opens that gate and moves nothing by itself; the ledger stays `21 DONE / 0 open` and `RCA-8(f)` admits no successor row until that unit's own record lands.** **THE SCOPE OF THE SUPERSESSION, stated so it is not over-read:** the ruling supersedes the **as-filed `REFUSE-WITH-REASON` of that record's `B(i)` limb FOR THE FAMILY-SIDE GEOMETRY READING ALONE** — a `reading` of a member's cell/slot box remains **forbidden by row**, and the record's `B(i)` row carries this annotation beside it, as filed, in its own §4. **NO PRE-EXISTING ROW IS AMENDED, WEAKENED, SUPERSEDED OR RE-READ BY THIS ROW:** `SHELL-CHROME-PANES-ZONES-IN-SCOPE`, `GUTTER-E3-REMAINS-THE-POLICY-FREE-CLAMP-COMMIT-LAYER`, `E5-B-2-EDGE-IS-AN-OPAQUE-CALLER-VALUE-UNDER-THE-NO-REACH-CLAUSE` and `PBT-REGISTER-REQUIRED-FOR-CODE-UNITS` are **cited BY ROW NAME and marked none of them `AMENDED`/`SUPERSEDED`**; **no clause of any spec is changed by this row** (the ruling's landing in a spec is that new unit's own spec-gate duty, not this row's); and **no count, no ledger cell and no `docs/next-steps.md` row moves.** | the architect's ruling of 2026-10-01, carried into the gate-1 record `docs/specs/zone-gesture-preview-and-collapsed-drop-target-review.md` and dispositioned at `docs/pending.md` `§O` (the `B(i)` row); the derivation of the lawful homes at `docs/specs/foundation-app-data-model.md` §6 (the minimum value, the minimized state, the minimized member's slot/location); the landed clamp and comparator `src/shared/gutter.ts` `clampToBounds` and `src/shared/relocate.ts` `withinProximity`; the forbidding set `docs/specs/container.md` §3.4 `R-6`, `docs/specs/zones.md` §2.3 item 7 / §3.3 `I-10` / §3.4 `R-7` / §4.4 `S-6`, `docs/specs/census.md` §2.5 / §3.3 `I-8` / §3.4 `R-6` / §4.4 `S-7`, and the mandatory clause `S-d11` in `docs/specs/provident-electron-shell-chrome-handoff-review.md`; the fork-side record of the ask at `docs/feature-requests/`-equivalents as cited by the four `archive/gate1/2026-10-01-ZONE-GESTURE-SEAMS-gate1-step{1,2,3,4}-*.md` reports |
