| **DECIDED: FOUR-TIER-DATA-OWNERSHIP-MODEL — the design record: four stores with their own get/set surfaces and four tiers (`file` / `mem` / `temp` / `secure`), ONE ascending-durability read that stops at the first hit, never invents a default and REFUSES every `secure.*` reference; commit-clears-lower (C-1..C-5) with the clears AFTER the higher tier durably accepted and NOTHING cleared on a refusal; ONE clear-then-notify event per affected reference carrying `cleared[]`; a DECLARED NAME REGISTRY as the store's second contract surface (undeclared / double-declared / wrong-tier / malformed / reserved names refused at write time); CONSTRAINTS as a first-class model element evaluated on every write with a stated outcome (the first is exactly-one-active, outcome REPAIR); `remove(name)` as a first-class PERSISTED operation (`set(name, undefined)` is NOT a deletion and NOT a third state); and the realm split AS DECIDED (tier 1 renderer-owned through a main-side channel, `mem`/`temp` renderer, `secure` main-only, NO MIRROR)** | 2026-10-01 | **THE DESIGN RECORD FOR THE FACILITY THE ROW ABOVE OPENS — the architect's answered decisions of 2026-10-01, folded into `docs/specs/data-ownership-model-plan.md`. THIS ROW PINS THE MODEL; THE PLAN'S SECTIONS CARRY THE DERIVATIONS AND ARE THE AUTHORITY FOR EVERY DETAIL.** **WHAT IT PINS, in nine clauses:** **(1) THE FOUR TIERS AND THEIR SCOPE.** `file` = the file-stored persistent cache (config, settings, file tracking) · `mem` = the in-memory persistent cache (per realm) · `temp` = the in-memory temp cache (per episode) · `secure` = the access-controlled, file-persisted security cache. **Each tier has its OWN get/set surface**; **a commit to a higher-persistence tier clears the same reference's DECLARED lower aliases and never a higher tier** (C-1..C-5; **the clear happens AFTER the higher tier durably accepted the value, and a refused/throwable commit clears NOTHING**). **(2) THE ONE-READ RULE.** **`read(name)` searches `temp` → `mem` → `file` in ASCENDING order of durability and returns the FIRST tier holding the reference, with THAT TIER'S OWN REFERENCE and the resolved NAME** (`{found, value, tier, cache, name}`); **a miss in all three answers `{found:false, value:undefined, tier:null, name}` — never a throw and NEVER AN INVENTED DEFAULT** (the family's *"no policy defaults"* rule, `zones.md` `§2.2` `P-3`). **THE `cache` MEMBER IS A LIVE OBJECT REFERENCE AND THEREFORE CANNOT CROSS AN IPC BOUNDARY** (`docs/specs/mcp-endpoint.md` `P-E6`) — which is why every non-security tier lives in ONE realm. **(3) THE SECURITY TIER IS OUTSIDE THE FALL-THROUGH BY CONSTRUCTION, AND ITS REFUSAL IS TYPED.** **`read`/`commit`/`subscribe`/`clear`/`remove` REFUSE every `secure.*` reference**; **the renderer NEVER reads tier 4** — it reads the operator pane's SNAPSHOT over the existing manual-UI channel plus `maxJournalLength` at boot, and **no token, no group set and no tier-4 value reaches a graph node, a tool result, a resource or a notification payload** (`docs/specs/mcp-endpoint.md` `§6.4`; layer 1 `§5` `A-7`). **(4) THE EVENT RULE — ONE EVENT, CLEAR-THEN-NOTIFY.** **A commit fires ONE event per affected reference, AFTER the clears, carrying `cleared[]`; a removal (`remove(name)`) fires the SAME one-event shape with no committed value.** The `cleared[]` array is **contract**, not decoration (there is no second clear-event). **(5) THE DECLARED NAME REGISTRY IS THE STORE'S SECOND CONTRACT SURFACE.** A reference is **declared once** with its tier segment and its declared lower aliases; **an undeclared name, a name declared twice, a name whose first segment disagrees with its declared tier, a malformed name, a name in a tier outside the four tokens and a `remove()` on a RESERVED name are each REFUSED** — and **the registry ships no consumer vocabulary** (`R-1`; the `P-CT-1`/`H-r15` mirror-class ban binds its bytes). **(6) CONSTRAINTS ARE FIRST-CLASS, AND THE FIRST ONE REPAIRS.** **A constraint is declared per reference, evaluated on EVERY write, with a STATED OUTCOME. The model's first is EXACTLY ONE TAB CARRIES `active=true` WHILE ANY TABS EXIST, and its declared outcome is `REPAIR` — not refusal — reported in the write's receipt (`repaired:[…]`).** **The store refuses NAMES, TIERS and CAP OVERFLOWS; it NEVER refuses a SIZE** (no store-side clamp exists, so `clampToBounds` stays the family's ONE clamp site, `S-PURE-4`). **(7) REMOVAL IS FIRST-CLASS AND PERSISTED.** **`remove(name)` deletes at the OWNING tier, PERSISTS the removal, notifies ONCE (with `cleared[]`) and clears the reference's declared lower aliases; `set(name, undefined)` is NOT a deletion and there is NO `removed` third state — only a found value or a miss.** **(8) THE REALM SPLIT AS DECIDED (the plan's `§2.5`).** **Tier 1 `file` IS RENDERER-OWNED and written through a MAIN-SIDE channel; `mem` and `temp` live in the renderer; `secure` stays MAIN-ONLY; and THERE IS NO READ-THROUGH MIRROR** — the values are the renderer's own, and `main` owns the FILE, the ATOMIC WRITE (`${path}.tmp` + `renameSync` + `fsync`), the corruption-at-read-back fallback and the RECEIPT (never a swallow). **The crossings are `Y-1` (boot hand-off) / `Y-2` (commit, one per write, one IPC round trip) / `Y-3` (change push, a declared no-op on today's single-window app), and the BOOT ORDER changes because the renderer owns the values (no graph load before the hand-off).** **`provident-security.json`'s NON-ATOMIC `writeFileSync` is NAMED AS THE PATTERN NOT TO COPY.** **(9) WHAT IT DOES *NOT* TOUCH, STATED SO THE ROW IS NOT OVER-READ.** **No MCP surface** — no new tool, resource, group, `VALID_GROUPS` member, `RpcMethod` member, `MUTATING_METHODS` entry or notification (**the plan's `§5.5` `PAR-1`..`5`**) · **no package edit** (`P-E1`) · **no frozen-contract change** — **`docs/specs/gsession.md` `§2.5` KEEPS ITS ELEVEN ITEMS BYTE-FOR-BYTE, because the answer to *"is a session-level change channel wanted?"* is NO and the paradigm lands at the composition boundary and on the store's own `subscribe`** · **no graph authority moved** (the graph stays canon: `P-E2`; a store value is MCP-visible only when a graph node carries it) · **no unit admitted and no count moved.** **AND THE TWO CONTRACTS THIS MODEL OWES AN AMENDMENT TO ARE ROUTED, NOT REWRITTEN HERE: `docs/specs/mcp-endpoint.md` §3.8 and `docs/specs/focus-tool.md`'s refusal rows (the tabs slice makes `unknown-id` unreachable from the focus verb — an unknown or unrenderable target opens an ERROR TAB), and `docs/specs/gutter-ui.md` §2.3 row 16 (the model TAKES `RH-2`, whose fix supersedes that row's *"the wiring does not re-attach"*). EACH OWES ITS OWN GATE, and this pass edits none of them.** **Two further rows are ROUTED as owed: the fork-facing re-write (`Q-14`, one documentation pass after `U-STORE-MODULES`) and the `§5.9` unread-row sweep.** | the architect's answered decisions of 2026-10-01 on `docs/specs/data-ownership-model-plan.md` — its `§0`'s `A-1`…`A-14` block (the fourteen answers), `§1.1`–`§1.8` (the model, the registry, the tabs slice), `§2.5` (the revised realm split and the `Y-*` crossings), `§3.9` (the tenants, the `Q-5` migration and the recount), `§4.3` (the tabs walk), `§5.1`/`§5.2.6`/`§5.6.5`/`§5.9.1` (the facility, the fifteen edges, the re-vendor list, the re-classification), `§6.5` (the amended unit split), `§7.1`/`§7.3` (the hazard tally and the `NW-*` rows), `§8.7` and `§9.1` (the costs and the decided ledger); the cited landed rows `docs/specs/mcp-endpoint.md` `P-E1`/`P-E2`/`P-E6`/`§6.4`, `docs/specs/zones.md` `§2.2` `P-3`, `docs/specs/container.md` `§2.2` `P-CT-1`, `docs/specs/gutter.md` `§4.4` `S-PURE-4`, `docs/specs/gsession.md` `§2.5`, `docs/specs/mcp-endpoint.md` `§3.8`; the plan's predecessor row `NO-FOUNDATION-CONFIG-FILE-FACILITY` (superseded) and this file's own `GSESSION-DELEGATE-SURFACE-IS-FROZEN-FOR-E3-E4` / `E10-SINGLE-SINK-CHANNEL` / `PBT-REGISTER-REQUIRED-FOR-CODE-UNITS` rows (cited by name, unamended) |

**⟶ DATED POINTER BESIDE THE MODEL ROW ABOVE (2026-10-01, THE ROUND-2 AMENDMENT PASS — a pointer, NOT a rewrite: the model row's own bytes are unchanged, `RCA-8(d)`).** **The row above (`FOUR-TIER-DATA-OWNERSHIP-MODEL`) is AMENDED IN SUBSTANCE BY THE ARCHITECT'S ROUND-2 ANSWERS, folded into `docs/specs/data-ownership-model-plan.md`: `§1.2`/`§1.4`/`§1.9` (v) (the qualified/unqualified read rule and the logical-path clear, superseding the `lowerAliases` shape), `§1.9` (i)–(iii) (the residency trie, the merged read with `parts`, the path-scoped ping), `§1.8` (the pattern table, the read-side refusals `H-1`…`H-4`, `G-9`/`G-10`), `§1.5` (the one event envelope), `§1.9` (iv) (the constraint table with `REPAIR`, evaluated on every write and on `remove`), `§5.2.7` (the purity criterion and the `8 STORE-BACKED / 7 PURE` verdict set, superseding `§5.2.6`'s fifteen-edge reading), `§2.5`/`§3.6` `A-1` (`file.window.bounds`), `§6.5` (the unit split, extended, and the deferred `U-STORE-TABS-STRIP` PROPOSAL), `§7.3` (`NW-10`…`NW-17`), and `§5.10` (both gate-1 review reports, finding by finding).** **The new ACTIVE row below is the round-2 record; the model row above is read WITH it, and NOTHING in the row above is marked `SUPERSEDED` by this pointer.**

| **DECIDED: QUALIFIED-READ-MERGED-READ-SUBTREE-EVENTS-AND-THE-PURITY-CRITERION — the ROUND-2 data-ownership semantics: a tier segment is an EXPLICIT QUALIFIER only (an unqualified read searches temp → mem → file and returns the first hit, a qualified read addresses ONE tier, and there are NO per-tier alias declarations — the two-name reference problem is DISSOLVED, not patched); a read of a path NO tier holds but whose DESCENDANTS are resident returns a MERGED value (file as base, then mem, then temp overlaid — DESCENDING durability, the reverse of the search order) whose return shape gains a `parts` list of `{tier, path}` pairs and carries `merged:true`/`tier:null`/`cache:null`; each tier maintains an in-memory RESIDENT-PATH PREFIX TRIE inside its own `set`/`commit`/`remove` (no extra write, no extra ping); a write PINGS EXACTLY ITS OWN PATH and an ancestor fires only for a subscriber that opted in with `{subtree: true}`, whose event carries `cause:'descendant'`, `origin:<written path>` and NO value; CONSTRAINTS are a store-side TABLE (kind · repair action · refusal reason) evaluated on EVERY WRITE **AND ON `remove`**, a violation REPAIRED in the same committed write, a repair EMITTING ITS OWN EVENT (`cause:'repair'`), and ZERO-ACTIVE a REPAIR and never a refusal; there is ONE EVENT ENVELOPE PER REFERENCE — `{name, tier, value, cleared[], cause}` with `cause ∈ {set, commit, clear, sweep, remove, repair, descendant}` — with the required member set stated PER ARM (an N-entry sweep is N events; a refused commit fires NOTHING; `origin` is required on the `descendant` arm alone); the READ SIDE has its own rule (an UNDECLARED name is a TYPED REFUSAL, a declared-but-never-written name is a MISS `{found:false}`, a declared `secure.*` name is REFUSED, and the `secure` check PRECEDES the registry check so no caller learns the security tier's schema); `file.window.bounds` is OWNED BY THE RENDERER'S TABLE and APPLIED BY `main` (read through the channel at construction, applied on change); the TAB STRIP is DEFERRED to its own proposal; and the FIFTEEN SHARED MODULES ARE DECIDED BY A PURITY CRITERION — *"pure functions that only use internal data can stay pure; external I/O outside of calling params and return values must go through data stores"* — giving `8 STORE-BACKED / 7 PURE`, with the five kernels keeping their landed purity rows and owing NO store edge** | 2026-10-01 | **THE ARCHITECT'S ROUND-2 ANSWERS (2026-10-01), taken on the filed plan `docs/specs/data-ownership-model-plan.md` after the two gate-1 review reports (`archive/gate1/2026-10-01-DATA-OWNERSHIP-MODEL-gate1-step1-validity.md`, verdict `BLOCKED-ON-SEMANTICS` beside `NOT-DELEGABLE`; `archive/gate1/2026-10-01-DATA-OWNERSHIP-MODEL-gate1-step2-critique.md`, `BLOCKED-ON-SEMANTICS`) forced three items and the plan's `§5.10` dispositions every finding of both. THIS IS A NEW FACT, which is why it is rowed rather than cited: it PINS semantics the two rows the last amendment landed (`FOUNDATION-STORE-FACILITY-IS-OPENED-AND-SUPERSEDES-NO-FOUNDATION-CONFIG-FILE-FACILITY` and `FOUR-TIER-DATA-OWNERSHIP-MODEL`, both cited BY ROW NAME and neither rewritten) did NOT decide — the read's resolution, the subtree mechanism, the registry's declaration unit, the constraint table's evaluation points, the event envelope, the read-side refusals, the window-bounds authority, the strip's deferral, and the purity criterion.** **WHAT IT PINS, in eight clauses, EACH CARRIED BY THE PLAN'S OWN SECTION (cited BY SECTION NAME, never by line).** **(1) THE READ RULE — `docs/specs/data-ownership-model-plan.md` `§1.2`'s round-2 block and `§1.9` (v):** **a tier segment is an EXPLICIT QUALIFIER only; an unqualified name is searched in ASCENDING durability order and answers the FIRST hit; a qualified name addresses ONE tier and misses outside it; the `lowerAliases` declaration shape is SUPERSEDED BESIDE (`§1.4` `C-4`, `§1.8`'s row shape, `G-8`) and NO per-tier alias declarations exist; a commit clears the SAME LOGICAL PATH in every lower tier (`C-4-R`) and nothing else; a `remove` on a path resident in more than one tier is REFUSED unless the caller names the tier (`C-4-R2`, `reason:'ambiguous-path'` with the resident tiers).** **(2) THE SUBTREE MECHANISM — `§1.9` (i)–(iii) and the worked walk `§4.4`:** **the per-tier residency trie (in-memory, maintained inside the tier's own mutators, no extra write, no extra ping, with the totality row); the MERGED READ (file base → mem → temp overlay, i.e. DESCENDING durability — explicitly the REVERSE of the search order — with `parts` a list of `{tier, path}` PAIRS naming paths and never values, and `merged:true`/`tier:null`/`cache:null` on that arm only); and the PATH-SCOPED PING (a write pings exactly its own path; an ancestor fires ONLY for a `{subtree: true}` subscriber, carrying `cause:'descendant'`, `origin:<written path>` and NO value, forcing a re-read).** **(3) THE REGISTRY — `§1.8`:** **TWO DECLARATION KINDS (`concrete` and `pattern` matched BY SHAPE, with a named wildcard slot accepting exactly one non-empty segment), the MATCHER'S PRECEDENCE (concrete beats pattern; then the more literal shape wins; an AMBIGUOUS table is REFUSED at load — `G-9`, with `G-10` refusing a malformed pattern), the concrete-beside-pattern rule (a pinned INSTANCE, so the reserved `file.tabs.landing` sits beside the tab patterns), the pattern/`secure.*` interaction (the first segment stays structural and `G-5` is unchanged), and the declared instance bound (`maxInstances: null` today — a DECLARED position, `§9.1` `OPEN-1`).** **(4) THE READ SIDE — `§1.8` `H-1`…`H-4`:** **an UNDECLARED name on `read`/`subscribe` is a TYPED REFUSAL and never a silent miss; a DECLARED-BUT-NEVER-WRITTEN name is a MISS `{found:false}` (the as-filed miss shape, never an invented default); a DECLARED `secure.*` name is REFUSED; and the ORDER IS DECLARED AND FIXED — `secure` refusal, then registry match, then miss — with the stated reason (a registry-first order would leak the security tier's SCHEMA to any caller that can spell a name, the widening `docs/specs/mcp-endpoint.md` §6.4 forbids).** **(5) THE CONSTRAINT TABLE — `§1.9` (iv), with `§1.7` items 3/4/5 annotated:** **constraints are DECLARED PER REFERENCE as an id into a table whose columns are **kind · repair action · refusal reason**; the table is EVALUATED ON EVERY WRITE **AND ON EVERY `remove`**; a violation is REPAIRED IN THE SAME COMMITTED WRITE and a repair EMITS ITS OWN EVENT (`cause:'repair'`, so an unreported repair is a silent second write); ZERO-ACTIVE IS A REPAIR, NEVER A REFUSAL, and the entry activated is the one the CALLER'S OWN `file.tabs.order` names (never a store-side choice); the landing reference's activation is that REPAIR, not a caller `set`; and the table's `refusal reason` is `null` where a row cannot refuse (the store refuses NAMES, TIERS and CAP OVERFLOWS — never a SIZE).** **(6) THE EVENT ENVELOPE — `§1.5`:** **ONE envelope per reference, `{name, tier, value, cleared[], cause}`, where `name` is the caller's own spelling, `tier` is the tier that FIRED, `value` is present as a KEY on every arm (undefined where the arm carries none), `cleared[]` is present and EMPTY rather than omitted, and `cause` is one of exactly SEVEN tokens; the REQUIRED MEMBER SET PER ARM is the table (`set` · `commit` · `clear` · `sweep` · `remove` · `repair` · `descendant`), with three arms the as-filed text did not cover now stated: a REFUSED COMMIT FIRES NOTHING (its only channel is the receipt), a commit that cleared nothing STILL FIRES with `cleared: []`, and `origin` is required on the `descendant` arm alone.** **(7) THE WINDOW-BOUNDS AUTHORITY — `§2.5`'s `B-8` block and `§3.6` `A-1`'s annotation:** **`file.window.bounds` is OWNED BY THE RENDERER'S TABLE and APPLIED BY `main`, which reads it through the tier-1 channel at construction and applies the renderer's write on change; the as-filed `A-1` clause *"it needs no IPC at all"* is a FALSE cell and is annotated beside; a main-side direct write would be the second writer the decided split forbids; and a miss at construction lands the landed `980 × 720` literals as a DECLARED FALLBACK, never an invented size.** **(8) THE PURITY CRITERION AND THE FIFTEEN — `§5.2.7` (new), with `§5.6.5` recomputed and `§6.5`'s `U-STORE-MODULES` row annotated:** ***"Pure functions that only use internal data can stay pure; external I/O outside of calling params and return values must go through data stores"*** **— so the verdict set is `8 STORE-BACKED` (`gutter.ts` · `relocate.ts` · `menu-template.ts` (latent) · `owned-list-host.ts` · `slot-host.ts` · `overlay.ts` · `theme.ts` · `focus-model.ts`) and `7 PURE` (`zones.ts` · `census.ts` · `container.ts` · `gesture-session.ts` · `layout-projection.ts` · `mount-invariant-guard.ts` · `gutter-affordance.ts`, of which the FIVE KERNELS keep their landed purity rows intact and owe NO store edge); `§5.2.6`'s *"all fifteen adopt the store/event paradigm"* reading and its *"the store read enters a kernel only as an argument"* hard row are SUPERSEDED BESIDE; the fork arithmetic is recomputed (`15 = 7 byte-moving + 1 edge-without-bytes + 7 pure`); and a `STORE-BACKED` module's unit owes the THREE register rows — the import census with its positive control, the no-module-level-binding row, and the TWO-RUN STORE-STATE-INDEPENDENCE DIFFERENTIAL (the only row that detects an ambient read).** **AND WHAT IT DOES *NOT* DO, STATED SO THE ROW IS NOT OVER-READ.** **NO PRE-EXISTING ROW IS AMENDED, WEAKENED, SUPERSEDED OR RE-READ BY IT: `FOUNDATION-STORE-FACILITY-IS-OPENED-…` and `FOUR-TIER-DATA-OWNERSHIP-MODEL` are CITED BY ROW NAME and marked none of them `AMENDED`/`SUPERSEDED` (the second carries a DATED POINTER only); `NO-FOUNDATION-CONFIG-FILE-FACILITY`'s `SUPERSEDED` marker, `E10-SINGLE-SINK-CHANNEL`, `GSESSION-DELEGATE-SURFACE-IS-FROZEN-FOR-E3-E4`, `PBT-REGISTER-REQUIRED-FOR-CODE-UNITS`, `ZONE-SIZE-DOMAIN-IS-CONSUMER-CARRIED-AND-THE-MINIMUM-CLAMP-IS-FAMILY-SIDE` and `AUTONOMY AFTER SPEC APPROVAL` are untouched; | **DECIDED: R-9-SCOPE-BANS-A-GLOBAL-ENGINE-ID-KEYED-MAP-AND-NOT-A-PER-LINK-CALLER-KEYED-WALK-GATED-TARGET-SET — the architect's `R-9` SCOPE RULING: `docs/specs/store-core.md` `§3.4` `R-9` bans a GLOBAL ENGINE-ID-KEYED map and the global string-to-entry map shape it names, and does NOT ban a PER-LINK, CALLER-KEYED, WALK-GATED TARGET SET keyed by name; `R-9`'s no-counter / no-UUID half STANDS and binds the store-minted handle's minting, its positive control is re-pointed so that it is NOT VACUOUS, and the held contract owes this ruling its own amendment row at the successor's spec-gate pass** | 2026-10-01 | **THE ARCHITECT'S RULING UNDER THE AMENDED STORE ARCHITECTURE — a SCOPE RULING, which names the class a prohibition binds and the argument that distinguishes the classes; it is NEVER a re-reading of the clause. Taken on the gate-1 record `docs/specs/store-node-graph-review.md` (its `§2` verdict table, its `§4` `DR-*` triage, its `§5`(c) collision reconciliation BY ROW ID and its `§6` `C-3`), after the proposal MADE the argument at `docs/specs/store-node-graph-proposal.md` `§0.4` `A-H`. THIS IS A NEW FACT, which is why it is rowed rather than cited.** **WHAT IT PINS, six clauses:** **(1) THE CLASS THE CLAUSE BINDS.** `R-9`'s *"no string-to-entry map"* is read as **a GLOBAL, process-wide, ENGINE-ID-KEYED map** — the map shape the design record counts as a THIRD holder over the same ids the engine's registry and `cssIndex`/`propsIndex` already index (`docs/specs/data-ownership-model-plan.md` `§7.2` `F-5`, `§3.3` row `2.3-8`; the proposal's `§0.4` `A-H` item (2)). **(2) THE CLASS IT DOES NOT BIND.** A **PER-LINK TARGET SET** scoped to **ONE anchor on ONE node**, keyed by **the caller's OWN declared segment** — *"a declared name is a declaration of the CALLER'S spelling, not a vocabulary the store owns"* (`docs/specs/data-ownership-model-plan.md` `§1.8` `R-6`) — consulted as the LAST STEP of a walk that already reached that node, never as a global probe. **(3) THE OBSERVABLE THAT DISTINGUISHES THEM — the load-bearing half, named because a scope ruling must be testable rather than taken on trust.** **A per-link set is UNREACHABLE WITHOUT ITS WALK:** no caller can address a node by spelling its id from outside; the same id string declared under a different top-level name is a DIFFERENT address; and a segment that is not the id of any node the walk reached is a **diagnostic failure, not a lookup miss**. **A GLOBAL engine-id-keyed map has the opposite observable: ANY caller holding an id string resolves it in ONE PROBE, across namespaces and across graphs — and the ENGINE's own resolution (`[H]` `src/renderer/runtime.ts:resolveTarget`, resolving an engine `nodeId`, then an authored `css.id`, then an authored `props.id`) already has exactly that property, so a store that did the same would be the third holder BY CONSTRUCTION.** **(4) WHAT SURVIVES UNCHANGED.** `R-9`'s **no-counter / no-UUID-site** half STANDS and binds the store-minted handle's minting — the proposal's `§13` `DR-10`(a) prices exactly that as its cost; `R-5`'s first half (no ENGINE id) and the held `§3.4` `R-9` row's own compensating cells (`P-13`, `§2.3` item 5) stand unamended. **(5) THE AMENDMENT ROW THE HELD CONTRACT OWES, AND THE RE-POINTED POSITIVE CONTROL.** `docs/specs/store-core.md` `§3.4` `R-9` **gains its own amendment row at the successor's spec-gate pass** (the proposal's `§0.4` `A-H` item (2)); and **`R-9`'s positive control — as filed *"a `Map` keyed by a derived value FAILS"* — is RE-POINTED SO THAT IT IS NOT VACUOUS: it must REDDEN on a GLOBAL engine-id-keyed map and stay GREEN on a per-link, caller-keyed, walk-gated target set; a control that passes on BOTH classes FAILS as vacuous** (the proposal's `§13` `DR-10`'s own falsifier). **A control that cannot express its failing case is itself a finding.** **(6) WHAT THIS ROW DOES NOT TOUCH, STATED SO IT IS NOT OVER-READ.** **NO PRE-EXISTING ROW IS AMENDED, WEAKENED, SUPERSEDED OR RE-READ BY IT.** The four data-ownership rows — **`FOUNDATION-STORE-FACILITY-IS-OPENED-AND-SUPERSEDES-NO-FOUNDATION-CONFIG-FILE-FACILITY`** · **`FOUR-TIER-DATA-OWNERSHIP-MODEL`** · **`QUALIFIED-READ-MERGED-READ-SUBTREE-EVENTS-AND-THE-PURITY-CRITERION`** · **`NEXT-SURVIVING-REPAIR-LANDING-AS-A-TABID-INSTANCE-PER-REFERENCE-CLEAR-EVENTS-DOWNWARD-REMOVAL-THE-REFERENCE-SET-CROSSING-AND-THE-CRITERION-AS-A-PLACEMENT-RULE`** — are **CITED BY ROW NAME and NONE is marked `AMENDED` or `SUPERSEDED`**; | **DECIDED: THE CONSTRAINTS ARE PASSED FUNCTIONS, NOT A DATA TABLE — a constraint is a function the register/link calls with the changed data and the current + next states, expecting a boolean and optionally an error message / refusal reason back; a repair is a function called on a constraint violation that takes corrective action to the data and outputs a success boolean and an optional error message; **CONSTRAINTS ARE CODE FEATURES, NOT RUNTIME DATA**; `unique-path/tier` is a GLOBAL BEHAVIORAL STATE of the graph, NOT a constraint; `count-exactly-one` is ONE example use of the general-purpose constraint/repair system** | 2026-10-03 | **THE ARCHITECT'S CORRECTION OF THE STORE'S CONSTRAINT/REPAIR ARCHITECTURE, verbatim in substance.** The four-tier store contract's `§2.7` and the frozen-surface artifacts declare a **data-table constraint model** (`GraphConstraint = { id, kind, matchedSet, evaluatedOn, repair, onRepeat, refusalReason }` with the closed kinds `'count-exactly-one' | 'unique-path-tier'` and the closed repair tokens `'next-surviving-by-order' | 'none'`). **THE ARCHITECT'S RULING — `D-GP-…-CONSTRAINTS-ARE-FUNCTIONS` (the operative ACTIVE row for the constraint/repair machinery):** (1) **A CONSTRAINT is a PASSED FUNCTION** that the register/link calls **with the changed data and the current and next states**, and **expects a boolean and optionally an error message / refusal reason back** — the predicate is code the caller supplies at construction, not a row the caller declares as data; (2) **A REPAIR is a PASSED FUNCTION** called on a constraint violation, **taking corrective action to the data** and **outputting a success boolean and an optional error message** — the corrective action is the caller's code, not a row-level token; (3) **CONSTRAINTS ARE CODE FEATURES, NOT DATA TO BE ADDED AT RUNTIME** — no constraint install path, no runtime table mutation, no `store.constraints` array of data rows in the authored store sense (the mechanisms may hold callables the caller passed in, but the store authors no constraint row and no table schema); (4) **`unique-path/tier` IS A GLOBAL BEHAVIORAL STATE OF THE GRAPH — NOT WHAT IS MEANT BY CONSTRAINT**: the single-holder-per-`(path, tier)` rule is a graph invariant enforced by the store's own write machinery (like monotonic persistence and the tree-by-construction invariant), never a caller-suppliable constraint row; (5) **`count-exactly-one` IS THE EXAMPLE** of a specific use of the general-purpose system: the constraint is a function checking **exactly one tab is marked `active`**; the repair is a function that **de-activates surplus active tabs** (when > 1), **activates the most-recently-active tab** (per tab-local data, when none), or **opens the landing page** (when the tab set is empty). **THE CONSEQUENCE FOR THE IN-FLIGHT UNIT: the contract's `§2.7` constraint table, the `GraphConstraint` type, the `evaluatedOn`/`repair`/`onRepeat`/`refusalReason` row members, and the frozen-surface artifacts' constraint rows are the DATA-TABLE model and must be RE-DERIVED to the passed-function model** — recorded as the ordered re-derivation (the T9-shaped constraint drift; see the `G1` acceptance cell + the compliance review `§9B` + this row) — with `count-exactly-one`'s tab use-case becoming the worked example of the function pair. **NO COUNT MOVES: the unit stays `OPEN`, the ledger stays `21 DONE / 3 open` UNITS = `24`, no unit is admitted, `RCA-8(f)` holds.** | the architect's correction of the store's constraint/repair architecture (2026-10-03), transcribed here and carried at the unit's re-derivation record; the superseded model is the contract's `§2.7` data table + the `GraphConstraint` type + the frozen-surface artifacts' constraint rows (each to be annotated SUPERSEDED-BESIDE at its own site by the re-derivation pass, `RCA-8(d)`); the `count-exactly-one` worked example (the active-tab constraint + its three-arm repair) is the architect's own; the queue item that carries it practically is `docs/pending.md` `§Q`'s queued pane-drag contract (the zone-size constraint's discard + two-arm repair) |

| **DECIDED: SECURE-TIER-IS-A-FILESTORE-PEER — its ONLY distinctions from the `file` tier are its FILE, its NO-LOWER-TIER-ALIAS rule and its ACCESS-CONTROL POLICY; the closed three-member `SecuritySettings` shape is NOT a security property and is to be OPENED to arbitrary data keyed by the app's own subsystems; the agent SECRET case is OUT OF SCOPE (an agent never reads or writes API keys — agent data belongs in the `mem`/`file` tiers); and the tier's exclusion from the durability ordering STANDS** | 2026-10-05 | **THE ARCHITECT'S RULING, verbatim in substance:** *"The only distinction between the security store behavior and the file store behavior should be saving to different files, not permitting lower-tier copies, and the access control policies limiting it from being readable by MCP-accessible methods. This includes arbitrary data storage. In practice this was previously constrained by presenting the secure settings UI in a separate provident graph that was not accessible from the MCP server."* **ISSUED ON THE GATE-1 DISPOSITION of the RCA + change-analysis pass (verdict `BLOCKED-ON-SEMANTICS` at its first filing, UNBLOCKED by these rulings); the dossier is `docs/specs/secure-tier-generalization-adoption-dossier.md` (filed this pass from the scratch artifact; digest `sha256:744d7f19…`), the gate-1 record is `docs/specs/secure-tier-generalization-review.md`, and the park row is `docs/pending.md`'s tier-4 region.** **WHAT IT PINS, six clauses:** **(1) THE PEER CLAIM — THREE DISTINCTIONS AND NO MORE.** Tier 4 is a file-backed store whose differences from the `file` tier are exactly: **(a)** it persists to its OWN file (`<userData>/provident-security.json`, one of the EXACTLY-TWO persisted files — the `G2` DONE pin, `docs/specs/store-security.md` `§1.2` item 2); **(b)** **NO LOWER-TIER COPY EXISTS FOR A `secure` NAME**; **(c)** its **access-control policy** refuses it to MCP-reachable methods. **A pass that finds a FOURTH distinction — a fixed field count, a closed member set, a shape restriction — has found a derived accretion, not a design rule, and must surface it rather than enforce it.** **(2) CLAUSE (b) IS A RESTATEMENT, NOT A REVERSAL — THE WEIGHT-BEARING READING (`D-CLAUSE-2`).** `secure.*` names **never resolve through `file`/`mem`/`temp`**, and a tier-4 write **never clears, aliases or creates a lower-tier holder** — i.e. `store-core-graph.md`'s `§0` `A2` (*"`secure` IS A SEPARATE MAIN-SIDE COLLECTION OUTSIDE THIS ORDERING AND CARRIES NO GRAPH NODE"*) and `P-GR-IM-14` **STAND UNMOVED**. **THE DESTRUCTIVE READING IS EXPLICITLY REFUSED:** tier 4 does **NOT** join the durability ordering as its highest member, because under the landed commit-clears-lower rule (`C-1..C-5`) `commit('secure.settings.theme.token', v)` would **CLEAR** `file.settings.theme.token` — the theme unit's landed read (`theme-store.ts`, `H2b`) — so writing a secret would delete a same-named persistent setting, and the reverse direction would let a `file` write delete a secret. **(3) THE ACCESS-CONTROL SCOPE — `D-SCOPE`: EVERY MCP-REACHABLE METHOD.** The predicate "MCP-accessible" is closed as **the MCP server's tool surface ∪ its resource surface ∪ its notification surface** — *every* method reachable through the backend's request path, **not** merely `ALL_TOOLS`. The manual-UI `IPC_SECURITY_GET`/`IPC_SECURITY_SET` channel is **NOT** an MCP method and stays operator-reachable; the operator pane keeps its own snapshot path. **(4) THE AGENT-SECRET CASE IS OUT OF SCOPE (`D-AGENT-SECRETS`), and this is what keeps the design coherent.** Verbatim in substance: *"Agent data goes in the mem/file tiers. The agent should not be using or modifying API keys, except to communicate with the app in the first place."* **So tier 4 is NOT an agent-readable secret store**, no agent-facing secret namespace is admitted by this row, and the central tension the gate-1 critique raised (*"an agent that must hold a secret"*) is **DISSOLVED rather than answered by a carve-out**: the agent's own data has three landed arbitrary-storage tiers (`mem.*`, `temp.*`, `file.settings.*`), and the app's token is handled by the handshake that authenticates the agent to the app, **never by an agent read of tier 4**. **(5) THE SHAPE IS OPENED — BUT THE VALIDATOR SURVIVES.** The closed three-member `SecuritySettings` record is a **derived** constraint (provenance: `ENGINE/STRUCTURAL` in the RCA — a boot-read robustness choice in `sanitize()`, `src/main/security-store.ts:37-46`, plus the pane's four fire-and-forget handler bodies, `secure-panels.ts:107-136`; **not** a security decision), and it is to be **opened to arbitrary data keyed by the app's own subsystems**. **THE `enabled`/`token`/`maxJournalLength` VALIDATION IS NOT DROPPED BY THIS OPENING** — it is the only thing standing between a torn or foreign `provident-security.json` and a `SecuritySettings` object the live `SecurityGate` trusts (`security-store.ts:53-61`); a general-record tier 4 that validates nothing is **inadmissible**. **(6) THE FORBIDDEN CARRIERS ARE UNTOUCHED.** No tier-4 value may appear in a graph node, a tool result, a resource or a notification payload (`data-ownership-model-plan.md` `§3.8`'s three forbidden reads; `store-security.md` `CURRENT STATE` item 6); the isolated pane graph (`docs/specs/secure-panels.md` D1–D8) **STANDS** — see the row below, which rules it. **WHAT THIS RULING DOES *NOT* DECIDE, stated so it is not over-read:** it does not name the persistence format, the declaration/minting mechanism for arbitrary names, the boot INGESTION shape, or the migration of the existing file — those are the units' contracts at their spec gates (clauses (3)/(4) of the row below). **NO `src/**` BYTE, NO SPEC CLAUSE AND NO TEST IS CHANGED BY THIS ROW**; the landed constraint cells it outgrows are annotated BESIDE. | architect's ruling (2026-10-05, the tier-4 generalization answer); the RCA + change-analysis pass (the provenance table classifying `set(patch)`/`sanitize()` as ENGINE/STRUCTURAL, the fall-through exclusion as ARCHITECT-DECIDED, and the typed `secure.*` refusal as DERIVED-BY-AGENT from `data-ownership-model-plan.md` `§1.3` `R-4` + `§2.3` row 2.4-12 + `store-core-graph.md` `§0` `A2`); `docs/specs/secure-tier-generalization-adoption-dossier.md`; `src/main/security-store.ts`; `docs/specs/store-security.md` `§1.2`/`§2.1` |

| **DECIDED: THE MCP SERVER AND THE SECURE TIER ARE MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE, THE GATE IS ADDED BESIDE THE ISOLATED PANE GRAPH (NOT INSTEAD OF IT), AND THE BOOT SEQUENCE IS OPEN→INGEST→CLOSE→ENABLE — `D-GATE` · `D-18` · `D-19`** | 2026-10-05 | **THE ARCHITECT'S RULING, verbatim in substance:** *"Proposed post-store implementation is to make the MCP server and the security store access mutually exclusive — opening the security store for read/write first requires disabling the MCP server, and vice versa"* · *"Add the gate, keep D1–D8"* · *"On boot, open and then close the store before turning on the MCP server so that other application functions can read and cache needed information (ex. access config for the gnosis engine or API keys)."* **WHAT IT PINS, six clauses:** **(1) THE EXCLUSION IS AN ENFORCED SERVER-SIDE INVARIANT, NOT AN OPERATOR PROCEDURE.** The legal state pairs are `{MCP-ENABLED, TIER-4-CLOSED}` and `{MCP-DISABLED, TIER-4-OPEN}`; the illegal pair is unreachable. **THE ENFORCEMENT SITE IS THE INVOCATION TURN, NOT REGISTRATION ALONE** (the `moduleToolAllowed` precedent, `src/main/security.ts:70-84`): `applyGatePatch` mutates the SDK registry and **cannot interrupt a call already dispatched to the renderer** (`src/main/mcp-server.ts:336-348`), and the HTTP transport is **per-POST** (`:934`), so a registry-only re-gate leaves a window bounded only by the request timeout. **The gate must therefore (a) refuse at invocation, (b) stamp an epoch on accepted work and invalidate in-flight work on the transition, and (c) apply to BOTH transports.** **(2) THE REFUSAL TOKEN IS A CHANNEL TOKEN, NOT A STORE-UNION MEMBER (`F-6`, closed).** The exclusion's refusal rides the `SecurityWriteReceipt` precedent — `'write-failed'` is deliberately **not** a member of the store's closed 16-member union (`src/main/security-store.ts:16-23`; `store-core-graph.md` `§2.1`'s annotation), and a second security token added to that union is a collision finding. **So the union stays 16 and the store module's bytes are untouched by the gate.** **(3) THE GATE SUPPLIES *STATE*; THE STORE KEEPS THE DECISION (`F-11`, closed).** The single `B-SECURE-GATE` refusal site in `src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts` (:478-479, :766, :922-923, :1417, :2277) **remains the ONE site that decides a `secure.*` name**; `main` owns only the open/closed state. **An implementation that re-implements a `secure.*` segment check in `main.ts` is a `P-7` second-authority finding.** **(4) `D-18` — THE ISOLATED PANE GRAPH STANDS.** `docs/specs/secure-panels.md`'s D1–D8 contract (own `GraphScope` + hub + `Supervisor` + `DomAdapter`; *"No cross-graph addressability"*) is **NOT retired and NOT demoted to a presentation detail**: the gate is **ADDED BESIDE IT** as the load-bearing control for the *renderer-originated generic path*, and the pane's `cfg` snapshot **stays OUT of the generic store** — so `data-ownership-model-plan.md` `§2.3` row **2.4-12** (*"It must NOT be re-homed into a generic store … would widen `§6.4`'s channel by construction"*) **STANDS UNMOVED and its second sentence is NOT superseded.** **The threat the graph closes — an operator socially induced by agent-authored rendered content to open the store — is exactly the threat a switch cannot close, which is why the graph is kept.** **(5) `D-19` — THE BOOT SEQUENCE IS PINNED, AND IT IS THE INGESTION MECHANISM.** Verbatim in substance: **open the tier at boot, let the app's own subsystems read and cache the values they need into their own holders, CLOSE the tier, THEN enable the MCP server.** **Consequences, recorded:** (a) the tier's values are consumed **once, at boot**, into main-side holders — so the subsystems that need a value hold it themselves and **the tier's read path stays closed for the whole MCP-enabled lifetime**; (b) **tier 4 does NOT join the graph** — this clause is what retires the `hydrate(rows)` problem (`store-core-graph.ts:2184-2196` skips every non-`file` token by declaration, a frozen clause), because nothing tier-4 needs to be minted into a graph at boot; (c) **the boot terminal for the crash case is thereby DEFINED AND FAIL-SAFE** (`F-2`, closed): the tier is closed and MCP is enabled unless a boot pass explicitly opens it, and because `sanitize()`'s corrupt-file fallback makes a torn record indistinguishable from a cold one (`security-store.ts:53-61`), a torn exclusion record resolves to the SAFE pair. **(6) THE REFERENT THAT WAS OPEN IS NOT PART OF THIS FOUNDATION.** The gate-1 pass could not resolve the boot-read example's named consumer; the architect's answer is that **it is an EXAMPLE CASE FOR A LATER FORK, NOT A FOUNDATION COMPONENT** — so the foundation's obligation is the **mechanism** (open→read→cache→close→enable) and its **timing contract**, while *which* values a fork reads at that point, and *what* it does with them, are **fork-side** (the `H-r6` fork-side-return convention: this repo writes no file under the fork's tree). **The base surface ships no fork-specific consumer.** **WHAT THIS RULING DOES *NOT* DECIDE:** the arbitrary-name storage mechanism, the persisted format, and which subsystems ingest at boot are the units' to specify (the row above, clause (5)); and the live-battery obligation for the gate's UI control is carried as a MANDATORY gate-6 condition by the review record, not waived by this row. **NO `src/**` BYTE AND NO SPEC CLAUSE IS CHANGED BY THIS ROW.** | architect's rulings (2026-10-05: `D-GATE`, `D-18`, `D-19`, and the gnosis-as-fork-example answer); the gate-1 critique's threat-model table; `src/main/security.ts:70-84`; `src/main/mcp-server.ts:336-348`, `:934`; `src/renderer/secure-panels.ts`; `docs/specs/secure-panels.md` D1–D8; `docs/specs/data-ownership-model-plan.md` `§2.3` row 2.4-12 |

| **DECIDED: THE TIER-4 GENERALIZATION IS SPLIT PER UNIT — `U-SECURE-EXCLUSION` FIRST (no frozen artifact), `U-TIER4-ARBITRARY-STORAGE` SECOND — taken per `D-GP-SAD-5`** | 2026-10-05 | **THE ARCHITECT'S RULING, verbatim in substance: *"Gate first, store-generalization second."*** **WHAT IT PINS — THE DECOMPOSITION ROW (`D-GP-SAD-5`, nine columns, keyed `decomposition`, dispositioned HERE on this record and never inside a spec):** **`recommendation` = SPLIT-RECOMMENDED** · **`proposed fraction` = TWO units** · **`boundary (named)` = **(i) `U-SECURE-EXCLUSION`** owns the exclusion state machine and its enforcement sites — `src/main/main.ts` (the boot gate construction `:86-90`, the `IPC_SECURITY_*` handler sites `:369-383`, the transport construction + `mcp.start()`), `src/main/security.ts` (`SecurityGate`), `src/main/mcp-server.ts` (`applyGatePatch` `:438`, the captured transport handles `:335-348`, the invocation-turn refusal, the HTTP per-POST path `:934`), `src/main/preload.ts`, one channel constant, and ONE new operator control in `src/renderer/secure-panels.ts`; a new contract `docs/specs/secure-exclusion.md`. **It touches NO frozen artifact and NO store byte — that is the boundary's decisive property.** **(ii) `U-TIER4-ARBITRARY-STORAGE`** owns the tier's shape — `src/main/security-store.ts` (`:25-33` the API, `:37-46` `sanitize()`, the persisted format), the boot INGESTION pass (open→read→cache→close per `D-19`), and the spec amendments (`mcp-endpoint.md` `§6.4`) — **and it owes the tier's own contract, whose form depends on whether arbitrary runtime writes are in scope.** · **`remainder / coherent core` = (i) is NOT the coherent core, because it is independently landable, independently falsifiable and carries no re-freeze: the exclusion alone removes the specific guarantee that made tier 4 unreachable while touching nothing frozen. (2) is the coherent core: it is ONE contract about ONE tier's shape, whose sub-parts (the name mechanism, the persisted format, the validator's survival, the refusal-token home) all hang off the same decision and are not independently delegable.** · **`why not NO-SPLIT` = merging them makes the gate's landing contingent on a re-freeze the gate does not need, and `AGENTS.md` RCA-2/RCA-5 make "a multi-unit deliverable split PER UNIT" a hard gate; Writes at runtime are permitted iff the MCP is blocked"* · **THE FIVE ANSWERS THIS ROW RESTS ON, in substance (issued `2026-10-11`; gate-1 record `docs/specs/tier4-clone-and-write-gate-review.md` `§12.2`, the verdict re-issued at `§12.6`):** **(1) `D-2` IS STRUCTURAL — not a runtime predicate over MCP state: the tier's write surfaces stay NON-MCP-REACHABLE, and enforcement lives where enforcement already lives, at the exclusion gate's INVOCATION TURN over the MCP transports** (so no gate state in the store and no decision in `main` — `F-11`'s standing rule). **(2) THE CLONE DISCIPLINE IS A DEEP COPY — cycle-safe and prototype-safe — AND ANY VALUE THE TIER CANNOT PERSIST AS JSON MUST NOT BE ACCEPTED** (the precedent the answer names is the landed `snapshotValue`, `src/renderer/store-core-graph.ts:362-388`: a `seen` map, `Object.create(null)`, no depth bound — the `G4-F5` pin); **a JSON round-trip is NOT the implementation of either half** (it throws on cycles/`BigInt`), so the admission test is a VALUE-PRESERVATION test on the write path, never a serialization. **(3) THE READ-SURFACE SET IS THE FULL ACCESS SURFACE — NO CARVE-OUT — AND ACCESS IS FULL ONLY WHEN THE TIER IS UNLOCKED AND THE MCP IS BLOCKED** (the measured census is exactly `["get","lastWriteReceipt","set"]`, and `lastWriteReceipt()` — `src/main/security-store.ts:142-144` — hands back the SAME receipt object by identity, so it is IN the set: a FIX OBLIGATION with its own red row, never an exemption). **⟶ FIX OBLIGATION DISCHARGED, ANNOTATED BESIDE, DATED `2026-10-11` (GATES 7+8 OF UNIT `S3`; **⟶ FOLLOW-UP RULING ANNOTATED BESIDE, DATED `2026-10-11` (this pass's date; `RCA-8(d)`): THE ROW ABOVE STANDS BYTE-FOR-BYTE — not one clause of it is rewritten — and this block is an INSERTION between that row and the `_(none yet — decisions opened 2026-08-21.)_` sentinel, so NO previously cited `docs/decisions.md:<n>` anchor moves.** **THE QUESTION PUT TO THE ARCHITECT, in substance:** *on a refused persist, is the correct reading **(a)** apply in memory but never report committed, or **(b)** the live state must equal the durable state, so a refused persist rolls the record back?* **THE ARCHITECT'S ANSWER, IN FULL AND VERBATIM: *"Live state equals durable state"* — i.e. READING **(b)**.** **WHAT IT RULES, five clauses:** **(1) LIVE STATE EQUALS DURABLE STATE — a refused persist ROLLS THE RECORD BACK, so a READ never shows a value the FILE does not carry.** `current` is assigned ONLY after a SUCCESSFUL `persist()`, and a refusal RETURNS THE PRE-WRITE RECORD. **(2) THE RECEIPT'S CONSEQUENCE:** a refused write reports `refused`/`write-failed` **AND leaves the tier's live record AT ITS PRE-WRITE VALUE** — the receipt and the tier AGREE, which is precisely what the landed tree does NOT do today (the receipt is honest while the record advances: `SECURITY-STORE-RECORD-ADVANCES-PAST-A-REFUSED-PERSIST`, `docs/defects.md` `## OPEN`). **(3) THE PANE'S CONSEQUENCE:** the pane renders the **PRE-WRITE** state and NEVER an uncommitted one — no phantom value is ever shown, and the refusal's own signal becomes something the spec MUST design (the pane's four handler bodies read nothing today — the gate-1 record's `§8 F-4`). **(4) THIS DECIDES the condition the gate-1 record `docs/specs/tier4-clone-and-write-gate-review.md` carried as a `CONDITION` at its `§12.3` and listed among the architect's conditions as `C-A1` at its `§12.6`:** the reading is NO LONGER spec-dischargeable by a declared default, and it is no longer `C-A1`'s to name. **(5) IT SUPERSEDES THE OPPOSITE DISCIPLINE WHERE IT IS DOCUMENTED — `docs/specs/store-security.md`'s `§2.1` item 2, the in-memory-applies clause** (*"**The in-memory config is the process-lifetime authority** … the receipt makes its divergence from the file VISIBLE (`§2.3`), it does not change `get()`'s answer"*): a dated `SUPERSEDED BY` note is filed beside that clause in this same pass, and THE CLAUSE'S AS-FILED BYTES ARE KEPT VISIBLE AND ARE NOT REWRITTEN. **THE MEASUREMENT THE RULING ANSWERS — CITED, NOT RE-MEASURED:** the store-compliance battery's Family E (`docs/specs/store-compliance-live-battery.md` `§9.3`) measured the VIOLATION — driver `tests/store-compliance-live.mjs` sha256 `ad7bbb6639ef9b7ad0f2d8ffa3b2c2d64bb6d3e03d5579fa1929562c06647751`, commits `f700aaf`/`742b848`, battery `46` rows = `34 PASS / 11 FAIL / 1 MANUAL`, exit `1` — at THREE unwritable paths (the parent is a file; a `0500` directory; the target itself is a directory) the receipt is HONEST (`{"status":"refused","reason":"write-failed"}`) **but the in-memory record ADVANCES — the read-back shows the change while the file bytes are unmoved** — and the LIVE arm booted the app on a scratch profile whose `provident-security.json` was a **DIRECTORY**, refused the operator's write, and STILL showed the new value on the live read-back while the on-disk write returned `EISDIR`. **ANNOTATION ONLY: no unit is admitted, no clause of the row above is rewritten or deleted, and NO LEDGER COUNT MOVES (`RCA-8(f)`).**

**⟶ THE EARLIER UNDECIDED CLAUSE IS KEPT VISIBLE, AND THIS ONE LINE IS THE POINTER THAT SUPERSEDES ITS EFFECT — NOT ITS BYTES.** The row above carries **`SECURITY-STORE-RECORD-ADVANCES-PAST-A-REFUSED-PERSIST`** as a condition whose readings are named and neither chosen (the gate-1 record's `C-A1`) — **THAT ITEM IS NOW DECIDED: READING (b), LIVE STATE EQUALS DURABLE STATE,** a refused persist ROLLS THE RECORD BACK. The as-filed clause is NOT deleted and NOT reworded; a reader takes the row above TOGETHER WITH this block, and this block governs the reading.

**⟶ SECOND FOLLOW-UP RULING ANNOTATED BESIDE, DATED `2026-10-11` (THE `S3` POST-DONE AMENDMENT PASS — the pass that follows the `U-SECURE-STORE-DISCIPLINE` gate-10 close-out and its commit `54f6688`; `RCA-8(d)`: THE ROW ABOVE, ITS FIRST FOLLOW-UP BLOCK AND THE POINTER LINE ABOVE ALL STAND BYTE-FOR-BYTE — this block is an INSERTION between the pointer line and the `_(none yet — decisions opened 2026-08-21.)_` sentinel, so NO previously cited `docs/decisions.md:<n>` anchor moves).** **THE TWO QUESTIONS PUT TO THE ARCHITECT, AND THE TWO ANSWERS, IN FULL AND VERBATIM, ONE SENTENCE EACH:**
**(1) *"Is the parent-directory creation intended?"* — ANSWER: *"Yes, create directory"*. IT IS A DOCUMENTATION RESOLUTION AND ADDS NO BEHAVIOUR.** The landed write path's own first step (`mkdirSync(dirname(opts.path), {recursive:true})`, `src/main/security-store.ts`) CREATES a missing parent and the write COMMITS with the file present; **the creation is therefore a DECLARED ARM OF `opts.path`'s DOMAIN**, never an accident of the landed five-step replace, and the one reading that said otherwise — the `S3` contract's `§6` `PAR-1` OUTSIDE column's *"a nonexistent parent"* arm — is superseded in effect with a dated note BESIDE its own cell. **The measured witness is the gate-5 blind row `SSD-G-75`** (`receipt={"status":"committed"}`, `get().token === 'NP'`, `fileExists=true`), whose `SPEC-AMBIGUOUS` entry is resolved beside it (verdict kept as filed; no scenario re-run, no verdict converted). **No code, no test and no counterexample move.**
**(2) *"Is the intra-write ORDER required?"* — ANSWER: *"Lock out the reads until the commit lands". IT IS A NEW BEHAVIOURAL CLAUSE, AND IT DECIDES THE RESIDUAL IN THE AFFIRMATIVE.** **WHILE A WRITE IS IN FLIGHT, READS ARE LOCKED OUT UNTIL THE COMMIT LANDS: at every instant of the write's window the read surface's answers and the real path's bytes form ONE AGREED PAIR** — so no reader observes the PRE-WRITE record once the commit has landed and none observes the uncommitted CANDIDATE before it does; the in-flight attempt's own receipt is WITHHELD until its terminal lands, and **no new token, no new receipt form and no union member is introduced** (the closed two-form receipt and the store's `16`-member union are UNMOVED). **Consequence, stated because it is a CODE OBLIGATION and not a re-description: the record's advance MUST LAND AT THE COMMIT (the successful `renameSync`), inside the write's own window** — the as-landed advance (after `persist()` returns) is the FAILING shape the gate-5 row `SSD-G-81` measured, and the change is INSIDE the admitted unit's own edit set.
**WHERE BOTH LAND (`CROSS-REF`), SO THE NEXT PASS READS THEM AT THEIR OWN BYTES:** `docs/specs/secure-store-discipline.md` — **`§2.6`** (the write lock, APPENDED: `§2.1`–`§2.5` renumber nothing and their items are untouched), its item 5's precedence clause against `§0A` item 2 and `§2.1` item 3 steps 4/5, its `§2.4` `R-7` state row, its `§3.1` `M-8` / `§3.2` `F-13` / `§3.3` `I-10` behaviour rows, its `§4.1` item 3 (the amendment's red-set obligation: authored and RUN RED before any host byte), its `§5.1` item 6 (diff scope: inside the existing edit set), its `§5.2` item 6 (`§7.1` re-checked: `DOES NOT TRIGGER`, with ONE reading FLAGGED for the orchestrator rather than decided), its `§5.3` item 5 (what an amendment to a `DONE` row owes), its `§5.6.1` row **`10`** (`P-M-SM-3`, `S-SS-LOCK-1`, `12` declared attempts; the register's operative total is now **`130 = 51 + 12 + 6 + 7 + 16 + 2 + 8 + 8 + 8 + 12`**, `P-IM 66` · `P-SM 20` · `P-TP 44`, caps `51 ≤ 100` / `130 ≤ 400`), its §6 `PAR-1`/`PAR-7`/`PAR-10` dated notes, its `§7d` (both residuals answered, with the page-design determination re-checked: no `docs/skills/designing-pages.md` exists and none is owed) and its dated notes beside `§0A` item 2 and `§2.1` item 3 step 5 / item 4; and `docs/specs/secure-store-discipline-greens.md` `§5b`/`§6`'s resolutions beside `SSD-G-75`/`SSD-G-81` (both verdicts kept as filed). **`RCA-8(f)`: THE UNIT STAYS `DONE` — the ledger does NOT move for an amendment to an existing DONE row (`31 DONE / 2 open` = `33` UNITS is UNMOVED, no row added, no row re-opened) — and the row now owes a POST-DONE AMENDMENT CYCLE (`docs/next-steps.md`'s `## DONE — U-SECURE-STORE-DISCIPLINE`: red first → the host change → gate 4's confirmation → the trio → an amendment block on the DONE row).** **NO `src/**` BYTE, TEST, DRIVER, CONFIG, SCRIPT OR PASS RECORD WAS TOUCHED BY THIS PASS; NO EARLIER RULING WAS RE-OPENED** (`§7c`'s `Q-1`/`Q-2` keep their declared defaults).

**⟶ THIRD FOLLOW-UP RULING ANNOTATED BESIDE, DATED `2026-10-11` (THE `S2` TIER-4 GATING-CLAUSE AMENDMENT PASS — the pass that files AMENDMENT TWO of the gate-1 record `docs/specs/tier4-clone-and-write-gate-review.md`; `RCA-8(d)`: THE ROW ABOVE, ITS TWO EARLIER FOLLOW-UP BLOCKS AND THE POINTER LINE ABOVE ALL STAND BYTE-FOR-BYTE — this block is an INSERTION between the second follow-up block and the `_(none yet — decisions opened 2026-08-21.)_` sentinel, so NO previously cited `docs/decisions.md:<n>` anchor moves; `RCA-8(f)`: **NO ACTIVE ROW IS ADDED BY THIS PASS — the architect admits ROWS, and this is a dated block beside an existing row**).** **THE STATEMENT AND THE CLARIFICATIONS, IN FULL AND VERBATIM IN SUBSTANCE:** *"The write should fail while MCP is open, as should read. The MCP should inversely fail while the store is open. The open state can be determined by direct lookup."* — with the five ambiguity answers **(1)** the failure form is an **appropriate error message** · **(2)** the enforcement sites are **every MCP endpoint, and the read/write functions** · **(3)** the reads in scope are **all security reads** · **(4)** the source of truth is **a top-level boolean switch both read from** · **(5)** the transition/illegal-pair question is **"see above"** — and, on being shown the two reviews' `FLAWED` finding, the three-part answer **(a)** the open/closed state *"is not data located within the store's internals"*, which is **what the top-level boolean carries** · **(b)** *"Yes I know it's contrary to earlier designs, said earlier designs were breaking"* · **(c)** *"Invent a legal token, I just said that."*
**WHAT IT RULES, SIX CLAUSES. `(1) THE SUPERSESSION IS AUTHORIZED, AND ITS GROUND IS MEASURED, NOT RHETORICAL:`** the earlier structural reading is superseded because the earlier design is **measurably breaking** — the store-compliance live battery's Family D measured a tier-4 write **committing while the tier was CLOSED** (`SC-D-05`) **and again after the operator's return** (`SC-D-07`), both `FAIL`, reproduced across two rounds and counted as a term by Family E's `SC-E-09` `c4`; `docs/pending.md` `§R` `P-R6` is the park row. **`(2) THE ROW'S ANSWER (1) IS SUPERSEDED IN EFFECT:`** the clause *"`D-2` IS STRUCTURAL … so **no gate state in the store** and **no decision in `main`**"* no longer governs. **THE REPLACEMENT READING, IN THE ARCHITECT'S OWN OPERATIVE WORDS: *"the store's read/write functions DO consult the shared state, and the state remains ONE value (the boolean) with ONE writer (the transition)."*** **THREE THINGS MOVE:** **(i)** the **write** refusal is evaluated at the **store's own write function**, not only at the MCP invocation turn; **(ii)** the **read** refusal is evaluated at the **store's own read functions** — a site the structural reading did not have; **(iii)** the **MCP-side** enforcement principle (`D-GATE` clause (1)'s invocation turn over the MCP transports) **STANDS UNCHANGED**. **`(3) AND TWO STANDING RULES ARE EXPLICITLY *NOT* SUPERSEDED, STATED SO THE REVERSAL IS NOT OVER-READ:`** **`F-11`'s rule still governs `main`** — *"`main` supplies STATE, never takes the DECISION"* (`N-1`/`N-2` unmoved), and **`D-GATE` clause (2)'s channel-token rule is unmoved** — **the new refusal token is a CHANNEL token, never a member of the store's closed `16`-member union.** **`(4) THE TOKEN IS INVENTED UNDER ANSWER (c), AND THIS IS ITS NOMINATION:** **`'tier4-closed'`**, declared **beside the landed `EXCLUSION_CLOSED` constant in `src/main/mcp-server.ts`**, carried on the third member of the already-declared receipt form family `{status:'refused', reason:'tier4-closed', message:<…>}` with a **server-authored** message naming the cause (the MCP is open) and the remedy (disable MCP), **never a caller-supplied one** (`PAR-8`), and **NOT** `'exclusion-closed'` — **whose declared domain is the MCP-call cause and whose cause is the INVERSE (the MCP refused because the store is open)**; the two tokens are **mutual inverses on the same axis**, which is what makes a live claim about either one checkable. The nomination, its home, its shape and its message are at the gate-1 record's **`§13.6` `TOK-1`**. **`(5) THE STATE'S HOME, WRITER AND BOOT VALUE:`** the boolean **is the exclusion state itself** — **not a new fact and not a second fact**, so *"not data located within the store's internals"* is satisfied literally — and its **one value already has exactly one home** (`src/main/security.ts:202`'s private `_exclusion` record; read by `exclusionState()` `:248-250`), with **one writer**: the **transition** (`withExclusion`, `:265-281`, invoked by `mcp.applyExclusion`). **THE BOOT VALUE IS DECLARED `STORE-OPEN`** (the access-full state the architect's own words require — *"access is full only when the tier is UNLOCKED and the MCP is blocked"* — and **`D-19`'s open→ingest→close→enable order needs the store open at boot**), and it flips to **store-closed before the MCP is enabled** — so the transient pair `{MCP-DISABLED, TIER-4-CLOSED}` is a **real but TRANSIENT boot state, not a legal steady pair**; the declaration is **reversible in one initialization**, and the record states that flipping it would instead require an explicit boot-ingestion exception the `D-19` order avoids. **AND THE DEADLOCK THE TWO REVIEWS FOUND IS DISSOLVED, NOT EXCEPTED:** the operator's indicator — the pane's `MCP:` segment and the exclusion toggle's `data-state` — **must be fed by the BOOLEAN, not by the `exclusion` member the `IPC_SECURITY_GET` carrier derives from a store read**, so **gating store reads can no longer blank the only operator-visible reader of the state it is conditioned on**. **`(6) WHAT THIS NOTE DOES *NOT* DO — the bound, so a later pass does not read it as wider:** it **does not** move a ledger count, admit a unit or open a spec gate (`RCA-8(f)`); it **does not** discharge `docs/pending.md` `§R` `P-R6`, which stays `OPEN` with its owner; it **does not** touch `docs/specs/secure-tier-generalization-review.md`; and it **names but does not re-grain** the rows the change reaches (`docs/specs/secure-exclusion.md` `§2.1` item 6 / `I-EX-2` / `P-EX-IM-3`'s cell (g) — the *"module-level mutable record FAILS"* clause now needs to distinguish **a second home** from **a reader of the one home**; `§3.2` `FS-EX-13`, which is **unmoved by its own terms** because it binds a `secure.*` **NAME** check in `main`, not a **STATE** consult; `PAR-11`'s pane cells; `store-channels.ts`'s constant census, **unmoved**). **THE FULL RECORD, WITH EVERY CELL, ITS OWNER AND ITS COST, IS THE GATE-1 RECORD'S `§13`: read this block TOGETHER WITH `§13.7`.** **`RCA-8(f)` HOLDS: THE ARCHITECT'S *"Proceed"* IS `S2`'s ADMISSION, AND THE ROW ALREADY EXISTED AS `OPEN`, SO NO LEDGER COUNT MOVES — `32 DONE / 1 open` UNITS = `33` is UNMOVED.**

current behaviour; the `MODULE-EXTENSIONS` row that shared this section has LANDED

**GATED PROPOSAL (2026-08-25):** the MCP-resources proposal
(`docs/FORKER.md` §4, R1-R5 digest) passed the three-agent gate
(verdict PROCEED-WITH-RESHAPES) —
resources `mcp://provident/app`, `mcp://provident/node/{nodeId}` (template),
`mcp://provident/targets`, gated as `read`-group members (R1 blocking: never
always-registered), live re-gated (R2), both transport builds (R3), node-template
in-tree validation + no SecurePanels reach (R4). **LANDED 2026-08-25** —
implemented (`src/main/mcp-server.ts` + `security.ts`), tests
`mcp-resources.test.ts` + `mcp-resources-adversarial.test.ts`, greens
`mcp-resources-greens.md`, spec `mcp-endpoint.md` §3.6. Trio green 472 / 2.
Rows open 2026-08-21.

**GATED PROPOSAL (2026-08-25):** the live-change-notification proposal
(`docs/FORKER.md` §4, N1-N7 digest) passed the three-agent gate
(verdict PROCEED-WITH-RESHAPES) —
server-initiated push so an agent reacts to state changes instead of pull-reading
(and invalidates cached resource URIs). Reshapes N1-N7: (N1) a notification-type
enum; (N2) stdio-only push ENFORCED (HTTP is stateless → no-op + surfaced marker);
(N3) app-Runtime-only push source (SecurePanels never emits — no operator leak);
(N4) a new `provident:notify` renderer→main IPC channel; (N5) gated + opt-in
DEFAULT-OFF; (N6) coalescing + stdio test matrix; (N7) low-level
`sendResourceUpdated` + `resources/subscribe` gate. Overrides the
`mcp-endpoint.md` §8 "No server push" non-goal. **LANDED 2026-08-25** — a
stdio-only, app-Runtime-sourced `notifications/resources/updated` push
(`mcp://provident/app`) after a mutating app-graph op, gated `read`-group,
never the SecurePanels graph. **N7 residual:** the `sendResourceUpdated` uses
the low-level `McpServer.server` but does NOT track per-client
`resources/subscribe` — it emits on the connected stdio server regardless (a
documented client-contract residual, not a correctness break for the always-fresh
contract). Tests `mcp-notify.test.ts` + `mcp-notify-adversarial.test.ts`; trio
480 / 2.

**GATED PROPOSAL (2026-08-25):** `code.loadBatch` / write buffer (A4) — stage N
`code.*` envelope edits, ONE re-derive — passed the three-agent gate

registration checklist; the no-envelope case; honest benefit framing (round-trips

the **non-placement attribution run reproduces `count 2`**, so the defect is **not placement-specific**. **THE MECHANISM (visible in the serialization, not inferred):** `tearDownGraph()`'s re-render mounts an **emptied previous root** (`class="demo-shell"`, no children), then `loadEnvelope`'s **`resetRenderState()` drops the diff baseline** (`src/renderer/runtime.ts:803-809`: `domPrevMap`/`ssrPrevMap` → `null` + `prevStates.clear()`), so the removal ops the diff would have emitted are never emitted and **the new root is APPENDED ALONGSIDE the old one**; both carry `data-node-id` (`:90`'s `{ nodeIdAttribute: true }`), so both count. **REACHABILITY, stated so the fix is scoped:** the **live boot order does NOT reach it** (`src/renderer/renderer.ts` `bootstrap()`es before any load), but **the host's OWN load entry on a not-yet-rendered runtime DOES** — and the landed placement row **`tests/runtime-host.test.ts:183-189` uses exactly that sequence** (construct → `loadEnvelope`, no `bootstrap()`). **WHAT THE FIX MUST ACHIEVE (the invariant the unit exists to keep):** *after every re-derivation of the four paths of §1 item 4 completes, the mount holds exactly ONE engine-emitted root element and it is the graph's current root node* — so `M-17` (the reproducer) and `M-18` (the attribution + placement variant) both report **`ok === true` / `count === 1` / `violation === null`**, with the previous root's element no longer a direct child of the mount. **Either reading is licensed** (complete `tearDownGraph()`'s diff-based emptying **before** the baseline is dropped, **or** reconcile the mount before the new tree is emitted) **and the fix must say which it took**; neither may skip the other paths, touch the shim (§5.1's outside-scope list), add an MCP surface, or change `teardown()`'s measured state (**that path is not the defect path**). **THE PROBE STAYS THE UNIT'S ACCEPTANCE EVIDENCE** — §2.1's surface is unweakened, and the acceptance is the probe's result on the reproducer, **never** a private seam. **DISPOSITION: `OWED`** — recorded in the spec's **§3a** (a **HOST** finding, fixed here) and **deliberately NOT** a `docs/defects.md`/`docs/HANDOFF.md` row (§4.4 `S-1`; the **36 module-absent** reds are §4.2 step 2's expected class, and the **3 red-against-the-tree** rows (`M-14` raw `2`; `M-11`/`M-12` raw `0`) are the findings. | the red run 2026-09-27 (`docs/specs/mount-invariant-guard.md` §4.6, §4.4 `S-1`, §3a `RED-1`…`RED-6`); on a NEVER-BOOTSTRAPPED runtime the FIRST `teardown()` leaves ONE mounted root (the graph's live in-tree root, `inTree === 1`), later cycles leave ZERO, and that difference is pinned by `M-19`; the invariant is stated over RE-DERIVED points, and the teardown path is NOT one** | 2026-09-27 **(DRIVE QUALIFIER ADDED 2026-09-27 — the adversarial + blind-verification pass; on a **NEVER-BOOTSTRAPPED** runtime (`new Runtime({mount, envelope})`, no `bootstrap()`, no prior load) the **FIRST** `teardown()` leaves **ONE** mounted root — the graph's **live in-tree root** (`listTargets` agrees, `census.inTree === 1`) — and cycles **2** and **3** leave **0**, so the idempotence claim is **drive-specific too**. **That difference is pinned by the spec's appended `§3.1 M-19`; the mechanism is `tearDownGraph()` → `render()`, whose compile branch mounts the graph whenever `bootstrapped === false` (`src/renderer/runtime.ts:155-173`), with `reconcileMount()` reachable only via `resetRenderState()`.** **The unconditional form — "`teardown()` leaves the mount with ZERO engine-emitted roots", the row's own as-filed heading, kept verbatim in the date cell above — is SUPERSEDED by this qualifier.** The as-filed rows asserted *"`count === 1` after teardown"*; **the measured truth is `count === 0` with `mountHTML: ""`**, matching `tests/runtime-host.test.ts:157`/`:160`'s `mount.innerHTML === ''` — **the citation the as-filed row itself called "the serialization side of the same fact", which asserts an EMPTY mount** (so the as-filed row was internally inconsistent: the citation resolves, the count did not). **Under the spec's own §3.2 rule that state reads as `'no-root'`.** **THE TWO-LAYER FACT, and a row must assert BOTH halves:** **(a) the MOUNT** — `count === 0`, `roots` empty, `ok === false`, `violation.code === 'no-root'`; **(b) the GRAPH** — the root **remains in the graph**: `census.inTree === 1` (`src/renderer/runtime.ts:783-786` skips the root inside `tearDownGraph()`; `teardown()` is `:560-563`). **The root is deliberately DETACHED FROM THE MOUNT while the graph KEEPS it** — that is the whole content of the pair, and a one-half reading is the contradiction this row removes. **CONSEQUENCE FOR §1.1's INVARIANT WORDING:** the invariant reads *"exactly one engine-emitted root **at every point where the graph has been RE-DERIVED**"* — and **the teardown path is not such a point**, because `teardown()` detaches the root from the mount on purpose while the graph retains it. **The amended wording lives in the spec (§1.1 item 1, with the as-filed sentence kept visible), no row is renumbered and no `M`/`I`/`F` id moves**; and **`M-12`'s idempotence subject changes only in its expected value** — cycles 1/2/3 all hold the SAME state (`0` on the mount, `1` in the graph), so a later cycle producing `1` or `2` is the failure the row rejects. | the red run 2026-09-27 (`docs/specs/mount-invariant-guard.md` §3.1 `M-11`/`M-12` as amended, §1.1 item 1, §4.6 item 5); each has a disposition in `docs/specs/mount-invariant-guard.md` §3b.** **(1) THE BLOCKER, MEASURED TWICE INDEPENDENTLY (adversarial pass + blind verification):** the teardown wording of the contract **and of this file** was **unconditional** and is **true only on a bootstrapped / re-baselined runtime**; on a runtime **constructed and never `bootstrap()`ed** (and never loaded before the teardown) the **FIRST** `teardown()` leaves **ONE** engine-emitted root — genuinely the graph's live in-tree root (`listTargets` agrees, `census.inTree === 1`) — while cycles **2** and **3** leave **0**, so the idempotence claim is **drive-specific too**. **Verbatim:** cycle 1 `childCount 1`, `nodeIds ["<root>"]`, `class "demo-shell"`, `mountHTML '<div data-node-id="…" id="preempt-node-…" class="demo-shell"></div>'`, `census {"registered":11,"inTree":1,"unplaced":10,"destroyed":0,"prototypes":0}`; cycle 2 `childCount 0`, `mountHTML ""`; cycle 3 identical to cycle 2; bootstrapped control → `0` on the mount, `inTree 1`, `html ""` (unchanged). **Mechanism (confirmed in code):** `tearDownGraph()` calls `render()`, whose compile branch runs whenever `bootstrapped === false` (`src/renderer/runtime.ts:155-173`), so on that drive the emptying render **MOUNTS** the graph it is about to discard; `reconcileMount()` is reachable only via `resetRenderState()` (a load) and `teardown()` never calls it. **DISPOSITION: RESOLVED-BY-PINNING** — spec §1.1 re-pinned to the drive, §7 item 13 amended, §3.1 `M-11`/`M-12` now NAME their (bootstrapped) drive and cross-reference **`M-19`**, the appended row that pins the drive; the blind run's two FAILs (`TEAR-3`/`TEAR-4`) are the same finding, one row per reading, and are resolved the same way. **(2) THE OTHER FINDINGS, each with its disposition (spec §3b):** the reconciler is **not total** (`holder.children` / `Array.from(kids)` sit outside the local `try`) → **PARKED-with-revisit-condition** (host-owned; revisit on the next `runtime.ts` change or a red row on a hostile mount); the **detach rule is a heuristic** that could detach **another graph's live root** if two graphs shared a mount → **PARKED-with-revisit-condition** + the stated precondition **ONE RUNTIME PER MOUNT** (`MULTI-GRAPH-ISOLATION` is the adopted answer); the probe **refuses a real-DOM mount** (`Array.isArray(children)` vs `HTMLCollection`) so spec §5.2's optional `[U]` row **cannot be taken until fixed** → **OWED-with-owner** (the pass that would take that row); 10. **`UI-LEG-TIMEOUT-CLASS-RACE-SNAPSHOT-RULE` (`:168`) — the `ui` leg's TIMEOUT-CLASS RETRYABILITY RACE is FIXED, and the leg now matches the clause it already had (added 2026-09-27, FIFTH pass — the RCA + fix pass; a status/annotation note + a new ACTIVE decision row, **not a contract change to any pre-existing row and not a change to any normative spec clause**).** **The finding it closes:** the live battery's **`F-2`** (`docs/specs/ci-ui-leg-live-status.md` §6.2) — *"the timeout class's retryability is a RACE, so §3.7's PRECEDENCE CLAUSE is only half-implemented"*. **The RCA, in one paragraph:** the leg's failed-attempt path handed the predicate the **live** `resolved` flag on a line that runs **after** the bounded termination-grace `await`, so the **handshake half of the signature was sampled ~2 s after the timeout half**; a slow-but-HEALTHY boot whose handshake completed inside the grace window therefore flipped a timeout-class attempt to **non-retryable**, and the boundary was **NON-MONOTONIC** (`30`/`100 ms` retried to exhaustion; **`200`/`260 ms` aborted on attempt 1** with attempts 2–4 unrecorded and no `RT-7` marker; `300`/`400 ms` green). **The fix:** snapshot the handshake state **at the instant the raced verdict settles** (`const handshakeResolvedAtSettle = resolved`, `scripts/electron-ui.mjs:641`, read) and hand the **snapshot** to the predicate (`:660`, read) — both halves of the signature now describe the **same instant**, which is what the timeout signature already meant. **The verified outcome:** the boundary is **MONOTONIC** (`120`/`200`/`260 ms` → `RT-7 EXHAUSTED: 4 of 4 (3 retries)`, exit `1`, **every attempt recorded in order**; `300 ms` → accepted, `11/11`, `427x22`), and the pass is **regression-free** (unit rows **`68/68`**; `npm test` **58 files / 863 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed**; typecheck clean; battery **184 checks / 0 failures**; `npm run divergence` **`R13 RESULT: 9 checks, 0 failures`**; `npm run ui` → **exit 0**, `11/11`, `427x22`, **`retries=0`**). **What did NOT move:** `RT-1`/`RT-2`/`RT-4` item 3 and the **PRECEDENCE CLAUSE** are **unchanged** — **the leg was corrected to the contract, not the reverse** (`AGENTS.md` item 3/RCA-1) — and this file's `REALDOM-UI-LEG-RETRY-RULED-AND-LANDED` row (`:165`) is untouched. **Recorded honestly (and it is the general lesson, which is why the new ACTIVE row is written as a RULE and not as a leg footnote):** the unit's `RT-*` rows drive the predicate with **hand-built attempt records**, so they pin the predicate's **shape** and **cannot observe the live sampling point** — the race lives in the **order of two reads** in the leg's `catch` path, reachable only in a **real boot**. **RECOMMENDED, NOT IMPLEMENTED (a TestWriter-side change, not this pass's):** a **snapshot-ordering row** — see the new row's *What pins it* cell. **HOST-owned → no `docs/HANDOFF.md` round and no upstream issue is owed either way** (`docs/defects.md` `UI-LEG-TIMEOUT-CLASS-RACE`, CLOSED host-side).

(added 2026-09-27, third pass; this is a documentation-duty discharge, not a contract change to any pre-existing row).** The retry ruling's contract (`docs/specs/ci-ui-leg.md` §3.7 `RT-1`–`RT-9`) makes the attempt count and the per-attempt handshake timeout **operator-configurable**, and its `RT-4` items 2–3 required their **names** to be recorded **in the leg and in this file**. The leg records them (`scripts/electron-ui.mjs:67-72`, read); **this file recorded neither** until now — the blind verification run measured **`grep -c PROVIDENT_UI docs/decisions.md` = `0`** (its FAIL row `RT-08`), which is what made the retry rows **non-drivable from documentation alone**. **The record, exact:** **`PROVIDENT_UI_BOOT_ATTEMPTS`** = attempts per boot, **default `4`**, **range `1`–`4`** (the operator may **LOWER**, **never RAISE** — `1` means *"no retry"*); **`PROVIDENT_UI_BOOT_TIMEOUT_MS`** = per-attempt handshake timeout, **default `30 000 ms`**, **range `≥ 1 ms`**. **A malformed value of either is a PROGRAMMING ERROR ⇒ exit `1`, before the precondition and before any boot — NEVER a silent clamp, NEVER a fallback to the default** (`ci-ui-leg.md` §3.6 exit 1, §3.7 `RT-2` class 5; `RT-4` items 2–3 pin the same rule for the contract). **Also recorded in the same pass, because both were measured against the same row:** the **per-boot wall-clock ceiling is `121 750 ms`** (the derivation is the pin; the `122 000 ms` label it conflicted with is **marked SUPERSEDED** in `ci-ui-leg.md` §3.7 `RT-4` item 4, its AMENDMENT BLOCK 2 `A2-3` and its status block), and the leg's live result line is **`UI RESULT: 0 failures (11/11 assertions green, mapped onto the five declared rows R0-R4)`** — the older `5/5 rows green` form **quoted in this file's `REALDOM-UI-LEG-LANDED` (`:152`) and `REAL-DOM-UI-GATE-LEG` (`:65`) checkpoint cells is STALE** and must not be re-quoted (five = the declared row identities `R0`–`R4`; eleven = the `row(...)` assertion call sites asserting them; `ci-ui-leg.md` `MS-2` + AMENDMENT BLOCK 3 `B3-4`). **Rows `:65` and `:152` themselves are NOT rewritten** (this file's trailing-note convention: the row text is the historical record, read together with this note), and **no clause of the retry ruling changes**: exhaustion still exits **`1`**, and a **timeout-class** bootstrap failure is **retryable** per `ci-ui-leg.md` §3.7's PRECEDENCE CLAUSE (AMENDMENT BLOCK 3 `B3-3`) — noted here because the **landed leg and one landed test row still implement the older `RT-1`-only reading**, a drift reported to the supervisor and **not** reconciled in this file.
